# Findings ledger — 9 areas, 119 findings (blocking 1, major 35, minor 46, note 37, refuted 0)

## angles-houses-aspects-2 — Aspect 'applying' flag is wrong whenever orb < 0.01 x relative speed (the last ~13 minutes before every exact Moon aspect)
- severity: blocking · status: confirmed/adjusted · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: findAspects decides applying by stepping both bodies 0.02 day forward and asking whether the new orb is smaller than the current one (chunk-INI453VG.js:57-64). When the pair will pass exactness within half that step, the stepped orb is larger although the aspect is applying now, so every applying aspect with orb < 0.01 x |relative speed| (about 0.11-0.15 deg for Moon pairs) is reported as separating. The retrograde, 180-degree-wrap and near-station cases are otherwise handled correctly.
- evidence: Code: chunk-INI453VG.js:57 (const stepDays = 0.02), :64 (applying: Math.abs(nextSeparation - angle) < match.orb). Synthetic (real dist findAspects): Moon 99.90 / Sun 100.00 (Moon 13.2 deg/d, orb 0.10, approaching) -> applying:false, reference true; Moon 9.95 square Mars 100.0 (orb 0.05) -> false; Moon 179.85 opposition Sun 359.90 (orb 0.05, wrap) -> false; Moon 159.99 sextile Saturn (orb 0.01) -> false; Moon 0.13 before trine -> true (just above the threshold); retrograde Mercury 101.0 (-1.0 deg/d) vs Sun 100.0 -> true (correct); both retrograde Mars/Jupiter square -> true (correct); exact orb 0 moving apart -> false (correct). Year scan 2024 every 30 min with computeBodies + findAspects: 236,910 aspects, 510 misclassified (0.22%), every one with orb <= 0.01 x relSpeed (max misclassified orb 0.148 deg), all involving the Moon (e.g. 2024-01-04T03:30Z Sun-Moon square orb 0.0031 engine false, reference true). Artifact: .../audit/angles-houses-aspects/aspects-applying.json (script aspects_applying.mjs, 25 s).
- impact: A shipped boolean is wrong in a well-defined, reproducible window; any consumer (transit alerts, MCP, receipts) reading 'separating' for a Moon aspect minutes before exactness is misled. Swiss has no aspect API, so fixing it costs nothing in parity and is required for the 'more advanced' claim.
- fix: Replace the finite step with a sign test: applying = (signedSeparation(a,b) - angle) * d(separation)/dt < 0 where d/dt is derived from the signed relative speed (sign of (a.lon - b.lon) wrapped to (-180,180] times (a.speed - b.speed)); treat |rate| < 1e-9 as 'stationary' (a third state, or false with a flag). Add unit tests for orb < 0.01 x relSpeed, wrap at 0/180, both-retrograde, and speed 0.
- verifier: Code read at chunk-INI453VG.js:57 (stepDays = 0.02) and :64 (applying = |nextSeparation - angle| < orb) matches the citation. Analytically, with the linear model the stepped orb is |0.02*rel - orb|, which is < orb iff orb > 0.01*rel, so every applying aspect with orb < 0.01*|relSpeed| is reported separating; numerically the boundary sits exactly there (rel 6.1: orb 0.0611 -> true, 0.0609 -> false; rel 12.22: 0.1221 -> false). Shipped dist findAspects on Moon 99.9 (13.2 deg/d) vs Sun 100.0 (0.98) returns {conjunction, orb 0.10, applying:false} although exactness is 11 min away; Moon 0.13 before trine -> true; retrograde Mercury case -> true (correct). Independent 2024 scan (every 30 min, computeBodies, reference = real motion: orb at t vs orb at t+60 s from recomputed longitudes, NOT the auditor's linear model): 236,910 aspects, 486 misclassified (0.205%; auditor 510 with the linear reference, the 24-case difference is boundary cases where the two references differ), zero separating-reported-applying, all 486 with orb <= 0.01*relSpeed, max misclassified orb 0.1484 deg. One aside in the auditor's evidence is inexact: 83 of the 486 are non-Moon pairs (Sun-Uranus 5, Venus-Saturn 6, Mars-Saturn 3, ...), so the window applies to every pair, merely most often to the Moon. Severity blocking is earned under the rules: a shipped boolean is deterministically wrong in a reproducible window, and STRATEGY.md advertises applying/separating as a feature.
- second lens: Tried to dissolve it three ways and could not. (1) Units/field misread: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js longitudeSpeed returns deg/day (central difference over +/-0.25 d divided by 0.5 d), so 'stepDays = 0.02' in chunk-INI453VG.js:57 is dimensionally consistent; orb is |sep - angle| as the d.ts documents. (2) Convention: the shipped contract (dist/types-BQGrRiSd.d.ts:75) says 'True when the aspect is still tightening at the chart instant' - an instantaneous definition, so a half-step-ahead comparison cannot be defended as an alternative convention. (3) Closed form instead of finite differences: with s = wrapped signed lonA-lonB, d(orb)/dt = sign(|s|-A) * sign(s) * (vA - vB); applying iff that is negative. For an applying pair with rate r the engine's test |orb - r*h| < orb fails exactly when orb <= r*h/2, i.e. when time-to-exact <= h/2 = 0.01 day = 14.4 minutes, for ANY pair - a synthetic Moon-Sun sweep flips from false to true between 14.3 and 14.5 min before exact, and a Jupiter-Saturn conjunction 10 min before exact (orb 0.00104 deg) is also reported separating (verify/angles-houses-aspects/closed-form.json, closed_form.mjs). Independent ephemeris: pyswisseph 2.10.03 SWIEPH (flag check: 0 fallbacks) Sun..Pluto every 30 min over 2024 fed into the shipped findAspects and judged by the closed form: 506 of 236,932 aspects misclassified (phase 1 had 510/236,910 on the engine ephemeris), zero false positives, max time-to-exact 14.36 min, 83 of the 506 are non-Moon pairs (swiss-compare.json; the engine-ephemeris rerun at 10-min cadence gives 1,626/710,786 with 275 non-Moon, all <= 14.39 min). Cited example re-derived: Swiss bisection puts the exact Sun-Moon square at 2024-01-04T03:30:28Z; Swiss orb at 03:30:00 is 0.00344 deg with the Moon closing at 10.96 deg/d, and Swiss positions fed to findAspects return applying:false; the engine's own computeBodies shows the orb shrinking 0.00306 -> 0.00179 -> 0.00052 -> 0.0000122 deg at 03:30:00/10/20/24Z with the flag false at every one of those instants (engine-self.json). The defect stands; the claim needs two corrections: the window is exactly 14.4 min (half the 0.02-day step), not '~13 min', and it precedes every exact aspect of every pair, not only Moon pairs (phase 1's 'all Moon pairs' is a 30-min sampling artifact - slow pairs spend the same 14.4 min misflagged but at orbs of 0.001-0.01 deg). Severity lowered to major: the flag is deterministically wrong, contradicts its documented contract, and is surfaced verbatim by ChartCalculator.tsx:2913, explorer Inspector.tsx:406, share-card.ts:1273, Wheel.tsx:498 (dash pattern) and compare/diff.ts:324, but the exposure is ~0.21% of aspect rows at a random instant, only at orbs the UI prints as 0.0-0.1 deg, with no false positives and a 0.25-day fix; ranking it above finding 1 (up to 512 arcsec on every chart's ASC) would invert the impact order. Horizons requests used: 0.

## alpha-reduction-math-1 — Outer planets are system barycentres, up to 0.093 arcsec off the physical centre; Swiss can return centres, the alpha cannot
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 4 d
- corrected claim: For Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, Neptune and Pluto the alpha returns the planetary-system barycentre (ephemeris.mjs:247-254 BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE, flagged isSystemBarycentre in every result); the only route to a centre is the undocumented, non-contract options.offsetKm hook in reduce.mjs:182-206, which nothing shipped feeds. Measured from the NAIF satellite kernels with a 0.5-day sweep, the apparent geocentric centre-vs-barycentre offset reaches Jupiter 0.0758" (p50 0.0187", 220 km; tree's proven bound 0.0767"), Saturn 0.0529" (p50 0.0307", 312 km), Neptune 0.0035", Pluto 0.0934" (p50 0.0562", 2132 km), over kernel-limited windows (Jupiter 1997-2150, Saturn 2014-2050, Pluto 2013-2150). Swiss Ephemeris returns the same barycentres by default (verified: 1-22 km from the DE440s barycentres vs 136-2131 km from the centres) and offers SEFLG_CENTER_BODY, whose code path demands sepm9599/9699/9799/9899/9999.se1; with those files Swiss can return centres, the alpha cannot.
- claim: For Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, Neptune and Pluto the alpha returns the planetary-system barycentre (ephemeris.mjs BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE, honestly flagged) with no path to the body centre. Measured from the NAIF satellite kernels the centre-vs-barycentre offset in apparent geocentric position reaches Jupiter 0.0709" (p50 0.0186", offset 219.6 km), Saturn 0.0529" (p50 0.0303", 311.4 km), Neptune 0.0035", Pluto 0.0934" total (0.0829" lon / 0.0862" lat, 2132 km). Swiss Ephemeris returns the same barycentres by default but offers SEFLG_CENTER_BODY, which with its planetary-moon files (sepm9599/9699/9999.se1) returns the centre; on this machine the flag fails with 'SwissEph file sepm9599.se1 not found', which proves the capability exists and that plain calls are barycentres. This is a concrete accuracy item where Swiss (with its data) beats the alpha by up to 0.09" on Pluto and 0.07" on Jupiter, i.e. 7-9 times the alpha's 0.0104" residual against Swiss.
- evidence: R/examples/precision-alpha/src/core/ephemeris.mjs:366-373 (BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE), reduce.mjs:41-42,68. S/centre/sat-offsets.py -> S/centre/sat-offsets.json (3,000 epochs per body inside each kernel's coverage intersected with 1850-2150; 7.4 s). Horizons arbiter check at JD 2460000.0 TT: 599-minus-5 dlon +0.0018" dlat +0.0004", 999-minus-9 dlon -0.0706" dlat +0.0436" (S/horizons/q-{599,5,999,9}-2460000.txt, queries in the .query files) vs my kernel-derived +0.0018/+0.0001 and -0.0703/+0.0437. Swiss flag test S/centre/swiss-centre.py -> swiss-centre.json: plain calc flags 258 (SWIEPH), FLG_CENTER_BODY raises the missing-sepm-file error for Jupiter/Saturn/Pluto and is a no-op for Mars. Consistent with the committed numerics track (docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/RESULTS.md section 4, B2: Jupiter <=0.0767", Pluto <=0.0935" proven).
- impact: Within the alpha's own domain this is the largest known systematic against the sky for Jupiter, Saturn and Pluto, and it is invisible to any Swiss-default comparison because Swiss shares the convention. A stack that wants to be more accurate than Swiss must return centres; Swiss already can.
- fix: Compile a compact centre-relative-to-barycentre Chebyshev series per planet from the NAIF satellite kernels (jup365/sat441 or the 348/480 files present) into the pack as a 'sun'-style delta frame (the offset is a smooth few-hundred-km quasi-periodic function; 8 coefficients per 4-day record over 300 years is about 2.6 MB per planet, far below the 313 MB the numerics track feared), apply it at the emission epoch exactly as reduce.mjs's offsetKm hook already does, set isSystemBarycentre false, and add the kernel-derived offset to the tier-b tests. For dates outside satellite-kernel coverage keep the barycentre and say so per result.
- verifier: Reproduced independently. (a) Swiss plain output IS the planetary-system barycentre: with FLG_SWIEPH|FLG_XYZ|FLG_EQUATORIAL|FLG_BARYCTR|FLG_J2000|FLG_ICRS|FLG_TRUEPOS|FLG_NOABERR|FLG_NOGDEFL (returned flags 155250, SWIEPH set) Swiss sits 1.3/2.1/8.3 km (p50; max 3.8/10.7/21.8) from DE440s targets 5/6/9 and 136/289/2131 km from the 599/699/999 centres (jup348/sat480/plu060), 200/200 epochs closer to the barycentre for Jupiter, Saturn and Pluto (verify/alpha-reduction-math/swiss-bary-ident.json). (b) pyswisseph 2.10.03 has FLG_CENTER_BODY=0x100000; with the local .se1 path it raises "SwissEph file 'sepm9599.se1' (9699/9799/9899/9999) not found" for Jupiter/Saturn/Uranus/Neptune/Pluto and is a no-op (0.0") for Mars, exactly as the auditor recorded (swiss-flags.json); the capability exists in the code path but its output accuracy could not be tested here because no sepm file is present. (c) Offsets from the NAIF kernels, my own 6,000-epoch run plus a dense 0.5-day sweep (111,730 Jupiter / 25,754 Saturn / 100,072 Pluto epochs): Pluto 0.0934" (p50 0.0562", 2132 km) reproduces; Saturn 0.0529" (p50 0.0307", 311.9 km) reproduces; Neptune 0.00354" reproduces; Jupiter reaches 0.0758" at JD 2455459.5 (p50 0.0187", 220.3 km), ABOVE the auditor's 0.0709" -- the auditor's 3,000-point grid (18-day step against the 7-17 day Galilean modulation) undersampled the maximum; the tree's proven bound <=0.07672" (docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/RESULTS.md:297) is consistent with my 0.0758". Sampled windows are kernel-limited (Jupiter 1997-2150, Saturn 2014-2050, Pluto 2013-2150), not the pack's full 1850-2150. (d) Cached Horizons pairs (audit/alpha-reduction-math/horizons/q-{599,5,999,9}-2460000.txt, QUANTITIES=31, CENTER=500@399, TT) give 599-5 dlon +0.0018" dlat +0.0004" and 999-9 dlon -0.0706" dlat +0.0436"; my kernel-derived values at the same JD are +0.0018/+0.0001 and -0.0703/+0.0437 (Horizons prints 1e-7 deg = 0.00036" resolution). (e) Citation errors: ephemeris.mjs is 254 lines long and BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE is at line 254 (comment 247-253), not 366-373; and 'no path to the body centre' is overstated -- reduce.mjs:182-206 carries the non-contract options.offsetKm hook that adds a constant km vector at the emission epoch (the auditor's own fix depends on it), though nothing shipped populates it. Severity major is earned: Swiss with its sepm files can return centres, the alpha cannot, and 0.05-0.09" is 5-9x the alpha's 0.0104" residual against Swiss (Jupiter/Pluto residuals on the auditor's 2,000-row corpus are only 0.0019"/0.0015", independently confirming both stacks return the same barycentre).

## alpha-reduction-math-2 — No topocentric, heliocentric, barycentric, J2000/astrometric, sidereal or rate output: Swiss offers all of them
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 8 d
- claim: The alpha's contract is a single quantity: apparent geocentric ecliptic longitude/latitude of date plus a light-time-corrected distance (reduce.mjs CONTRACT.coordinates, notModelled). It reports no apparent angular rate (CONTRACT.velocities), no topocentric place (no geodetic observer, no diurnal aberration), no heliocentric or barycentric place (barycentric position is deliberately excluded, CONTRACT.barycentricPositionExcluded), no J2000/astrometric/true-position variants outside the experimental fixed-frame search modes, and no sidereal longitudes. Swiss provides each through flags (FLG_TOPOCTR with geodetic observer and altitude, FLG_HELCTR, FLG_BARYCTR, FLG_J2000, FLG_TRUEPOS, FLG_NOABERR, FLG_NOGDEFL, FLG_SIDEREAL, FLG_SPEED with speeds consistent with the apparent chain). Every chart-level consumer (houses need the apparent obliquity and sidereal time, station finding needs rates, a birth chart needs the topocentric Moon at the 1-arcsec level of parallax) is therefore outside the alpha today.
- evidence: R/examples/precision-alpha/src/core/reduce.mjs:39-90 (CONTRACT), README.md 'What it supports, exactly'. Swiss flags exist and answer with .se1 data on this machine (swiss-centre.json shows FLG_SPEED honoured; FLG_TOPOCTR etc. are documented Swiss features, not measured here). The interval modules already carry the derivatives needed for rates (aberration.mjs aberrateInterval PDot, deflection.mjs deflectInterval DDot, frame-of-date.mjs Rdot).
- impact: Keeps the stack materially below Swiss in capability even where its geocentric numbers are better. Lunar topocentric parallax alone is up to ~1 degree; ignoring it is not an option for a birth chart that claims to exceed Swiss.
- fix: Add to the Reducer: (a) an observer model (WGS84 geodetic to GCRS via eraGd2gc-equivalent, Earth rotation via ERA/GAST from the same IAU 2006/2000B chain, polar motion optional) giving a topocentric observer position and velocity, with diurnal aberration falling out of using the observer's total barycentric velocity; (b) analytic apparent rates by differentiating the pointwise chain (the derivative formulas already exist in the interval modules; port them to point form); (c) heliocentric/barycentric/astrometric/J2000 variants as switches on the existing stages; (d) sidereal by subtracting an ayanamsa computed from the same frame chain. Validate each against pyerfa (atco13-style topocentric) and Swiss flags as instruments.
- verifier: reduce.mjs:39-90 is the CONTRACT exactly as quoted: coordinates = apparent geocentric ecliptic lon/lat of date plus geometric distance; velocities = 'no apparent angular rate is reported'; notModelled includes 'diurnal (topocentric) parallax and diurnal aberration: the observer is the geocentre'; barycentricPositionExcluded.excluded = true. README.md:20-30,45-55 says the same. The public surface (src/index.mjs: PrecisionRuntime.apparent/apparentAtJdTt/search/searchGeometric; src/experimental.mjs: modes validated-retarded-geometric, -aberrated, -aberrated-of-date, -aberrated-deflected-of-date, the fixed ones in the 'ecliptic of the ICRS equator', not the J2000 mean equinox) exposes no rate, no observer, no heliocentric/barycentric place, no sidereal longitude, and the result object (reduce.mjs:288-302) returns dpsiArcsec/depsArcsec but neither the true obliquity nor any sidereal time; grep for topocentric/heliocentric/sidereal across src/core/*.mjs returns only notModelled entries. Swiss side tested on the local .se1 data (verify/alpha-reduction-math/swiss-flags.py -> swiss-flags.json): FLG_TOPOCTR (after set_topo), FLG_HELCTR, FLG_BARYCTR, FLG_J2000, FLG_TRUEPOS, FLG_NOABERR, FLG_NOGDEFL, FLG_SIDEREAL (Lahiri, -24.1779 deg), FLG_EQUATORIAL and FLG_SPEED all return with FLG_SWIEPH set and the requested flag kept, producing plausible shifts (Moon topocentric -0.854 deg lat/+0.280 deg lon at New York, J2000 -0.3208 deg for every body, TRUEPOS/NOABERR ~0.006 deg on Sun/Jupiter). Moon topocentric-minus-geocentric separation sampled every 3 h for a year at New York peaks at 1.021 deg, matching the impact statement's '~1 degree'. The derivative machinery named in the evidence exists: aberration.mjs:178-234 aberrateInterval returns PDot, deflection.mjs:544 deflectInterval takes dDot/eRawDot/qRawDot, frame-of-date.mjs:358-362 returns Rdot. Not verified: that Swiss FLG_SPEED rates are 'consistent with the apparent chain' (speeds were returned but their consistency was not measured); this does not affect the finding. Severity major is earned under the capability-gap rule.

## alpha-search-partition-1 — acceptPartialPlan is inert: an accepted partial plan searches nothing, contradicting the option's documented purpose
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: searchDeflectedOverPartition sets `status` to the plan's non-finished status before any subsearch (partitioned-search.mjs:281-284), and `runOn` (line 340) returns early for every span whenever `status !== 'finished'`. So with `acceptPartialPlan: true` no admissible or boundary span is ever searched, although the refusal text (partitioned-search.mjs:199: 'pass acceptPartialPlan: true to search the part it did classify') and types/experimental.d.ts:297-303 promise exactly that.
- evidence: Synthetic pack (examples/synthetic-pack.mjs), Mars, target 95°, window [-38 d, +38 d]. Full plan: finished, 1,816 evaluations, 1 admissible span; full search finds 1 event. Plan starved to 605 evaluations: budget-exhausted, 1 admissible span of 2,160,000 s (25 d) plus 1 unprocessed. Search over it with acceptPartialPlan:true and maxEvaluations 4,000,000: status 'budget-exhausted', reason 'the supplied plan stopped with budget-exhausted…', events 0, searchEvaluations 0, spanResults 0, notSearchedSec 2,160,000, exhaustiveOverAdmissible false. Artifact: <audit>/alpha-search-partition/partition/accept-partial.json (script accept-partial.mjs beside it, 0.3 s). README.md:251 is more careful ('the run then reports finished: false') but still implies the classified part is answered.
- impact: The one mechanism for amortising a partition across processes or budgets does not work, so the 'plan once, query many' story (PARTITION-RESULTS 'Cold against repeated') holds only for finished native plans in one heap. The result is internally honest (mayHoldUnfoundSupportedEvents true, notSearched listed), so no wrong answer is returned; the documented capability simply does not exist.
- fix: Separate the two axes in searchDeflectedOverPartition: keep a `stopped` flag that only subsearch outcomes and the shared budget set, let `runOn` gate on that, and fold the inherited plan status into the final `execution.status/finished` after the subsearches. Add a tier-a test asserting spanResults.length === plan.admissible.length + plan.boundary.length for an accepted partial plan with ample budget, and that events found in the classified part match the full plan's events there.
- verifier: Re-ran the auditor's reproduction (copied to scratch, 0.34 s): synthetic pack, Mars, target 95 deg. Full plan finished at 1,816 evaluations, its search finds 1 event (bracket -1319099.2487 s TDB). Plan starved to 605 evaluations: status budget-exhausted, admissible [-3283200,-1123200] (2,160,000 s), 1 unprocessed span. searchRetardedAberratedDeflectedOfDateOverPlan with acceptPartialPlan:true and maxEvaluations 4,000,000: status 'budget-exhausted' (inherited), events 0, searchEvaluations 0, spanResults 0, notSearchedSec 2,160,000, exhaustiveOverAdmissible false, mayHoldUnfoundSupportedEvents true -- identical to accept-partial.json. Extension: plans starved to 50/90/99 % of the full budget (classified admissible 3,283,200 / 5,875,200 / 6,480,000 s = up to 75 d) also return 0 searchEvaluations, 0 spanResults, 0 events. Code confirms the mechanism: partitioned-search.mjs:281-284 sets `status = plan.execution.status` before any subsearch, and runOn (line 340) is `if (status !== 'finished') { notSearched.push([lo, hi]); return; }`, so every span is skipped. The refusal text at line 199 ('pass acceptPartialPlan: true to search the part it did classify') and experimental.mjs:141 promise the search; types/experimental.d.ts:297-303 and README.md:251 only promise `finished: false` (accurate). The tier-a test partitioned-search.nodetest.mjs:169-187 pins status inheritance and asserts nothing about spans being searched. Result is honest (no wrong answer), so major, not blocking, is the right tier.

## alpha-search-partition-2 — The Moon's partition failures (P-3, P-4) are an enclosure-construction defect, not the geometry: cos(elongation) is enclosed 2,900x too wide
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 4 d
- corrected claim: The partition's cos(elongation) enclosure over a 60-s Moon cell is ~2,900x the true variation (4.045e-2 vs 1.398e-5) and the ratio is constant in cell width, so at the declared 60-s tolerance the undecidable residue is ~2,900 cells per crossing whatever the tolerance (shrinking cells shrinks the residue only linearly, at the cell-count cost that PARTITION-RESULTS' 5-s sweep already shows breaks P-1). Decomposed by measurement: replacing the split R.pos-O.pos geocentric enclosure (domain-partition.mjs:315-329, widths 5,852/5,997/2,588 km) by the merged single-series form ((1+k)*moon, 124.5/126.3/42.4 km, true 18.3/58.3/18.9 km) cuts the cos width 44.8x to 9.0e-4; the remaining ~64x is the naive composition of -(e.d)/|d| in elongationCosInterval (deflection.mjs:178-180) -- even the exact sampled hull of d composes to 17x the true variation. PARTITION-RESULTS.md:105-123 misattributes the 0.042 width to how fast the elongation moves (the elongation's 12 deg/day gives only 1.4e-5 over 60 s). The same looseness governs the planets: Saturn F7's two coalesced boundary spans are 2,489 s and 2,658 s and Mars F5's 11,264 s and 12,192 s (40-200 floor cells per crossing). The retarded and aberrated rungs are NOT failing on the Moon (RETARDED-RESULTS 296,106 evaluations, ABERRATED-RESULTS A2 264,398, both finished), so the defect's practical impact is the deflected rung/partition (P-3, P-4, DEFLECTION-RESULTS Moon exhaustion at 4,000,001), not 'every retarded rung'. The projected ~0.04 d post-fix residue is an order-of-magnitude estimate: the same linear model predicts 40 d for the shipped enclosure against 15.8 d measured.
- claim: PARTITION-RESULTS.md:105-123 attributes the 5.266 % Moon boundary to the mean-value enclosure 'scaling with the cell width' and calls it 'the honest verdict at the declared tolerance'. Measured, the enclosure's width ratio to the true variation is constant (~2,900x at 60 s, 600 s and 3,600 s), so shrinking cells cannot fix it; the looseness comes from (a) classifyCell enclosing the geocentric vector as R.pos − O.pos with the observer and target enclosed separately (domain-partition.mjs:315-329), which discards the Earth–Moon co-motion (the geometric mode's contributions() merges those weights and does not pay this), and (b) naive interval composition of −(e·d)/|d| in elongationCosInterval (deflection.mjs:178-180) where d appears in numerator and denominator and each dot-product term is enclosed independently.
- evidence: Real DE440s pack (payload sha256 0a218764…, identical to the evidence pack), F2 Moon window, floor crossing at 1977-10-12T11:28:05Z (9.03 h before the 0.38° conjunction). 60-s cell straddling the crossing, tight light-time interval ±0.02 s: enclosure width 4.045e-2 in cos, true variation 1.398e-5, ratio 2,893x; at ±6 h offsets ratios 1,704x–8,859x; every one of 24 cells (offsets −6 h…+18 h, two light-time widths) classifies 'boundary' although 20 are truly admissible or truly excluded with margins up to 6.7e-3. Geocentric vector over the same 60-s cell: split R.pos−O.pos width 5,852/5,997/2,588 km vs true variation 18.3/58.3/18.9 km (319x/103x/137x); merged single-series enclosure 124.5/126.3/42.4 km (6.8x/2.2x/2.3x). A centred form (value at midpoint + derivative bound × half-width) with a 3x-loose derivative would be ~4.2e-5 wide → undecidable residue ≈ ±180 s per crossing → ~0.04 d for 20 crossings (0.014 % of 300 d) against the measured 15.8 d (5.266 %). Reproduced the committed partition exactly: 939,176 evaluations, 158,121 cells, 10 boundary spans, 5.266 %, widest 42.19 s, 14.8 s wall. Artifacts: …/audit/alpha-search-partition/partition/enclosure-width.json, moon-relative-state.json, phase-share.json (scripts beside them; 1.4 s, 2 s, 20 s).
- impact: This is the mechanism behind both preregistered failures and behind the deflected rung's 47–831x cost and the Moon's 4,000,001-evaluation exhaustion (DEFLECTION-RESULTS): retardedCell builds the same split D = R.pos − O.pos (retarded-search.mjs:411) and its M1/M2 from it, so every retarded rung over-subdivides the Moon by roughly the same factor. Fixing it is the single largest lever toward making 'proven for apparent longitude of date' practical.
- fix: (1) Enclose the geocentric vector from MERGED weights: d(t) = [T(t) − O(t)] + [T(t−τ) − T(t)], where the first term uses one combined weight map (for the Moon a single series with weight 1+1/EMRAT, as validated-search.contributions does) and the second is a mean-value term −v_T·[τ.lo, τ.hi] ± ½·a_T·τ.hi² from the existing velocity/acceleration bounds. (2) Replace the naive cos(elongation) enclosure by a centred form: cos at the cell midpoint (point enclosure) plus an interval bound on d(cos)/dt from the velocity enclosures times the half-width; keep the naive form as a cross-check and take the intersection. (3) Apply (1) to retardedCell as well and re-run the cost baseline and the P-1…P-7 evaluation. Expected: P-3 and P-4 pass by orders of magnitude; Moon deflected search finishes well inside budget.
- verifier: Re-ran enclosure-width.mjs and moon-relative-state.mjs from scratch copies: floor crossing at TDB -701224266.695 s = 1977-10-12T11:28:05.3Z, 9.03 h before conjunction; at the crossing cell width 4.045e-2 vs true 1.398e-5 (2,892.7x), ratios 1,703.7x-8,859.4x over 24 cells, all 24 'boundary' though 20 are truly decidable (margins to 6.67e-3); split/merged/true widths reproduce to the printed digit at 60/600/3600 s. Own decomposition (decompose.mjs): split 4.045e-2 (2,892.7x), merged geometric 8.99e-4 (64.3x), merged + light-time mean-value term 9.03e-4 (64.6x), true hull of d 2.39e-4 (17.1x); all four enclose the sampled truth. Swiss as instrument (pyswisseph 2.10.03, flag 5746 with FLG_SWIEPH, ephe <swisslab>/ephe): light-time-only (NOABERR|NOGDEFL) Sun-Moon elongation at the alpha's crossing instant 4.9999997 deg vs the alpha's retarded elongation 5.0000000 deg (geometric 5.00547 in both); closest approach 0.3800 deg at 1977-10-12 20:27 UTC, matching PARTITION-RESULTS/DEFLECTION-RESULTS 0.381 deg 20:30Z; geometry and convention confirmed. Planet check (saturn-boundary.mjs -> planet-boundary.json): Saturn plan finished 10,268 evaluations, boundary spans 2,489.06 s and 2,657.81 s (0.0199 %); Mars 43,150 evaluations, spans 11,264.06 s and 12,192.19 s (0.0905 %); diagnostics.widestBoundarySec 42.1875 in both is the widest floor CELL, not a span. What the auditor got wrong: 'shrinking cells cannot fix it' contradicts PARTITION-RESULTS' own 5-s sweep (0.71 %) -- the residue scales linearly with cell width; the impact sentence about every retarded rung over-subdividing 'by roughly the same factor' was not measured and the retarded/aberrated Moon runs finished; the 0.04 d figure is a model, not a measurement. Severity major is earned: the Moon apparent-of-date deflected search is a documented FAIL that Swiss answers.

## alpha-search-partition-3 — Only longitude crossings exist; stations, aspects, latitude/declination/elongation events and eclipses are absent although the proof machinery covers them
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 12 d
- corrected claim: The alpha's proven (validated/experimental) modes support one operation, longitude crossings; the empirical mode adds a conditional 'aspect' kind (search.mjs SEARCH_CONTRACT.kinds). SEARCH_CONTRACT.notSupported (search.mjs:82-86) excludes stations, latitude/declination/distance/elongation events and rise/set/transit, and no eclipse or occultation operation exists anywhere in examples/precision-alpha/src. pyswisseph 2.10.03 offers solcross, mooncross, mooncross_node, helio_cross, rise_trans(_true_hor), sol_eclipse_when_glob/loc/how/where, lun_eclipse_when/_loc/how, lun_occult_when_glob/loc/where, heliacal_*, nod_aps and no station, aspect or geocentric planetary crossing finder -- so the alpha is below Swiss on eclipses, occultations, rise/set/transit and Moon node crossings (the material gap), and stations/aspects are an opportunity to exceed Swiss rather than a gap below it. Stations are roots of x*y' - y*x' (degree 2n-2 per piece); an aspect at angle theta is a root of a degree-2n bilinear polynomial whose roots include theta+180 deg and need a quadrant test; latitude crossings are roots of z_ecl. The claim that the partition 'already brackets elongation = 5 deg to 60 s' is false: coalesced boundary spans are 2,489-2,658 s for Saturn, 11,264-12,192 s for Mars and 10 spans totalling 15.8 d for the Moon (42.19 s is the widest floor cell). The production engine computes stations (src/lib/engine/transit-scan-core.ts stationBreakpoints) and eclipses (scripts/build-eclipses.mjs via astronomy-engine SearchGlobalSolarEclipse/SearchLunarEclipse) without completeness semantics, so the absence is in the proven layer, not the whole stack.
- claim: SEARCH_CONTRACT.notSupported (search.mjs:82-86) lists stations, latitude/declination/distance/elongation events and rise/set as unsupported, and no eclipse or occultation operation exists. For the geometric quantity each of the first four is a root problem of an explicit polynomial in the stored coefficients, so the validated mode's exclusion/monotone proof applies unchanged: stations are roots of N(t) = x·y′ − y·x′ (numerator of dλ/dt); an aspect θ between bodies 1 and 2 is a root of (x1·y2 − y1·x2)·cosθ − (x1·x2 + y1·y2)·sinθ (degree 2n−2); ecliptic latitude crossings are roots of z_ecl(t); an elongation/separation threshold is what the partition already brackets (boundary spans enclose elongation = 5° to 60 s).
- evidence: Code reading: validated-search.mjs:166-225 builds f, g and their sum|c_k| bounds for one linear combination of series; the same seriesBounds/derivative machinery (cheb.mjs:45-119) yields bounds for products via the Chebyshev product rule or by re-fitting the product on the piece. Swiss inventory: pyswisseph 2.10.03 dir() shows solcross, mooncross, mooncross_node, helio_cross, rise_trans, sol_eclipse_when_glob/loc, lun_eclipse_when, lun_occult_when_glob/loc — and no station finder, no aspect finder, no geocentric planetary crossing finder, none with completeness or error semantics.
- impact: Against the goal, the alpha currently beats Swiss on one operation (proven longitude crossings) and is absent on the operations astrologers and almanacs use most (stations, aspects, node/latitude crossings, eclipses). The polynomial route gives proven-complete versions of stations, aspects and latitude crossings for the geometric quantity at roughly the cost of the existing mode; elongation/separation events fall out of the partition machinery; eclipse detection is a separation threshold against apparent radii (contact times and local circumstances additionally need a topocentric observer).
- fix: Add to validated-search.mjs a generic 'piece polynomial from a product/combination of two state series' builder (product Chebyshev coefficients on the common refinement, exact up to rounding, with sum|c_k| bounds) and three operations on it: searchGeometricStation (roots of x·y′ − y·x′ with a sign/derivative test for direct→retrograde), searchGeometricAspect (two bodies, angle θ), searchGeometricLatitude (roots of z_ecl, half-plane by sign of ż). Generalise partitionDomain's floor to `separationThresholdEvents(body, other, angle)` and expose its boundary spans as bracketed events. Eclipses: separation < (apparent radii sum) over an enclosure, then contact bisection. Then lift each to the retarded/aberrated/of-date rungs via the existing interval chain.
- verifier: search.mjs:77-86 read: kinds {longitude, aspect}, notSupported lists stations, 'latitude, declination, distance and elongation events', rise/set/transit; grep 'aspect' in validated-search.mjs: no hits, so no proven aspect kind. Swiss inventory run on the local pyswisseph 2.10.03 (swiss-check.py): finders exactly as listed above; no name matching station or aspect. Boundary-span widths measured by running planDeflectedDomain on the real pack for Saturn F7 and Mars F5 (planet-boundary.json) and taken from the Moon F2 reproduction (10 coalesced spans, 5.266 % = 15.8 d): elongation = 5 deg is not bracketed to 60 s by the current partition. Polynomial checks by hand: x*y' - y*x' has cancelling leading terms (degree 2n-2, as claimed); x1*y2 - y1*x2 and x1*x2 + y1*y2 are degree 2n, not 2n-2, and the bilinear form vanishes at both theta and theta+180 deg. Production capability by grep of the worktree (transit-scan-core.ts:231-265; build-eclipses.mjs:26-67). Severity stays major on the rule's 'capability gap below Swiss' clause because eclipses, occultations, rise/set and node crossings exist in Swiss and not in the alpha; the station/aspect portion does not by itself put the stack below Swiss.

## angles-houses-aspects-1 — ASC and Placidus cusps use mean obliquity with apparent sidereal time and true-of-date longitudes: up to 512" (0.14 deg) error, 22" p95
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: computeChart builds the angle input with obliquity = meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(utc)) but gastHours = SiderealTime (apparent, includes nutation in RA) and body longitudes in the true ecliptic of date (Rotation_EQJ_ECT). The missing nutation in obliquity (up to 9.67" in the grid) propagates into the ASC with a latitude-dependent gain, reaching 8.5 arcminutes at |lat| = 66 and 16" at |lat| <= 45; Placidus intermediate cusps inherit the same error. Swiss (and IAU practice) pairs apparent sidereal time with the true obliquity.
- evidence: Code: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:129-134 (angleInput: gastHours: SiderealTime(MakeTime(input.utc)), obliquity: meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(input.utc))); chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:9-13 (meanObliquity, IAU2006 polynomial, no nutation term), :25-39 (computeAngles); dist/receipt.js:143 (angles: "gast-and-mean-obliquity"). Measurement (3,128 cases: years 1800..2200 step 25, hours 0..21 step 3, lat 0, +/-10..+/-66, lon 0; Swiss 2.10.03 swe.houses_ex 'P', SWIEPH confirmed, plus ERFA gst06a/obl06/nut06a arbiter): shipped ASC vs Swiss p50 2.41" p95 22.4" max 512.5"; |lat|<=45: max 16.2"; 50-60: p95 20.0" max 26.4"; >=63: p95 76.2" max 512.5"; MC p95 3.0" max 3.9". Isolation: same engine formula with Swiss ARMC + Swiss true obliquity -> ASC max 1.0e-5", cusps max 0.0041" (formula is exact); Swiss ARMC + engine mean obliquity -> max 504.8" (reproduces the defect); engine GAST + Swiss true obliquity -> max 70.9" (the remainder is Swiss's own sidereal-time model, see finding 11) and 6.9" against the ERFA arbiter. dObl(engine mean vs Swiss true) p50 6.09" max 9.67"; dObl(astronomy-engine e_tilt().tobl vs Swiss true) max 0.030" so the fix is available in the already-bundled dependency. Concrete: 1950-03-21T18:00Z lat -66 lon 0: engine ASC 135.62994 vs Swiss 135.48915 (506.8"), MC agrees to 0.005"; cusp11 92.9015 vs 92.8913, cusp12 98.6118 vs 98.5862. Artifacts: <audit>/angles-houses-aspects/engine-vs-swiss.json (summaryA, worstAsc), gridA-rows.json, swiss.json; scripts swiss_dump.py + engine_grid.mjs. Compute: 1.6 s + 0.9 s.
- impact: At Nordic/Alaskan/Baltic latitudes (55-66) the ASC is systematically 10-30" off and up to 8.5' within a degree of the polar circle, which is 30-3000x the 0.16" residual the DE440s prototype achieves for positions; the engine.test.ts 0.1 deg ASC gate would fail at lat 66 if a vector existed there. The documented '1.57" worst angle' is not representative. Cannot exceed Swiss on angles until fixed.
- fix: In ephemeris.ts computeChart, set obliquity to the true obliquity of date: e_tilt(MakeTime(utc)).tobl from astronomy-engine (or meanObliquity + deps/3600), and use the same value in placidusCusps. Rename the receipt convention to 'gast-and-true-obliquity' and bump the receipt schema. Add Swiss houses_ex vectors at lat 63, 65, 66 (both hemispheres, 1800/1950/2200) to engine.test.ts with gates of 5" not 0.1 deg. Re-run docs/engine-validation and replace the '1.57" worst angle' row with the grid distribution.
- verifier: Cited lines verified: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:129-134 builds angleInput with gastHours = SiderealTime(MakeTime(utc)) (apparent) and obliquity = meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(utc)); chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:9-13 meanObliquity is the IAU2006 polynomial with no nutation term; receipt.js:143 angles: 'gast-and-mean-obliquity'. Concrete case 1950-03-21T18:00Z lat -66 lon 0: shipped computeChart ASC 135.629937 / MC 88.789424 / cusp11 92.9015 / cusp12 98.6118; Swiss houses_ex 'P' (SWIEPH confirmed, calc_ut retflag 258) ASC 135.489154 / MC 88.789425 / cusp11 92.8913 / cusp12 98.5862 -> ASC 506.8 arcsec, MC 0.005 arcsec. Independent 3,128-case grid (1800-2200 step 25, hours 0-21 step 3, lat 0 and +/-10..66) run through the SHIPPED computeChart, not a reconstruction: ASC p50 2.409 / p95 22.406 / max 512.529 arcsec (2100-03-21T06Z lat 66); |lat|<=45 max 16.22; 50-60 p95 20.05 max 26.36; >=63 p95 76.21; MC max 3.94; Placidus cusp max 512.5 - all matching the auditor to three decimals. Isolation: engine computeAngles with Swiss ARMC + Swiss true obliquity reproduces Swiss ASC/MC to 0 (formula exact); Swiss ARMC + engine mean obliquity gives max 504.8 (reproduces the defect); engine GAST + astronomy-engine e_tilt().tobl gives p50 0.22 / p95 2.2 / max 71.3 vs Swiss, the remainder being Swiss's own sidereal model. dObl(engine mean vs Swiss true) p50 6.09 max 9.675; dObl(e_tilt tobl vs Swiss true) max 0.030; engine mean vs Swiss mean 0.0000. ERFA spot-check (pyerfa 2.0.1.5 gst06a/obl06/nut06a, UT1:=UTC, TT via swe.deltat): 2100 engine RAMC-ERFA +0.146 vs Swiss-ERFA -1.793; 1950 +0.091 vs -0.001; Swiss eps_true - ERFA <= 0.001, supporting the auditor's arbiter claims. Additional observation from the finding-3 ladder: with the polar constant relaxed, the same mean-obliquity pairing produces cusp errors up to 8081 arcsec (2.2 deg) at |lat| 66.5, so this fix is a prerequisite for widening the Placidus band. Severity major is earned (accuracy gap materially below Swiss, 0.14 deg exceeding the project's own 0.1 deg ASC gate at lat 66); the README overstatement is separately finding 12.

## angles-houses-aspects-7 — Capability gap: 2 house systems, no house position, no Vertex/East Point/co-ascendants, no cusp speeds, no azimuth/altitude, no minor/angle/node/declination aspects in the package
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 6 d
- claim: HOUSE_SYSTEMS = ['whole','placidus'] (chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:8; validateBirthSettings rejects anything else, chunk-K5MRTCWE.js:4-6; HouseSystem type src/lib/engine/types.ts:10). Swiss 2.10.03 offers 25 house codes with swe.houses_ex2 cusp speeds, ascmc[2..7] (ARMC, Vertex, equatorial ascendant, co-ascendants Koch and Munkasey, polar ascendant), swe.house_pos, swe.houses_armc and swe.azalt. The aspect set is 5 majors with fixed orbs (8/4/7/7/8, luminary 10/5/8/8/10, chunk-INI453VG.js:9-15) over ten bodies only (ASPECT_BODIES :17-28 excludes nodes; angles are never aspected); no quincunx/semisextile/semisquare/sesquiquadrate/quintile, no configurable orbs.
- evidence: Swiss inventory measured with swe.house_name on this machine (swiss.json 'systems'): P Placidus, K Koch, O Porphyry, R Regiomontanus, C Campanus, E/A equal, W whole sign, V Vehlow, X Meridian, H horizon/azimuthal, T Polich/Page topocentric, B Alcabitius, M Morinus, U Krusinski, G Gauquelin sectors, Y APC, L Pullen SD, Q Pullen SR, I Sunshine, i Sunshine alt, D equal(MC), F Carter poli-equatorial, N equal 1=Aries, S Sripati; each computed at 66.3, 66.9, 69.65 and 85 deg with status recorded. Grep of src/ for koch|regiomontanus|porphyry|campanus finds only tests and Swiss fixtures, no implementation. Houses that dominate practice and are missing: Koch, Equal, Porphyry, Regiomontanus, Campanus.
- impact: Materially below Swiss for any practitioner: Koch and Equal are the second and third most requested systems, Porphyry is the polar fallback everyone else uses, Regiomontanus/Campanus are standard in horary and traditional work. Without house_pos and angle aspects the engine cannot claim to be 'more advanced'.
- fix: Implement, in this order, with closed forms and unit tests against swe.houses_ex/ex2 on the grid used here: Equal (ASC + 30k), Porphyry (trisection of quadrants), Koch (closed form via the MC's oblique ascension, polar limit 90 - eps), Regiomontanus and Campanus (closed forms on the celestial equator and prime vertical; valid at all latitudes with Swiss's MC-flip convention documented), Alcabitius, Morinus, Meridian, Vehlow, Topocentric; add ascmc extras (Vertex, East Point, co-ascendants), cusp speeds by analytic differentiation, house position with latitude (finding 5), azimuth/altitude with optional refraction, and an aspect configuration (aspect set, per-pair orbs, angle and node aspects).
- verifier: Engine side: HOUSE_SYSTEMS = ['whole','placidus'] (chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:8, also exported from index.js); src/lib/engine/types.ts:10 export type HouseSystem = 'whole' | 'placidus'; chunk-K5MRTCWE.js:4-6 throws RangeError('houseSystem must be "whole" or "placidus".') for anything else and is called by natalChart (index.js:290) and geo.js:288; ripgrep of src for koch|regiomontanus|porphyry|campanus (excluding tests, fixtures, policy files) finds no implementation. ASPECTS at chunk-INI453VG.js:9-15 are exactly 5 majors with orbs 8/4/7/7/8 and luminary 10/5/8/8/10; ASPECT_BODIES :17-28 is Sun..Pluto with no nodes; findAspects takes only body records so angles are never aspected. Swiss side on the local pyswisseph 2.10.03 (SWIEPH confirmed): house_name returns 25 codes PKORCEAWVXHTBMUGYLQIiDFNS as listed (A and E both report 'equal', so 24 distinct algorithms plus one alias - immaterial); houses_ex2, house_pos, houses_armc and azalt all exist; houses_ex2 returns cusp and ascmc speeds (e.g. 254.565 deg/d) and ascmc[2..7] = ARMC, Vertex, equatorial ascendant, co-ascendant Koch, co-ascendant Munkasey, polar ascendant. Severity major is earned: 2 vs 25 house systems, no house position, cusp speeds, extra angles or configurable aspects is a capability gap materially below Swiss.

## data-toolchain-packaging-1 — Compiler is hard-wired to DE440s' span; no pack can cover the site's 1800–2199 epoch
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 3 d
- corrected claim: As stated, plus: kernels SHORTER than 109,600 days also fail — the compiler extrapolates the kernel's last record past T1 (spkref clamps the index), the tail records blow the budgets (DE432s emb 52.6 km vs 0.3 km) without any compiler refusal, the pack passes integrity and geometry and opens, and every apparent() then fails with bad-geometry. Only a kernel whose span is exactly 109,600 days (DE440s) compiles into a usable pack.
- claim: tools/compiler/compile.mjs cannot compile a valid pack from any kernel other than DE440s (1849-12-26..2150-01-22). SPAN_DAYS=109600 and LADDER (divisors of 109600) are constants in sources.mjs; compile.mjs:428 caps gridTo at SPAN_DAYS/Ldays, so for DE440 (1550–2650, ~401,775 days, ratio 3.67) bodies would carry records for only the first 109,600 days while coverage.stopEtSecTdb is set to the kernel's T1 (compile.mjs:353,375,511), and container.mjs:257-259 would refuse the result as bad-geometry ('body spans .. but the pack claims coverage ..'). The site accepts 1800-01-01..2199-12-31 (src/mcp/bounds.ts:22-25, localToUtc tests) and the production engine computes any year, so the precision stack is structurally unable to serve 1800–1849 and 2150–2199. Unconfirmed by execution (no DE440 kernel on this machine); confirmed by code reading.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/tools/compiler/sources.mjs:55-59 (LADDER, SPAN_DAYS=109600); compile.mjs:427-428 (gridTo = Math.min(SPAN_DAYS / Ldays, ...)), :342 default kernel path; container.mjs:251-260 coverage-vs-span refusal. Coverage of the compiled pack: -4734072000..4735368000 s TDB (1849-12-26..2150-01-22), scratch D-header.txt. Size scaling from the measured 1.6571 MiB / 300.07 y: 2.21 MiB for 1800–2200, 6.07 MiB for DE440 1550–2650, 167.8 MiB for DE441 (python one-liner in transcript).
- impact: Swiss with sepl_18/semo_18 covers 1800–2400 and with the full .se1 set -13000..+17000; the alpha covers 300 years and the toolchain cannot extend it without code changes. Any production integration must either fall back to astronomy-engine outside 1849–2150 (a 60″-class accuracy cliff at the boundary) or fix this first.
- fix: Derive span from the kernel (T1-T0), let nrec = ceil(span/L) with a partial last record clipped to coverage, drop the divisibility ladder or regenerate choice.json per kernel via sweep.mjs, and default --kernel to a required argument. Add a tier-A test that compiles a synthetic SPK of a non-DE440s span. Then compile a 1550–2650 pack from DE440 (~6 MiB by scaling) or a 1790–2210 crop (~2.3 MiB) for the site.
- verifier: Executed what the auditor could only code-read. Downloaded de440.bsp (119,799,808 B, sha256 a4ce9bf9...c4b5) and de432s.bsp from NAIF; compiled with a scratch copy of the alpha (worktree untouched). (a) Full DE440, --candidate=D: rc=0 in 25.4 s, 1,740,208 B — the same size as the DE440s pack despite a 401,792-day kernel span; header coverage 1549-12-31..2650-01-25 but every body's records span -14200747200..-4731307200 s (1550→1850, exactly T0+109,600 d; short by 292,192 d); after seal.mjs, openPackFile refused with code bad-geometry 'body mercuryBary spans -14200747200..-4731307200 but the pack claims coverage -14200747200..20514081600' — the exact refusal predicted from container.mjs:257-259. (b) DE440 cropped --fromYear=1800 --toYear=2200 (the site's epoch): header coverage 1800-01-01..2200-01-01, records again end at -4731307200 (1850-01), short by 127,809 d, refused bad-geometry. So 'no pack can cover 1800–2199' is demonstrated, and the cause is compile.mjs:428 gridTo=Math.min(SPAN_DAYS/Ldays,...) with SPAN_DAYS=109600 in sources.mjs:58 while coverage.stopEtSecTdb is the kernel T1 (compile.mjs:375,511). (c) Shorter kernel DE432s (36,544 d): compiles (rc=0, 8.2 s, 0.61 MiB), seals and OPENS — but because spkref.mjs:92-94 clamps the record index and extrapolates the last kernel polynomial, tail records overrun T1 by up to 1,816 d; emb provenPosKm is 52.6 km against a 0.3 km budget (venus 0.44/0.3, sun 0.52/0.3, moon 0.022/0.010) and the compiler does not refuse; emb widths reach 8 bytes; every apparent() call (Sun, Moon, Mercury, Mars, Jupiter, Pluto at 2000/2024/2049) throws bad-geometry 'no determinable direction', while the auditor's DE440s pack answers the same instants. So the auditor's 'cannot compile a valid pack from any kernel other than DE440s' holds for shorter kernels too, via an opens-but-unusable mode rather than a refusal. Artifacts: verify/data-toolchain-packaging/{compile-de440.log,inspect-de440.txt,open-de440.txt,compile-de440-crop.log,inspect-de440-crop.txt,open-de440-crop.txt,compile-de432s.log,inspect-de432s.txt,open-de432s.txt,raw-probe.out,compare-de432s-vs-de440s.txt}. Severity major is earned: a capability gap that keeps coverage below Swiss's 1800–2400 and below the site's own 1800–2199 bounds.

## data-toolchain-packaging-4 — No distribution path reaches users: 404 on npm, private alpha, no pack, and the alpha already exceeds the engine-chunk budget
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 5 d
- claim: @zodiacs/engine returns HTTP 404 from registry.npmjs.org (confirmed 2026-09-22); @zodiacs/precision-alpha is `private: true`; no coefficient pack is distributed; and the alpha's released browser entry bundles to 73,509 B minified / 27,533 B gzip, already above budgets.json `engine-chunk: 25` KB before any pack loader, chart adapter or house code is added (the experimental entry is 30,363 B gzip). The accuracy the tree has measured (0.16″ worst vs Swiss for the DE440s prototype against 64.8″ for the shipped engine) is therefore unavailable to any user, and the site's bundle gate would refuse the runtime as it stands.
- evidence: curl registry.npmjs.org/@zodiacs%2Fengine → HTTP 404 (scratch npm-zodiacs-engine.json); examples/precision-alpha/package.json:4 private:true; NOTICE.md 'No ephemeris data'; esbuild minify+gzip of src/browser.mjs and src/experimental.mjs (scratch alpha-bundle-size.txt); budgets.json engine-chunk 25, scripts/report-bundles.mjs:230-239 enforces it on the static closure of the full.*.js chunk; docs/engine-validation/README.md 'What none of this is' bullet 4.
- impact: This is the gap that keeps the stack below Swiss in practice: Swiss is installable (pyswisseph, sweph npm bindings) and ships its data; Zodiacs' better-measured engine is not installable and has no data.
- fix: (a) Publish @zodiacs/engine (operator decision; the tarball is reproducible, see finding 10). (b) Use the NAIF permission that already exists: redistribute de440s.bsp UNMODIFIED (permitted outright per RIGHTS.md quote) from zodiacs.org with its own attribution, and compile the ZODEPH02 pack client-side in a Worker (24.5 s in Node here; cache the pack in the Cache API keyed by kernel sha256) or at site build time into a user-downloaded file — this avoids the unsettled derived-coefficient question. (c) Load the alpha runtime as a separate dynamically imported chunk with its own budget line (e.g. `precision-chunk: 40`), keeping the eager engine-chunk gate intact. (d) Email NAIF the derived-coefficient question in parallel.
- verifier: curl https://registry.npmjs.org/@zodiacs%2Fengine → HTTP 404 {"error":"Not found"} (npm-engine.json, 2026-09-22). examples/precision-alpha/package.json:4 private:true; NOTICE.md:34 '**No ephemeris data.** No JPL kernel, no compiled coefficient pack...'. esbuild 0.28.1 bundle+minify, gzip level 9 (the same method scripts/report-bundles.mjs:165 uses): src/browser.mjs 73,509 B min / 27,533 B gz (brotli 23,955); src/experimental.mjs 86,586 / 30,363; src/index.mjs 72,144 / 27,117 — the auditor's numbers reproduce byte-for-byte (bundle-size.txt). budgets.json engine-chunk = 25; report-bundles.mjs:214-221 enforces it on the static closure of the single full.*.js chunk; committed src/data/engine-bundle.json natal gzipKb 24.7. Caveat on the inference, not the facts: the 25 KB gate applies only to the eager engine closure; a lazily imported alpha chunk would face only chunk-max 60 KB, so 'the gate would refuse the runtime as it stands' holds if the alpha were folded into the full.*.js closure, which is the auditor's fix (c) anyway. Severity major earned: not installable, no data, against pip-installable Swiss with data.

## data-toolchain-packaging-5 — Public API is a chart API, not an ephemeris API; no calc(body,time,flags), no frames/centres/time scales/bounds
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 15 d
- claim: @zodiacs/engine exposes positions/natalChart/transits/synastry/moonPhase/saturnReturn/findLongitudeCrossings; BodyPosition is {body, lon, lat, speed, retrograde, sign, degree} — no distance, no latitude/radial speed, no frame choice (always true ecliptic of date), no centre choice (always geocentric), no time-scale input (Date = UTC only; TT via astronomy-engine's ΔT with no override), no sidereal ayanamsa, no fixed stars, no asteroids, two house systems, and `positions()` carries no engine identity (only Chart.engineVersion). The alpha's apparent(body, ttDays, options) adds lon/lat/distKm, light-time and nutation diagnostics, TT input and typed refusals, but returns no speeds, no equatorial/cartesian selection beyond the raw `geometric` triple, no topocentric/heliocentric/barycentric option, no sidereal mode, ten bodies with Mars-outward as system barycentres, and no per-result identity (pack digest lives on runtime.integrity, not on the result). Swiss exposes swe_calc_ut(tjd, ipl, iflag) → lon/lat/dist + 3 speeds with FLG_J2000/EQUATORIAL/XYZ/TOPOCTR/HELCTR/BARYCTR/SIDEREAL/TRUEPOS/NOABERR/NOGDEFL/NONUT, swe_houses_ex with ~20 systems, swe_fixstar, asteroids, swe_deltat_ex, swe_set_tid_acc.
- evidence: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/index.d.ts (public surface), dist/types-BQGrRiSd.d.ts:44-56 (BodyPosition), :78-87 (Chart), dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:35-41 (eclipticOfDate: GeoVector→Rotation_EQJ_ECT, no options); examples/precision-alpha/types/index.d.ts:33-63 (ApparentPlace, ReductionOptions), :336-353 (PrecisionRuntime); ephemeris.mjs BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE.
- impact: Without a uniform calc call a consumer cannot ask the Zodiacs stack the questions Swiss answers; 'more advanced than Swiss' is not testable through the current surface.
- fix: Proposed shape (both surfaces): `calc(body: Body, t: {jd:number, scale:'utc'|'ut1'|'tt'|'tdb'} | Date, opts?: {frame:'of-date'|'j2000'|'icrs', coords:'ecliptic'|'equatorial'|'cartesian', centre:'geocentric'|'topocentric'|'heliocentric'|'barycentric', observer?:{latDeg,lonDeg,heightM}, sidereal?:{ayanamsa:string}, corrections:{lightTime,aberration,deflection,nutation}, deltaT?: number|'model'}) => {lon,lat,dist,dlon,dlat,ddist, units, bounds?:{lonArcsec,latArcsec,distKm, basis:'proven'|'sampled'|'none'}, identity:{engine, ephemeris:{name,version,digest}, deltaTModel, options}}`, plus `bodies()` enumeration with centre/coverage per body, `houses(t, observer, system)` and `fixedStar(name, t, opts)` later. Implement over the alpha reducer first (it already has options and TT), then map the production API onto it.
- verifier: Read node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/index.d.ts: exports positions(date: DateInput), natalChart, transits, synastry, moonPhase, saturnReturn, findLongitudeCrossings — positions() takes no options; types-BQGrRiSd.d.ts:44-56 BodyPosition = {body, lon, lat, speed, retrograde, sign, degree} (no distance, no lat/radial speed), HouseSystem = 'whole' | 'placidus' (:17), engineVersion only on Chart (:86). examples/precision-alpha/types/index.d.ts:33-63: ApparentPlace has lon/lat/distKm, light-time and nutation diagnostics, geometric triple; ReductionOptions has nutation/bias/aberration/deflection/timescale/observerVelocity — no frame, centre, sidereal or speed output (grep for speed/velocity/dlon in the .d.ts: none); PrecisionRuntime (:336-353) exposes apparent/apparentAtJdTt/search/searchGeometric with integrity on the runtime, not the result; ten bodies, BARYCENTRE_NOT_CENTRE = Mars..Pluto (ephemeris.mjs:254); raw()/seriesAt() are internal (ephemeris.mjs:101,163), not on the runtime. Swiss side tested live on pyswisseph 2.10.03 with <swisslab>/ephe (swe-probe.out): calc_ut(Mars, SWIEPH|SPEED) retflag 258 (SWIEPH answered, 6 values incl. 3 speeds); SWIEPH|SPEED|EQUATORIAL|XYZ|J2000 retflag 6498; Moon TOPOCTR after set_topo retflag 33026; Mars HELCTR retflag 1546; houses_ex accepts 24 system letters (ABCDEFGHIKLMNOPQRSTUVWXY); fixstar_ut, deltat_ex, set_tid_acc, set_sid_mode, calc_pctr, nod_aps_ut all present (fixstar needs sefstars.txt, absent locally). Severity major earned: a capability gap relative to Swiss's uniform calc surface.

## production-event-search-1 — sky.json lunations use an aberration-free Sun: 40 s late vs Horizons, Swiss and the site's own transits files
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: scripts/build-sky.mjs computes new/full moons with astronomy-engine's SearchMoonPhase, whose MoonPhase → PairLongitude calls GeoVector(body, time, false) (no aberration) for both bodies; the Sun's ~20" annual aberration therefore shifts every lunation instant by ~+40 s relative to the apparent-longitude convention used by the site's charts, by scripts/build-transits.mjs (which bisects bodyLongitude apparent longitudes), by Swiss and by JPL Horizons. Two committed data files disagree by ~40 s for the same physical event and the public sky API publishes the wrong one at millisecond precision.
- evidence: node_modules/astronomy-engine/esm/astronomy.js:4247-4256 (PairLongitude uses GeoVector(..., false)) and :4623 (MoonPhase); scripts/build-sky.mjs:199-207 (SearchMoonPhase). Measurement s9: all 124 committed sky.json lunations vs Swiss bisection of apparent Moon−Sun elongation: median |Δ| 32.5 s, p95 40.2 s, max 42.7 s, signed mean +32.8 s (sky later) — out/s9-sky-vs-swiss.json. Internal cross-check: transits-2026-01..11.json lunations minus sky.json for the same 124 events: mean −39.5 s, max 46.3 s (inline python in this audit, reproducible with the two committed files). Horizons arbiter (QUANTITIES 31, apparent, DE441) for the 2027-01-07 new moon: Horizons 20:24:23.225Z, sky.json 20:25:05.910Z (+42.7 s), Swiss 20:24:23.230Z (0.0 s) — out/horizons-arbiter.json, horizons/{sun,moon}-newmoon-2027-01-07.txt. src/lib/sky-api/build.ts:23 joins the two sources with a 6-hour tolerance, so the disagreement is absorbed rather than fixed; src/pages/developers/index.astro:36 advertises /api/v1/moon-phases instants.
- impact: The site's own lunation times are inconsistent with each other and with every reference by 40 s; Swiss users never see this. It also means the homepage/moon-phase/API instants are not the apparent-Sun convention the methodology page implies.
- fix: In build-sky.mjs replace SearchMoonPhase with the same apparent-longitude bisection build-transits.mjs uses (bodyLongitude('Moon') − bodyLongitude('Sun') through @zodiacs/engine/internal), regenerate sky.json, add a drift test asserting sky.json and transits-YYYY-MM.json lunations agree to < 2 s, and tighten LUNATION_JOIN_TOLERANCE_MS to minutes.
- verifier: Code confirmed: scripts/build-sky.mjs:199-207 uses SearchMoonPhase; astronomy.js:4247-4256 PairLongitude calls GeoVector(body, time, false) for both bodies, :4623 MoonPhase wraps it. Mechanism measured directly (v1-engine-epochs.mjs): at the 2027-01-07 new moon the aberration-free Sun is +20.845" ahead of the aberrated Sun while the Moon differs by 0" (GeoVector returns GeoMoon regardless of the flag), and SearchMoonPhase returns exactly the committed 2027-01-07T20:25:05.910Z. Committed-file cross-check I ran on the 60 transits-YYYY-MM.json files: 124 lunations matched to sky.json, transits minus sky mean -39.5 s (range -46.3..-33.7 s). s9 rerun identical: 124 lunations vs Swiss apparent elongation bisection (flag 258 asserted) median 32.45 s, p95 40.16 s, max 42.68 s, all sky.json later. Horizons cached tables reparsed: Horizons (IAU76/80 ecliptic-of-date apparent, UT, per the file footer) new moon 20:24:23.225Z, sky.json +42.7 s, Swiss +0.0 s. LUNATION_JOIN_TOLERANCE_MS = 6 h at src/lib/sky-api/build.ts:23 and the /api/v1/moon-phases listing at developers/index.astro:36 confirmed. Major is earned: two committed datasets disagree by 40 s and the public API publishes the non-apparent one.

## production-event-search-2 — Solar-return and Sun-ingress instants run 37–39 s late against JPL Horizons; Swiss is within 1.2 s
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 4 d
- corrected claim: The engine's Sun longitude carries a seasonal error of the truncated VSOP87 Earth series (engine minus Swiss apparent Sun +1.2" in mid-February to -1.67" in early June 2026, zero near April and December), which the Sun's 0.04"/s motion amplifies to solar-return and Sun-ingress instants between ~28 s EARLY (Feb-Mar) and ~42 s LATE (May-Jul); the committed Sun ingresses have signed mean +0.5 s, median |Δ| 9.8 s, max +38.6 s, and the 12-birth return corpus has three negative cases (-15.4, -13.5, -6.4 s). The 37-39 s lag is the mid-year extreme, not a uniform bias; Swiss stays within ~1.2 s of Horizons year-round. Chart-angle impact 0.09-0.24° applies at the extremes.
- claim: solarReturnInstant and the Sun ingress builder find the correct root of the engine's Sun longitude, but that longitude is ~1.3–1.8" behind DE441 in mid-2026 and the Sun moves only 0.04"/s, so the instant is amplified to ~38 s late; the returned solar-return chart's angles move 0.09–0.24° as a result. The methodology page says solar returns are 'accurate to the same standard as every other chart', which hides the ×24 s-per-arcsecond amplification.
- evidence: Swiss matched-target comparison (same natal Sun longitude fed to swe.solcross_ut) over 12 births 1950–2002, returns in 2026: engine − Swiss median +28.1 s, max +40.0 s; natal-derived (Swiss natal Sun) median 16.8 s, max 38.0 s — out/s5b-swiss-returns.json. Sun ingresses 2026–2029 from src/data/ingresses.json vs swe.solcross_ut: n=41, median 9.8 s, p95 28.9 s, max 38.6 s — out/s9-sky-vs-swiss.json. Horizons arbiter (apparent ecliptic longitude, 1-minute table, linear interpolation): solar return C9 (target 101.30535°) Horizons 2026-07-03T04:56:08.417Z, engine +38.8 s, Swiss −1.2 s; C6 engine +37.4 s, Swiss −1.2 s; Sun 60° ingress 2026-05-21 Horizons 00:36:45.484Z, ingresses.json +37.4 s, Swiss −1.2 s — out/horizons-arbiter.json. Chart impact at the engine instant vs the Horizons instant: ASC shift 0.121° (London), 0.242° (New York), MC 0.146–0.177° — out/s12-solar-return-impact.json. Delta-T explains only part: astronomy-engine TT−UT 75.36 s at 2026-07-01 vs Swiss 68.9 s (out/s12, out/s9), which would make Sun events ~6.5 s EARLY, so the Sun series error is ~1.8" (≈45 s) partly offset by the clock.
- impact: Solar return charts (the most-used timed chart) carry a systematic ~0.1–0.25° angle error and ~38 s timestamp error that Swiss does not; Sun ingress instants in the public API inherit the same bias. Exceeding Swiss here requires a Sun to better than ~0.05" (≈1 s) or a DE-backed Sun.
- fix: Put a DE440-backed (or full untruncated VSOP87 Earth) Sun under bodyLongitude for the Sun/Earth path used by solar-return.ts, year-scan.ts and build-ingresses.mjs, together with finding 3's clock fix; add a Horizons-anchored regression fixture for two solar-return instants with a 3 s gate; state the amplification (24 s per arcsecond) on the methodology page until then.
- verifier: s5a/s5b rerun identical (solar same-target engine-Swiss median +28.1 s, max +40.0 s, flag 258); cached Horizons arbiter reparsed identical (C9 +38.8 s, C6 +37.4 s, Sun 60° ingress +37.4 s, Swiss -1.2 s in each). My monthly probe (v1b-swiss-compare.py) of engine Sun minus Swiss apparent Sun on the 1st/15th of every 2026 month: +0.324" (Jan 1), +1.199" (Feb 15, event shift -28.5 s), +0.254" (Apr 1), -0.827" (May 1, +20.5 s), -1.672" (Jun 1, +41.9 s), -1.524" (Jul 1, +38.4 s), -1.410" (Oct 15, +34.1 s), +0.010" (Dec 1). Both Horizons solar-return cases (C6, C9) were July births, the worst quadrant; the s9 ingress table shows Pisces -28.9 s and Gemini/Cancer +38.6/+38.0 s with signed mean +0.47 s. The methodology sentence 'accurate to the same standard as every other chart here' is at src/pages/methodology/index.astro:166-167 as cited. Delta-T contribution (-6.5 s) confirmed, so the underlying series error in June is ~1.9".

## production-event-search-3 — Delta-T model biases every event instant: Moon events 5–7 s early now and growing
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: astronomy-engine converts the JS Date (UTC) to TT with the Espenak–Meeus polynomial, giving TT−UT = 75.36 s on 2026-07-01 and 76.30 s on 2028-01-01, while the real TT−UTC has been 69.184 s exactly since 2017 (Swiss reports 68.9 s). Every root the crossing solvers find is therefore shifted in UTC by the difference: lunar returns, Moon ingresses, void-of-course boundaries and Moon aspects are ~6 s early today and the error grows ~0.5 s/year.
- evidence: out/s12-solar-return-impact.json: astronomyEngineDeltaT_s {2020: 71.578, 2026-07: 75.356, 2028: 76.298, 2100: 202.652}; out/s9: swissDeltaT_s {2026: 68.9, 2028: 68.8}. Lunar returns, same target, engine − Swiss: all 12 cases negative, median −5.25 s, max −6.75 s (out/s5b). Horizons arbiter lunar return C1 (target 168.003078°): Horizons 2026-09-11T02:42:01.117Z, engine −6.6 s, Swiss −0.1 s (out/horizons-arbiter.json, horizons/moon-lunar-return-C1-2026-09-11.txt). Moon sign ingresses 2026–2028 (src/data/aura-moon-ingresses.json, 481 events) vs swe.mooncross_ut: signed mean −5.87 s, max 10.1 s (out/s9). The residual is the Delta-T gap (≈ −6.5 s) partly offset by the missing −0.7" Moon light-time term (finding 13, ≈ +1.3 s). docs/engine-validation/README.md concedes only the 2100 extrapolation (109.5 s) and RESULTS.md the 2.4 s at 2020; the present-day 6.5 s bias is not recorded.
- impact: A uniform time bias on every event Zodiacs publishes; Swiss and Horizons agree to ~0.1 s on the Moon. Fixing this alone brings lunar returns, Moon ingresses and VOC boundaries from ~6 s to <1 s of Horizons — better than the 1.2 s Swiss shows on Sun events.
- fix: Before calling astronomy-engine, convert UTC → TT with the exact leap-second table (TT−UTC = 32.184 + TAI−UTC, 37 s since 2017) for 1972-01-01 onward, treat UT1−UTC (|DUT1| < 0.9 s) as the only residual and document it, and use IERS Bulletin A/C extrapolation for the next few years; keep Espenak–Meeus only before 1972. Pass the resulting TT into MakeTime via AstroTime.FromTerrestrialTime. Add a fixture: 2026 Moon ingress vs Horizons within 1.5 s.
- verifier: My run: astronomy-engine MakeTime tt-ut = 71.578 s (2020-01-01), 75.356 s (2026-07-01), 75.497 s (2026-09-22), 76.298 s (2028-01-01); Swiss swe.deltat = 69.361 / 68.843 / 68.821 / 68.8 s; exact TT-UTC since 2017 = 32.184 + 37 = 69.184 s, so the engine clock is 6.2 s fast today. s5b rerun identical: lunar returns same-target engine-Swiss all 12 negative, median -5.25 s, max -6.75 s (Swiss Moon at the engine instant is 1.9-3.9" short of target). s9 rerun: 481 Moon ingresses signed mean -5.87 s, max 10.09 s. Lunar return C1 recomputed from the cached Horizons 1-minute Moon table (target 168.003078°): Horizons 2026-09-11T02:42:01.117Z, engine -6.6 s, Swiss -0.1 s. docs/engine-validation/README.md:81 concedes only the 2100 extrapolation gap (109.5 s); no present-day figure or leap-second table anywhere (grep 69.184/leap second: none). One sub-attribution is wrong in sign but does not affect the claim: omitting the Moon's -0.67" light-time puts the engine Moon AHEAD of the apparent Moon and makes Moon events ~1.2 s earlier (compounding, not offsetting); the smaller-than-6.5 s observed bias is instead offset by the engine's own Moon series residual.

## production-event-search-4 — Fixed-step crossing scan silently drops grazing pass pairs in Saturn returns and yearScan; the code comment claims the 5-day step is safe
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 2 d
- claim: findLongitudeCrossingsWith (and the package's findLongitudeCrossings) detect roots only by sign change between fixed samples with no station splitting and no derivative bound. When a body stations within a small dip of the target, the two crossings fall inside one 5-day step and are both lost. returns.ts:37-38 asserts 'The default 5-day step is safe for Saturn … a triple pass spans months, never 5 days', which is false for grazing seasons.
- evidence: Real-ephemeris construction (out/s3-saturn-graze.json, 12.4 s): Saturn's 2019-04-30T00:48Z retrograde station at 290.51794°; births in Feb 1990 with natal Saturn = station − dip. dip 0.003° (pair 2.7 days apart): saturnReturns and package saturnReturn both report 3 first-return passes (2019-04-28, 05-01, 12-24), matching a 0.25-day reference. dip 0.002° (7.2", pair 2.21 days): both report ONE pass (2019-12-24 only); the 0.25-day reference still finds 3 (2019-04-28T11:25, 05-01T14:15, 12-24T10:04); total crossings 7 → 5, no error, no flag. Saturn curvature at that station −1.64e-3 °/day², so the blind band at a 5-day step is 0.0103° (37"); order-of-magnitude chance per station-bearing season ≈ 6e-5 of natal longitudes. yearScan (Jupiter conjunction to natal Sun, out/s3b-yearscan-phase.json): at dips ≤0.005° (18") the pair is dropped for 2 of 5 grid phases, at 0.001° for 4 of 5; Jupiter blind band 0.0205°. Synthetic mid-cell parabola: pair 4.47 days apart, step 5 → 0 found, step 1 → 2 (out/s3b). docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/search/RESULTS.md §5 already records the same mechanism on Uranus (0.002° dip: 5-day scan 0 found); this adds the Saturn-return and yearScan product paths and the false comment.
- impact: Rare (≈1 in 10^4 seasons) but silent and unflagged: a user gets one return date where there are three. Swiss offers no guarantee either, so this is parity-at-best; the alpha's derivative-enclosure search is the only route to a certified count.
- fix: Short term: in returns.ts/year-scan.ts split the scan at stations exactly as transit-scan-core.ts:231-268 does, or scan at ≤1 day for Jupiter/Saturn (cost ×5, still <1 s), and rewrite the comment to state the blind band. Medium term: back findLongitudeCrossingsWith with the alpha's interval search (examples/precision-alpha/src/core/validated-search.mjs searchGeometricLongitude pattern: exclusion + monotone cells from Lipschitz bounds) and return typed verdicts so an unresolved cell is reported, never dropped.
- verifier: lib/ copies verified identical to src/lib/engine/*.ts except import specifiers. s3 and s3b reruns byte-identical to the auditor's: Saturn retrograde station 2019-04-30T00:48:18.8Z at 290.51794°, curvature -1.64e-3 °/day², blind dip at a 5-day step 0.01026° (36.9"); real Feb-1990 births with natal Saturn = station - dip: dip 0.003° gives 3 first-return passes in both saturnReturns and the package saturnReturn (matching the 0.25-day reference 04-28, 05-01, 12-24); dip 0.002° and below gives ONE pass (2019-12-24) in both, total crossings 7 -> 5, no error or flag, while the 0.25-day reference still finds 3 (pair 2.21 days apart). yearScan Jupiter conjunction to natal Sun: dip <= 0.005° loses both passes for 2 of 5 grid phases, 0.001° for 4 of 5; synthetic mid-cell pair 4.47 days apart: step 5 finds 0, step 1 finds 2. returns.ts:37-38 carries the quoted comment verbatim; year-scan.ts:63 calls findLongitudeCrossings with the default 5-day step and no station split. RESULTS.md §5 (lines 350-370) already records the Uranus analogue and quotes the same comment, as the finding says. Major earned: shipped code silently returns one date where three exist.

## production-event-search-7 — Outer-planet station instants in sky.json are 20–40 minutes off JPL Horizons and published to the millisecond with no uncertainty
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 3 d
- corrected claim: Outer-planet station instants in sky.json are 20-45 minutes off the DE441 reference and published to the millisecond: the Neptune direct station 2027-12-15 is 09:42:46.8Z in sky.json vs Horizons 09:06:03Z (body 899) / 09:07:36Z (Neptune barycenter 8), +2204/+2110 s, where Swiss is +17/-76 s; the cause is the engine's Neptune series (engine minus Swiss ~+11.1" with a ~0.02-0.05"/day speed error, worth ~36 min at the 5.65e-4 °/day² station acceleration), not the ±6 h finite-difference/16-bisection method, which lands within 6 s of a golden-section extremum on the same longitude. The Pluto comparison must be against the Pluto-Charon barycenter (Horizons 9), which is what Swiss and the engine model: barycenter station 2028-05-09T09:33:32Z, sky.json -2704 s (45 min early), Swiss -241 s; Horizons 999 (body center) is 4052 s earlier because of the 0.08" Charon wobble. The claim that Swiss with the .se1 files is worse than the engine on Pluto is refuted, and the local .se1 files report DE441 (denum 441), not DE431.
- claim: build-sky.mjs locates stations by the sign change of a ±6 h finite-difference speed and bisects 16 times (1.3 s resolution). Station time is ill-conditioned (δt = δv/acceleration), so the engine's ~0.05"/day speed error in Neptune/Pluto becomes tens of minutes; the retrogrades page says 'refined to the hour' and the API (/api/v1/stations, /retrogrades) emits ISO milliseconds without any band.
- evidence: out/s9-sky-vs-swiss.json: 92 committed station instants vs Swiss analytic-speed zero (bisected to <0.1 s): median |Δ| 170 s, p95 1396 s, max 2463 s; by planet max/median: Pluto 2463/612 s, Neptune 2186/819 s, Uranus 952/349 s, Saturn 813/225 s, Jupiter 295/134 s, Mercury 338/126 s. Horizons arbiter (hourly apparent longitude, quartic fit): Neptune direct station 2027-12-15 Horizons 09:06:03Z, sky.json 09:42:47Z (+2203.6 s), Swiss 09:06:20Z (+17 s); Pluto retrograde station 2028-05-09 Horizons 08:26:01Z, sky.json 08:48:28Z (+1347 s), Swiss(.se1, DE431) 09:29:31Z (+3811 s) — out/horizons-arbiter.json, horizons/{neptune-station-2027,pluto-station-2028}.txt. Code: scripts/build-sky.mjs:53-77,108-129; src/pages/retrogrades/index.astro:76; src/pages/developers/index.astro:33-34.
- impact: Neptune stations are 2200 s from the DE441 answer where Swiss is 17 s; the API's millisecond timestamps are precision theatre. For Pluto, Swiss with the .se1 files (DE431) is worse than the engine against DE441 — a DE440/441-backed engine would beat Swiss-as-commonly-installed here.
- fix: Publish each station with a level-set time envelope (the instants where |speed| ≤ the body's measured speed residual), display 'about HH:MM ± n min' and put the band in the API schema; move the station search onto a DE440-backed longitude (the prototype in docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/prototype) whose Neptune agrees with Horizons to seconds; add a Horizons-anchored fixture for one Neptune station with a 60 s gate.
- verifier: s9 rerun identical (92 stations: median 170 s, p95 1396 s, max 2463 s). Cached Horizons Neptune 899 reparsed: station 09:06:03Z; my fits at ±6/12/24 h with degree 2/4 all within ~1 min. My Horizons request COMMAND 8 (Neptune Barycenter, DE441, 2027-12-12..18, 1 h, QUANTITIES 31): station 09:07:36Z, sky.json +2110 s, Swiss -76 s. Golden-section minimum of build-sky's own lonAt (GeoVector aberration=true, EQJ->ECT; identical to @zodiacs/engine bodyLongitude to 0") gives 09:42:40.9Z, 6 s from the committed value, so the method is not the error. engine minus Swiss apparent Neptune Nov 2027-Jan 2028: +11.40" -> +10.70" (slope ~-0.02 to -0.05"/day); Swiss acceleration 5.65e-4 °/day² -> 0.06"/day = 42 min. Pluto: swe.get_current_file_data reports sepl_18.se1 denum 441 (flag 258 returned); Swiss Pluto acceleration -4.43e-4 °/day², so the 0.082" (measured 999-vs-9 longitude difference, ±0.081") Charon wobble is a ~0.08"/day speed wobble worth ~72 min of station time. My Horizons request COMMAND 9 (Pluto Barycenter, DE441, 2028-05-06..12, 1 h): station 09:33:32Z; sky.json -2704 s, Swiss -241 s, body-center 999 -4052 s. Retrogrades page 'refined to the hour' (retrogrades/index.astro:76) and millisecond ISO instants in sky.json/API confirmed.

## production-event-search-8 — Eclipse data omits everything Swiss provides beyond peak and kind: contacts, magnitudes, Saros, geography, local circumstances
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 4 d
- corrected claim: scripts/build-eclipses.mjs:66-104 stores only type, kind, peak, sign/lon/degree, obscuration and totalMinutes; peaks agree with Swiss to 13.7 s (24/24 kinds match), but none of the contact times, penumbral/partial semi-durations, greatest-eclipse coordinates or local circumstances that astronomy-engine already exposes (sd_penum/sd_partial, GlobalSolarEclipseInfo.latitude/longitude, SearchLocalSolarEclipse) are used, and Saros, magnitudes and observer circumstances that pyswisseph's sol_eclipse_when_glob/where/how/when_loc and lun_eclipse_how/when_loc return are absent. Correction to the totality sub-claim: the committed totalMinutes exceed Swiss's total_end-total_begin by 0.6-1.35 min because astronomy-engine's sd_total itself is larger (raw 2·sd_total = 59.41, 72.26, 102.51, 54.95 min vs Swiss 58.3, 71.3, 101.9, 53.6) — a shadow-model convention difference, not the Math.round (which contributes at most 0.5 min and is -0.4 min in the first case).
- claim: scripts/build-eclipses.mjs stores type, kind, peak, sign/lon/degree, obscuration and totality minutes. It uses none of the contact times, penumbral/partial durations, greatest-eclipse latitude/longitude, or SearchLocalSolarEclipse that astronomy-engine already exposes, and cannot produce magnitude, Saros series/member, central-line or observer circumstances that swe.sol_eclipse_when_glob/where/how/when_loc and swe.lun_eclipse_how/when_loc return. Peak times themselves agree with Swiss to 14 s.
- evidence: out/s8-eclipses-vs-swiss.json: all 24 committed 2026–2030 eclipses vs Swiss: kind matches 24/24; peak residual median 6.3 s, max 13.7 s; committed totalMinutes exceed Swiss totality by 0.7–1.4 min (59 vs 58.3, 72 vs 71.3, 103 vs 101.9, 55 vs 53.6 — the builder rounds 2·sd_total). Swiss fields available and unused, recorded per event in the artifact: Saros series/member (e.g., 2026-08-12 total: 126/48), C1–C4 and totality contacts, umbral/penumbral magnitude, central lat/lon. astronomy-engine surface unused: LunarEclipseInfo.sd_penum/sd_partial, GlobalSolarEclipseInfo.latitude/longitude/distance, SearchLocalSolarEclipse/NextLocalSolarEclipse (node_modules/astronomy-engine/astronomy.d.ts:2781-3028). Code: scripts/build-eclipses.mjs:66-104.
- impact: Peak timing is at parity with Swiss (seconds), but the /eclipses/ page and API cannot answer the questions eclipse users ask Swiss-based tools (visible where, when does it start here, how deep). This is a completeness gap, not an accuracy one.
- fix: Extend the builder with astronomy-engine's contact times, sd_* durations and greatest-eclipse coordinates now (0.5 d); add a per-observer route using SearchLocalSolarEclipse (partial begin/end, peak altitude, obscuration); compute Saros from the known series epochs (a table lookup); later derive Besselian elements from the DE-backed ephemeris for path polygons.
