Fix totalMinutes to (sd_total·2) unrounded or to Swiss-style contact difference.
- verifier: s8 rerun identical: 24 events, kindMismatches [], peak residual median 6.3 s max 13.7 s; per-event Swiss Saros (e.g. 2026-08-12 total: 126/48), C1-C4 and totality contacts, umbral/penumbral magnitudes and central lat/lon present in the artifact and absent from src/data/eclipses.json. pyswisseph 2.10.03 exposes sol_eclipse_how/when_glob/when_loc/where, lun_eclipse_how/when/when_loc, lun_occult_when_glob/when_loc/where (dir(swe)). astronomy.d.ts:2761-2762 (sd_penum, sd_partial) and :2814 (SearchLocalSolarEclipse) confirmed; I ran SearchGlobalSolarEclipse (2026-08-12 total, lat 65.22, lon -25.25) and SearchLocalSolarEclipse for Madrid (partial_begin 17:36:37Z, peak 18:32:14Z, altitude 7.3°) to confirm the unused surface works. build-eclipses.mjs:99-101 computes Math.round(2 * lunar.sd_total). Major earned as a capability gap that keeps eclipse features materially below Swiss.

## production-positions-1 — Truncated VSOP87 series, not the reduction, sets the engine's accuracy floor (up to 15.6" today)
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 8 d
- claim: astronomy-engine 2.1.19 evaluates a VSOP87 cut down to 12-58 longitude terms per planet (Mercury L=12, Venus 13, Earth 32, Mars 58, Jupiter 41, Saturn 49, Uranus 45, Neptune 13; smallest longitude amplitude kept: Neptune 18.6", Mercury 15.6", Venus 2.5", Jupiter/Uranus 2.0", Saturn 1.8", Mars 0.5", Earth 0.3"). The resulting geocentric direction error, measured geometric-to-geometric against DE440s with no reduction or Delta-T involved, is Neptune p50 15.0"/max 15.6", Saturn 8.0"/9.1", Uranus 4.2"/5.0", Jupiter 2.6"/4.0", Venus 2.1"/7.1", Mercury 2.2"/5.4", Mars 1.1"/4.9", Sun 0.7"/1.7", Moon 1.3"/4.4", Pluto 1.4"/1.5" (daily 2025). The full shipped chain with TT pinned gives the same numbers (Neptune lon +11.6" mean with lat +9.6", Saturn -7.8", Uranus -1.4" with lat +4.1", Jupiter -2.0"), so light-time/aberration/precession/nutation contribute under ~0.3" and the series is the whole gap to Swiss. Swiss's .se1 (DE431) agrees with DE440s to 0.000-0.003" on the same rows.
- evidence: <worktree>/node_modules/astronomy-engine/astronomy.js:386 (const vsop) and :2918-2979 (VsopFormula/CalcVsop); term counts in <audit>/production-positions/vsop-term-counts.txt; residuals in compare.json keys T2_daily2025_pinnedTT_engine_minus_swissApparent and T8b_daily2025_geometric_series_only; arbiter horizons/arbiter.json: at 2025-07-01 00:00 TT engine-Horizons Saturn -8.334" lon/-1.77" lat, Neptune +11.501"/+9.632", Uranus -1.306"/+3.987", while Swiss-Horizons is +0.046..0.048" for every body. The committed in-suite gate (docs/engine-validation/README.md: Saturn 1.93" at 2020-01-01) misses this because the truncation error is long-period and two dates cannot see it.
- impact: Every planet except the Sun/Mars is 2-16" from Swiss and from Horizons in ordinary modern charts, a hundred times the 0.13" the DE440s prototype already achieves; this alone keeps the stack below Swiss and no reduction work can fix it.
- fix: Replace astronomy-engine's planetary/Moon series with a DE440s (or DE441 for 1550-2650) Chebyshev pack compiled by examples/precision-alpha/tools/compiler (the committed prototype reached 0.134" max vs Swiss on 160 rows) and keep astronomy-engine only as a fallback outside pack coverage, flagged. If a data download is unacceptable for the homepage bundle, ship full VSOP87 (Sun/planets, ~0.1-1") plus ELP2000-82B (Moon) as a lazy chunk and reserve the DE pack for the full chart; either way stop shipping the 1-arcminute truncation.
- verifier: Independently parsed `const vsop` at astronomy.js:386: Mercury L12/B9/R8, Venus 13/4/9, Earth 32/2/17, Mars 58/8/25, Jupiter 41/7/24, Saturn 49/12/30, Uranus 45/6/25, Neptune 13/5/12; smallest kept longitude amplitude Neptune 18.55", Mercury 15.64", Earth 0.26" (auditor's numbers exact). My own run (engine-verify.mjs + swiss-verify.py, every 3rd day of 2025 = 122 instants, TT pinned via AstroTime.FromTerrestrialTime, roundtrip <1 ms; every Swiss retflag asserted SWIEPH and not MOSEPH): engine minus Swiss apparent lon p50/max Neptune 11.57/12.19" (mean +11.60, lat mean +9.60), Saturn 7.99/9.17 (mean -7.75), Uranus 1.41/1.66 (lat mean +4.13), Jupiter 1.86/3.40 (mean -2.04), Venus 1.53/5.81, Mercury 1.55/5.19, Mars 0.44/2.94, Sun 0.39/1.40, Pluto 1.35/1.69. Series-only geometric (HelioVector/GeoMoon EQJ) vs jplephem on de440s.bsp, angular separation p50/max: Neptune 15.05/15.60", Saturn 8.06/9.10, Uranus 4.18/5.04, Jupiter 2.61/4.00, Venus 2.10/7.12, Mercury 2.21/5.22, Mars 1.04/4.88, Sun 0.73/1.74, Moon 1.29/3.76, Pluto 1.45/1.54; Swiss ICRS geometric vs DE440s <= 0.002" for every body. Re-parsed the cached Horizons responses (DE441, TIME_TYPE=TT, CENTER=500@399, Q31 = IAU76/80 ecliptic-of-date apparent): 2025-07-01 engine minus Horizons Saturn -8.334"/-1.77", Neptune +11.501"/+9.632", Uranus -1.306"/+3.987", Sun -0.519"; Swiss +0.046..0.048" on every body. docs/engine-validation/README.md:49 does show Saturn 1.93" at 2020-01-01; my 2000-01-01 row gives Saturn +1.65", Uranus -11.1", Neptune +8.7", confirming the long-period character. ## production-positions-2 — Delta-T model is 6.7 s too large today (>=5.4 s beyond any physical value), 110 s at 2100, and is neither exposed nor documented
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 3 d
- claim: astronomy-engine's DeltaT_EspenakMeeus (2005-2050 polynomial 62.92+0.32217u+0.005589u^2) gives 75.50 s at 2026-09-22 while Swiss gives 68.82 s. Independently of Swiss, TT-UTC is exactly 69.184 s in 2026 and |UT1-UTC| < 0.9 s, so the true Delta-T lies in [68.3, 70.1] s and the engine is at least 5.4 s high. Measured Moon displacement on the production UT path: 3.41" at modern-06 (2026-09-20T08Z) vs 0.10" with TT pinned. Ladder 1800-2200 (engine minus Swiss): -5.1 s (1800, Moon -3.0"), -0.05..+0.7 s 1940-2010, +2.5 s 2020, +8.5 s 2030 (4.3"), +19.2 s 2050 (10.4"), +36.8 s 2060 (slope kink at the 2050 branch), +110.4 s 2100 (54.4"), +207 s 2150 (114"), +280 s 2200 (173"), +537 s 2400 (Moon 303"). The package has no Delta-T API, receipt field or README statement (grep of dist/*.js, *.d.ts, README.md finds none); the receipt conventions block omits the time scale entirely.
- evidence: astronomy.js:1023-1136 (DeltaT_EspenakMeeus), :1157 (TerrestrialTime), chunk-GBH7JIYF.js MakeTime(date) calls; compare.json key T9_deltaT_ladder (columns engine_deltaT_s, swiss_deltaT_s, moon_displacement_arcsec, measured_moon_lon_diff_productionUT_arcsec agree to <1"); command output 'DELTA-T TODAY' in this session (AE 75.50 s / Swiss 68.82 s at 2026-09-22); committed docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/RESULTS.md already concedes the 2100 figure (this adds the present-day error and the 1800-2200 ladder).
- impact: The Moon of every chart cast today is displaced ~3.5" by the time scale alone, growing ~0.6"/yr, and any far-future or far-past chart is dominated by this; Swiss uses an IERS-tabulated Delta-T with a documented extrapolation and exposes swe_deltat/swe_set_delta_t_userdef.
- fix: Add a Delta-T module: IERS/Bulletin-A tabulated values through the present, Stephenson-Morrison-Hohenkerk 2016 spline for the past, a documented extrapolation beyond the table, an override input (pin Delta-T or pass TT directly), and a receipt field recording the value used. Inject it via astronomy-engine's SetDeltaTFunction until the series are replaced.
- verifier: node: A.MakeTime(2026-09-22T00Z) tt-ut = 75.497 s; swe.deltat_ex(julday(2026,9,22,0), FLG_SWIEPH) = 68.821 s (diff 6.68 s). TT-UTC = 32.184+37 = 69.184 s in 2026 and |UT1-UTC| < 0.9 s, so true Delta-T is within [68.28, 70.08] s and the engine is >= 5.41 s high independent of Swiss. Ladder (engine vs Swiss): 1800-07-01 13.57 vs 18.68 (-5.1 s); 2030-07-01 77.92 vs 69.40 (+8.52); 2050 93.93 vs 74.73 (+19.21); 2060 114.63 vs 77.80 (+36.83, kink); 2100-07-01 203.82 vs 93.41 (+110.4); 2200-07-01 443.19 vs 163.48 (+279.7); 2399-12-01 1056.0 vs 519.3 (+537). Moon on the production UT path at 2026-09-20T08Z: engine minus swe.calc_ut = 3.41" vs 0.10" with TT pinned (predicted dT x rate 3.31"); 2030 4.16", 2050 10.46", 2060 20.62"; 2100-01-01 production Moon residual 64.77" vs 0.86" pinned; 2025 daily production Moon residual mean +2.64" vs -0.54" pinned with Delta-T diff 5.80 s. grep -i 'delta.?t|deltaT|terrestrial' over dist/*.js, dist/*.d.ts and README.md of @zodiacs/engine finds nothing; receipt.js CONVENTIONS (lines 138-147) has no time-scale entry. docs/engine-validation/README.md:214 concedes only the 2100 figure (93.18 vs 202.65, which my epoch-2100 row reproduces). Major, not blocking: no documented time-scale guarantee is contradicted.

## production-positions-3 — computeChart passes the MEAN obliquity to the angles while bodies use the TRUE ecliptic and GAST: Ascendant off by up to 18.5" vs Swiss today
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 1 d
- claim: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js computeChart builds angleInput with obliquity: meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(input.utc)) but gastHours: SiderealTime(...) (apparent, includes the equation of the equinoxes) and the bodies come from Rotation_EQJ_ECT (true ecliptic of date). Nutation in obliquity is +8.3" in 2025-2026 (Swiss ECL_NUT deps = 8.26" at 2026-09-20). Measured against swe.houses_ex on 28 cases (4 places x 7 instants in 2025-2026): Ascendant differences up to -18.51" (London 2026-09-20T20Z), 13.15" (Longyearbyen), 10.52" (New York), -11.25" (London 2025-07-01T00Z); MC differences +-1.9". Analytic sensitivity for an 8.26" obliquity error: max 1.8" at the equator, 10.4" at 40 deg, 18.4" at 51.5 deg, 39.9" at 60 deg. The receipt records this as the convention 'angles: gast-and-mean-obliquity', so it is documented but frame-inconsistent with its own body positions.
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js (function computeChart, lines 'obliquity: meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(input.utc))' and 'gastHours: SiderealTime(MakeTime(input.utc))'); chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:9-13 (meanObliquity); receipt.js:143; artifacts asc-check.json, asc-engine.json, asc-obliquity-sensitivity.txt. The committed swiss-node-polar pack's 1.57" worst angle (docs/engine-validation/README.md) is six instants and does not bound this.
- impact: The Ascendant is the most time-sensitive chart element (1 minute of clock ~ 15' of arc); a 10-20" frame error is small astrologically but is an outright inconsistency Swiss does not have, and it invalidates any 'angles agree to 1.6"' claim outside the six frozen cases.
- fix: Use the true obliquity of date (astronomy-engine e_tilt(time).tobl, or the new nutation module) for the angles, compute T from TT not UTC, change the receipt convention string to 'gast-and-true-obliquity', regenerate the frozen angle fixtures and re-run the polar pack; one-line engine change plus fixtures.
- verifier: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:124-129 verified: angleInput = { gastHours: SiderealTime(MakeTime(input.utc)) [apparent GAST], obliquity: meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(input.utc)) }, while bodies use Rotation_EQJ_ECT (true ecliptic of date, line 41) and EclipticGeoMoon (true obliquity); receipt.js:143 'angles: gast-and-mean-obliquity'. My 54 cases (9 UT instants in 2025-2026 x 6 places, adding Helsinki 60.17N and Sydney 33.9S to the auditor's four): natalChart().angles.asc equals computeAngles with the mean obliquity to 1e-9 deg in every case; shipped ASC minus swe.houses_ex(jd_ut, lat, lon, b'W', 0)[1][0]: London max 18.51" (2026-09-20T20Z, identical to the auditor), New York 10.52", Longyearbyen 14.68", Nairobi 2.04", Sydney 6.83", Helsinki 36.05" (2026-03-15T03Z, deps 9.03"); MC max 2.01". Recomputing the same computeAngles with A.e_tilt(time).tobl instead of the mean obliquity collapses every ASC residual to <= 0.18" and every MC to <= 0.11" over all 54 cases, so the obliquity input is the entire residual. The Helsinki 36" measurement matches the auditor's analytic 39.9" at 60 deg; the '18.5"' in the title is the London figure and the claim already states the 60-degree sensitivity. README table row 'Angles ... 1.57" worst angle' is a six-case measurement the README hedges as 'that run's', so major (frame inconsistency below Swiss) rather than blocking is right.

## production-positions-4 — True node carries a -7.25" mean bias (p50 7.0", max 19.9" vs Swiss TRUE_NODE) caused by the analytic Moon's velocity, not by a definition difference; committed 6.07" maximum understates it
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 2 d
- corrected claim: Engine North Node minus Swiss TRUE_NODE (TT pinned, 2025) carries a -7.3" mean bias (p50 7.0", max 16.7" on every-3rd-day sampling; 19.9" on the auditor's daily grid), and it is not a definition difference: rebuilding the node from Swiss's own of-date geometric Moon state reproduces TRUE_NODE to p50 0.95", max 2.92", mean -0.07". The cause is the analytic (Improved Lunar Ephemeris) Moon STATE error in both components, not the velocity alone: in a common J2000 frame, node(AE r, AE v) - node(Swiss r, Swiss v) has mean -7.27"; node(AE r, Swiss v) has mean -3.63" and node(Swiss r, AE v) has mean -3.64" (AE-Swiss velocity difference p50 0.03 m/s). Since GeoMoonState differences the same series, both halves come from the series and the fix (DE-based Moon state) is unchanged. The committed 6.07" six-case maximum (README:64) understates it.
- claim: Daily 2025 with TT pinned, engine North Node minus Swiss TRUE_NODE: mean -7.25", p50 7.03", p95 12.65", max 19.90" (production UT path identical to 0.02"). The engine's construction (ascending node of the geocentric angular-momentum plane r x v of GeoMoonState, rotated to the true ecliptic of date) IS Swiss's object: rebuilding that node from Swiss's own geometric Moon state in the equator/ecliptic of date reproduces Swiss TRUE_NODE to p50 1.01", max 2.96", mean -0.07" (the residual is Swiss's own three-position finite-difference method, NODE_CALC_INTV). Doing the same test in the J2000 frame gives 12"/34" because Swiss's J2000-flag node is the of-date node precessed as a point, a confound to avoid. Node speed differs from Swiss by p50 1.13"/day, max 9.29"/day, of which up to 8.9"/day is the +-6 h central difference on a fast-wobbling quantity. Node latitude reported as 0 is correct by definition (Swiss TRUE_NODE lat max 0 over 2025). Nutation correction changes nothing (max 20.03" after), so the bias is the Improved Lunar Ephemeris out-of-plane velocity error.
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js trueNodeLongitude (angular momentum from GeoMoonState; astronomy.js:2884-2897 GeoMoonState central difference dt=1e-5 d); compare.json key T7_node_daily2025; node-definition-ofdate.json; node-definition.json (J2000 confound); docs/engine-validation/README.md quotes 0.001685457 deg (6.07") as the measured maximum from six cases.
- impact: The node is the worst-behaved of the twelve shipped points (a 20" error is three times the Moon's), and it is the first place a Swiss user will notice a difference (Swiss prints the node to the arcsecond).
- fix: Compute the node from a DE-based Moon state (position and analytic velocity from the Chebyshev derivative) in the true ecliptic of date; the exact r x v with an analytic velocity will beat Swiss's own three-position method (1-3" internal noise measured). Derive node speed analytically (d/dt of atan2 of the rotated h) instead of +-6 h differencing. Add MEAN_NODE (Swiss has it; the engine does not) from the standard mean-element polynomial.
- verifier: My run: engine minus swe.calc(jd_tt, TRUE_NODE, SWIEPH|SPEED) mean -7.267", p50 7.035", max 16.712" (122 instants); node rebuilt from Swiss geometric Moon state (FLG_EQUATORIAL|XYZ|TRUEPOS|NOABERR|NOGDEFL|SPEED, true equator of date) on the true ecliptic of date vs TRUE_NODE: p50 0.952", max 2.923", mean -0.069" (auditor 1.01/2.96/-0.07) -- definition confirmed identical. Attribution by component swapping between A.GeoMoonState (EQJ) and Swiss FLG_J2000 geometric state, node taken on the J2000 ecliptic: AE-state node minus Swiss-state node mean -7.269" (p50 7.09, max 16.87, i.e. the full bias survives the frame change); AE position + Swiss velocity: mean -3.63" (p50 3.38, max 11.15); Swiss position + AE velocity: mean -3.64" (p50 2.70, max 16.25); the two halves sum to the total (cross term negligible). So 'provably a Moon-velocity error' is half the story; position error of the same series contributes equally. Code path (chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:50-63, astronomy.js:2884-2897 dt=1e-5 d central difference) confirmed as described. Severity stays major: the node is the largest residual of the twelve points and Swiss's own method is ~1-3" internally.

## production-positions-5 — Moon route (EclipticGeoMoon) applies no light-time/aberration (net -0.70", constant) on top of a 1.3"-4.4" analytic-series error that reaches 7.5" at 1800 and 20" at 2400
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 3 d
- claim: Swiss apparent minus Swiss TRUEPOS for the Moon is -0.703" mean, p50 0.701", max 0.761" over 2025 (the individually large legs are light-time with barycentric velocity, up to 21.6", and aberration, up to 20.9", which nearly cancel). The engine's Moon has neither: engine minus Swiss TRUEPOS (geometric) is -1.24" mean, p50 1.25", max 4.38"; engine minus Swiss apparent is -0.54" mean, p50 0.68", max 3.63" (2025, TT pinned) - so today the series bias and the missing correction partially cancel. Geometric vs DE440s: p50 1.31", max 4.43"; latitude max 1.14". Far epochs (TT pinned, lon): 1800 +7.55", 1850 +6.73", 1900 +2.92", 2100 +0.86" (Horizons: +0.68"), 2200 +3.28", 2400 +20.31". Horizons 2025-07-01 confirms -0.72" lon / -0.62" lat.

- fix: DE440s Moon (EMB->Moon segment) reduced with the alpha's retarded-position and aberration modules (already validated against ERFA in examples/precision-alpha), with true-of-date rotation via the new nutation; keep EclipticGeoMoon only as the flagged fallback.
- verifier: astronomy.js:2840-2866 EclipticGeoMoon read: CalcMoon -> spherical-to-vector -> e_tilt -> obl_ecl2equ_vec(mobl) -> nutation -> rotate by tobl; no GeoVector, light-time or aberration call; chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:46-49 moonOfDate uses it directly. Engine minus Swiss TRUEPOS mean -1.243", p50 1.19, max 3.73; engine minus apparent mean -0.54", p50 0.70, max 2.99 (auditor 3.63 on the finer grid); GeoMoon geometric vs DE440s p50 1.29"/max 3.76"; lat max 1.14". Far epochs TT pinned, engine minus Swiss lon: 1800-02-01 +7.55", 1850 +6.73", 1900 +2.92", 2000 +0.35", 2100 +0.86", 2150 +1.31", 2200 +3.28", 2399-12-01 +20.31" -- every value matches the auditor to 0.01". Cached Horizons (DE441, TT) re-parsed: Moon 2025-07-01 engine -0.719" lon / -0.615" lat, Swiss +0.046"; Moon 2100-01-01 engine +0.678", Swiss -0.18".

## swiss-parity-1 — Production Delta-T is 6.7 s wrong at the present epoch, displacing the Moon 3.5 arcsec today
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 3 d
- claim: @zodiacs/engine uses astronomy-engine's DeltaT_EspenakMeeus polynomial, which returns 75.50 s at 2026-09-22 while the observed value is bounded to [68.28, 70.08] s (TT-UTC = 69.184 s since 2017 and |UT1-UTC| < 0.9 s). Swiss gives 68.82 s and Horizons's own clock agrees with Swiss to 0.36 s. The error grows: +2.75 s at 2020, +6.68 s at 2026, +11.95 s at 2038, +27.9 s at 2055. At 0.549 arcsec/s the Moon is displaced 3.5 arcsec in production charts cast today and 7.4 arcsec at 2038; this is not a far-future extrapolation dispute but an error where the answer is known.
- evidence: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js MakeTime(date) -> astronomy-engine esm/astronomy.js:1131 (DeltaT = DeltaT_EspenakMeeus). Yearly table Swiss vs astronomy-engine 1990-2030 and the three-model comparison (Swiss, Horizons UT request, astronomy-engine) in <audit>/swiss-parity/deltat-three-models.json; row D4: dT_horizons-dT_swiss = +0.36 s, dT_ae-dT_swiss = +6.68 s, engine Moon minus Horizons Moon(UT) = 3.505 arcsec while the clock-pinned engine is 0.266 arcsec from the TT Horizons Moon.
- impact: Keeps the production Moon materially below Swiss at exactly the epochs users chart most; also poisons every Moon-driven event (void-of-course, lunar returns, phases) by ~6 s of time. It is the single largest correctable error in current-epoch production positions.
- fix: Replace the polynomial with an IERS-derived ΔT table (historical Bulletin B / finals.all through the present, monthly points) plus a declared long-term extrapolation (e.g. Morrison-Stephenson-Hohenkerk 2021) and record the model name in the receipt conventions; add a test pinning ΔT within 0.5 s of the IERS table for 1962-present.
- verifier: Own run (deltat_ae.mjs + deltat_cmp.py): astronomy-engine 2.1.19 MakeTime gives TT-UT = 75.50 s at 2026-09-22T17:00Z, Swiss deltat_ex(FLG_SWIEPH) 68.82 s, difference +6.68 s; +2.75 s at 2020-12-21, +11.95 s at 2038-01-19, +27.86 s at 2055-06-06, all matching the claim. Independent ground truth fetched from IERS finals2000A.daily (USNO mirror; datacenter.iers.org reset the connection): UT1-UTC = -0.0114 s at MJD 61305 (2026-09-22, flag I = observed), so the true Delta-T is 32.184+37+0.0114 = 69.195 s: astronomy-engine is +6.30 s wrong, Swiss -0.37 s, and the Horizons clock implied from the cached Moon_UT vs Moon requests (horizons_dt.py: +0.364 s vs Swiss at D4) is 69.18 s, within 0.01 s of IERS. astronomy-engine already sits outside the observed band [68.28,70.08] from about 2018 (71.85 s at 2020-07-01). Moon rate at the instant is 12.31 deg/day = 0.513 arcsec/s, so 6.3 s is 3.2 arcsec; the shipped engine's c_raw Moon at D4 is +3.64 arcsec from Swiss (auditor: 3.505 vs Horizons UT), consistent. Code path verified: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js imports MakeTime from astronomy-engine (line 20); astronomy-engine esm/astronomy.js:1131 `let DeltaT = DeltaT_EspenakMeeus`. Note for the fix: astronomy-engine exports SetDeltaTFunction (astronomy.js:1132), so an IERS-table Delta-T can be injected without forking. The receipt CONVENTIONS declare no Delta-T model and the public methodology page attributes Delta-T disagreement only to far-future extrapolation (2100), so the present-day error is undocumented. Major is earned: a current-epoch accuracy gap that keeps the production Moon below Swiss and below Swiss's Moshier fallback; not blocking because no documented guarantee is falsified.

## swiss-parity-2 — Shipped engine positions are 4-10x further from JPL than Swiss's own zero-data Moshier fallback
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 12 d
- claim: With the clock pinned to Swiss's ΔT, @zodiacs/engine 0.1.1-rc.6 longitudes are max 18.80 arcsec / p50 2.07 arcsec / p95 12.0 arcsec from Horizons over 240 rows (per body max: Neptune 18.8, Pluto 14.6, Venus 12.6, Uranus 12.0, Saturn 10.2, Mercury 8.0, Jupiter 7.7, Mars 4.5, Moon 4.2, Sun 1.4) and latitudes are off by up to 19.0 arcsec (Saturn, p50 8.2 arcsec; Uranus 16.5; Neptune 9.7). Swiss run with NO ephemeris files at all (built-in Moshier series) is max 2.82 arcsec (Pluto), Moon 1.25, Neptune 2.05, Sun 0.066 against DE440 on the same rows. The fair like-for-like (no data download on either side) is lost by a factor of 4-10; the alpha with its 1.66 MiB pack is at 0.0025 arcsec of Swiss.
- evidence: <audit>/swiss-parity/results.json (perBody['c_pinned-e'], resLat) and swiss-moshier-vs-de440.json; compare.out. Source of the series: astronomy-engine 2.1.19 truncated VSOP87 planets, ILE Moon, 5-term nutation, imported at chunk-GBH7JIYF.js lines 15-25.
- impact: Production is below Swiss in every configuration Swiss offers, including Swiss with no data. The alpha already closes this to sub-mas; the gap is an adoption gap, not a research gap.
- fix: Adopt the alpha's Reducer + ZODEPH02 pack as the default positional backend in @zodiacs/engine (the 1.66 MiB pack is small enough to ship or lazy-load; proven position error ≤1.3 km, compression ≤0.001 arcsec), keep astronomy-engine only as the no-pack fallback and label it in the receipt; follow examples/precision-alpha/CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md.
- verifier: Independent rerun with Swiss as the instrument rather than Horizons (swiss_corpus.py, engine_corpus.mjs, compare_corpus.py over the auditor's 24 preregistered instants; every SWIEPH call returned retflag 258 and every Moshier call 260, checked): @zodiacs/engine 0.1.1-rc.6 with its clock pinned to Swiss Delta-T is max 18.85 arcsec / p50 2.07 arcsec in longitude from Swiss .se1 (per body max: Neptune 18.85, Pluto 14.31, Venus 12.64, Uranus 12.07, Saturn 10.27, Mercury 7.97, Jupiter 7.74, Mars 4.41, Moon 3.88, Sun 1.35) and max 19.03 arcsec in latitude (Saturn, p50 8.19; Uranus 16.42; Neptune 9.71). Swiss with no data files (set_ephe_path to a nonexistent dir, FLG_MOSEPH, retflag 260) is max 2.82 arcsec (Pluto), Neptune 2.05, Moon 1.25, Sun 0.066 from the .se1 answer. All numbers within the claim. The ratio of overall maxima is 6.7x (inside the stated 4-10x); per body it runs from 3x (Moon) to over 100x (Mercury), so the headline understates for inner bodies. Cause check: the Saturn latitude residual is +4.4 arcsec at C1 and +17.6 at C4 with Saturn at nearly the same longitude (249.9 vs 249.7 deg), so it is not an ecliptic-frame tilt; consistent with truncated VSOP87 latitude series as the auditor states. Severity: major, not blocking; src/pages/methodology/index.astro:88-90 and developers/engine/index.astro:162-163 already publish 'worst 18.6 arcsec vs Swiss', so no documented claim is falsified, but the gap versus every Swiss configuration including zero-data Moshier is real.

- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 8 d

Swiss provides all of these (SE_CHIRON, SE_MEAN_NODE, SE_MEAN_APOG, SE_OSCU_APOG, SE_INTP_APOG, SE_CERES.., swe_fixstar2 with ~1000 stars and proper motion). Chiron, Lilith and the mean node appear in most professional charts.

Chiron and Ceres/Pallas/Juno/Vesta need a small-body ephemeris: extend the pack compiler to ingest JPL SBDB/Horizons-generated SPK for 2060 Chiron, 1 Ceres, 2 Pallas, 3 Juno, 4 Vesta (Chiron's ephemeris is poorly determined before 1900; state coverage). True/osculating apogee from the Moon state vector.
- verifier: src/lib/engine/types.ts lines 5-8: BodyName is exactly Sun..Pluto plus 'North Node' | 'South Node'; chunk-GBH7JIYF.js PLANETS array and node handling (lines 27-111) cover the same set with a true node only; examples/precision-alpha/src/core/reduce.mjs CONTRACT.bodies is the ten Sun..Pluto. grep -i 'chiron|lilith|apogee|mean node' over src/lib and src/mcp non-test .ts returns only src/lib/analytics.ts:81, an analytics event name 'chiron_toggle' with no computation behind it; the public pages disclose the gap (src/pages/methodology/index.astro:240 'Chiron and the asteroids aren't included yet', src/pages/developers/engine/index.astro:199 'No Chiron, no asteroids'), and src/data/glossary.ts:710 says the same. Swiss side tested on local pyswisseph 2.10.03: MEAN_NODE, TRUE_NODE, MEAN_APOG, OSCU_APOG, INTP_APOG, INTP_PERG all compute with retflag 258 (SWIEPH) from the main .se1 files alone; CHIRON, PHOLUS, CERES, PALLAS, JUNO, VESTA raise 'seas_18.se1 not found' (the library refuses rather than silently falling back - capability present, asteroid file not installed here), and fixstar2_ut needs sefstars.txt, also absent locally. So the Swiss breadth is a library capability requiring its standard data files, which the finding's fix estimate already implies. Major is earned as a documented but material breadth gap.

## time-1 — Production Delta-T (Espenak-Meeus 2004 polynomials) is ~6 s wrong today and the docs call the gap a 'convention'
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 4 d
- claim: astronomy-engine converts UT to TT with DeltaT_EspenakMeeus, a fixed 2004 polynomial set whose 2005-2050 leg assumes a +0.32 s/yr trend that did not happen. At 2026-01-01 it yields TT-UT = 75.05 s; Swiss swe_deltat gives 68.90 s and JPL Horizons' UT-vs-TT tagged Moon vectors imply 69.18 s (Horizons treats UT as UTC, so the arbiter is good to +-0.9 s). The engine is therefore +5.9 s off now, +0.6 s/yr worse each year (2020: +2.4 s; 2030: +8.4 s; 2050: +23.8 s vs Horizons, +18.4 s vs Swiss), and -6.1 s at 1700, +29.9 s at 1600. Moon longitude error = 0.549 arcsec per second: 3.2-3.4 arcsec today, 4.6 arcsec by 2030, 10 arcsec by 2050, 16 arcsec at 1600, 60 arcsec at 2100. The SDK never reads, exposes, pins or records Delta-T (no export in dist/*.d.ts; receipt CONVENTIONS has no time-scale entry), and the alpha never sees it (caller supplies TT). RESULTS.md and the public methodology page describe the 109.5 s gap at 2100 as 'a time-scale convention difference, not an algorithmic defect' and 'Neither extrapolation is wrong; the number isn't known yet' — for the observed era the number IS known and the shipped model is wrong; the 2100 divergence is the same stale polynomial, not a convention.
- evidence: Model: node_modules/astronomy-engine/astronomy.js:1023-1135 (DeltaT_EspenakMeeus), :1141 (`let DeltaT = DeltaT_EspenakMeeus`), :1157-1159 (TerrestrialTime), :1198-1209 (AstroTime sets tt from ut). Package entry: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:39-51 (MakeTime(date) with a UTC Date). Claims: docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/RESULTS.md:74-82; docs/engine-validation/README.md:78-86; src/pages/methodology/index.astro:95-104. Table (AE vs swe.deltat, 1600-2200, Jan 1): 1600 120.27 vs 90.37 (+29.90 s, Moon +16.42 arcsec); 1700 8.99 vs 15.09 (-6.11 s); 1900 -2.76 vs -1.95; 2000 63.85 vs 63.83; 2010 66.68 vs 66.07 (+0.61); 2020 71.58 vs 69.36 (+2.22); 2024 73.85 vs 69.10 (+4.75); 2026 75.05 vs 68.90 (+6.15 s, Moon +3.38 arcsec); 2030 77.59 vs 69.28 (+8.31); 2050 92.97 vs 74.58 (+18.39 s, +10.09 arcsec); 2100 202.65 vs 93.18 (+109.47 s, +60.1 arcsec). Horizons arbiter (Moon geocentric state vectors, TIME_TYPE UT vs TT, dT = dr.v/|v|^2): 2020 69.184, 2024-2027 69.184 (UTC-tagged); AE-Horizons 2024 +4.66 s, 2025 +5.26 s, 2026 +5.87 s, 2027 +6.48 s; Swiss-Horizons within -0.38..+0.18 s. Artifacts: <audit>/time/deltat-compare.txt, deltat-table.json, deltat-ae.json, horizons-deltat.txt, horizons-deltat.json, horizons/q2-*.query|txt, horizons/q3-*.query|txt (5 Horizons requests total incl. q1). Swiss model facts from pyswisseph 2.10.03: MOD_DELTAT_STEPHENSON_ETC_2016 = default (5), TIDAL_DE431 -25.8 / TIDAL_DE441 -25.936 / TIDAL_MOSEPH -25.58 applied by ephemeris, set_delta_t_userdef works (demo: 75.05 s pinned then restored).
- impact: Every Moon position the site ships today carries a systematic +3.3 arcsec time-scale error that Swiss does not have, and it grows 0.34 arcsec per year; it is larger than the whole p50 (1.88 arcsec) of the shipped-vs-Swiss benchmark and comparable to the p95. The alpha's sub-arcsecond reduction work cannot reach the user through a 6-second clock. The public 'neither is wrong' framing is unsupported and would not survive a sceptical reviewer.
- fix: (1) Replace the polynomial with a data-driven Delta-T: Stephenson/Morrison/Hohenkerk 2016 LOD spline before 1620, the observed annual TT-UT1 table 1620-1955 (Stephenson-Morrison / Astronomical Almanac), and IERS-derived TT-UT1 (32.184 + TAI-UTC - (UT1-UTC) from finals.all) 1955-present, refreshed by the existing weekly cron; extrapolate beyond the last observation with the last observed slope plus the tidal parabola, and adjust for the ephemeris' tidal acceleration (DE440: -25.936 arcsec/cy^2 convention as Swiss does). (2) Attach an uncertainty band (IERS formal error in the observed era, published Stephenson/Morrison sigma before 1955, growing with extrapolation distance) and carry it through to a per-body longitude band in the result and receipt (Moon 0.549 arcsec/s, Sun 0.041 arcsec/s). (3) Expose deltaT (value, model name, table date, band) in natalChart output and CONVENTIONS/receipt; add a caller pin analogous to swe_set_delta_t_userdef. (4) Install it via astronomy-engine's SetDeltaTFunction at engine load (server and browser) or, better, compute TT explicitly and construct AstroTime via FromTerrestrialTime. (5) Correct RESULTS.md, engine-validation README and the methodology page: the far-future gap is model staleness, and the present-day error is measurable.
- verifier: Own runs (<audit>/verify/time/deltat-ae.out, deltat-swe.out, deltat-summary.txt, hz.cjs): astronomy-engine 2.1.19 MakeTime(2026-01-01Z).tt-ut = 75.051 s (2024 73.848, 2030 77.590, 2050 92.968, 2100 202.652, 1600 120.269; slope 2026->2027 +0.618 s/yr). pyswisseph 2.10.03 with <swisslab>/ephe, SWIEPH confirmed active (calc_ut retflag 258, tidacc -25.936): swe_deltat 2026 = 68.900 s (2024 69.100, 2050 74.581, 2100 93.182, 1600 90.366) — every table entry matches the auditor. Horizons dT recomputed independently from the auditor's raw UT- and TT-tagged Moon state vectors (dr.v/|v|^2) = 69.184 s for every year 2020-2027, i.e. TT-UTC = 32.184+37, so Horizons 'UT' is UTC as the auditor says. I closed the +-0.9 s arbiter gap with one IERS request (finals.all.iau2000.txt, cached under verify/time): UT1-UTC = +0.0088 s (2024-01-01), +0.0463 (2025), +0.0741 (2026), so true TT-UT1 = 69.175 / 69.138 / 69.110 s. Errors: astronomy-engine +4.67 / +5.31 / +5.94 s; Swiss -0.08 / -0.14 / -0.21 s. Moon at 0.549 arcsec/s: +3.26 arcsec today for the shipped engine. Real Delta-T is flat-to-falling (-0.03 s/yr 2024-2026) while the 2005-2050 Espenak-Meeus leg (astronomy.js:1103-1106, 62.92+0.32217u+0.005589u^2) rises 0.6 s/yr, so the gap grows ~0.65 s/yr as claimed. Cited code verified: astronomy.js:1023 DeltaT_EspenakMeeus, :1141 `let DeltaT = DeltaT_EspenakMeeus`, :1157-1159 TerrestrialTime, :1198-1209 AstroTime tt from ut; chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:39-51 MakeTime(date). No 'deltat/DeltaT' symbol in any dist/*.d.ts or dist/*.js of @zodiacs/engine; no SetDeltaTFunction call anywhere in src/, scripts/, api/ or examples/ (only the bundled copy in examples/mcp-server/server.mjs:1241); receipt CONVENTIONS (receipt.js:137-147) has no time-scale entry. Doc quotes verified verbatim: RESULTS.md:79-82 'time-scale convention difference, not an algorithmic defect ... neither model is wrong'; engine-validation README:79-86 'a clock difference'; methodology/index.astro:99-104 'Neither extrapolation is wrong; the number isn't known yet'. All three speak only about the 2100/2190 cases; the auditor's point stands that the 2100 value (202.65 s) is the 2150-2050 bridge formula anchored on the already-wrong 2050 leg, so the same stale polynomial is the cause, and nothing in the docs discloses the measurable present-day error. Corroboration inside the tree: the shipped benchmark's own Moon residual for modern-06 (2026-09-20, corpus.mjs:35) is +3.41 arcsec (report-measure.json), consistent with the ~6.7 s AE-Swiss Delta-T gap at that date (3.7 arcsec expected). Severity major is earned: a systematic accuracy gap vs Swiss larger than the benchmark p50, but 3 arcsec stays inside the site's public 'few hundredths of a degree' promise and the 2100 sentence is about a genuinely unknown quantity, so not blocking.

## time-2 — Pre-1970 offsets for 98 zones are another city's history on ICU hosts (tzdb merger policy, no backzone)
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 5 d
- corrected claim: resolveLocalToUtc (SDK geo.js and the site copy) returns the host ICU's tzdb-default (post-1970 merged, no backzone) history. Reproduced: Europe/Stockholm 1947-07-01 12:00 -> 10:00Z, offset +120, flags [] (and 1947-06-15 -> +180; 1941-01-15 -> +120) in both copies, where the host's backzone tzdata gives +60 (Sweden had no DST 1917-1979); ICU's Stockholm offset equals Berlin's on all 29,219 days 1900-1979. Independent all-zone re-measure (verify/time/zone-div.mjs, 1850-2037): 98 of the 400 Intl zones that have TZif files (418 Intl zones total) differ somewhere; 87 have a >=15-min difference after 1900; 65 have a >=60-min difference lasting >=30 days after 1900 (60 when the pre-first-transition LMT era is excluded — the auditor's 60 came from a top-3 sample and is a lower bound); 7,269 zone-years total, but only 1,975 zone-years are >=15 min after 1900, and the >=60-min post-1900 exposure per zone is far smaller than the headline zone-years: Stockholm 7 yr (1917-18, 1940-49 summers/wartime), Oslo 7.1, Copenhagen 2.7, Luxembourg 10.6, Amsterdam 0.4 yr (its typical error is 20-40 min for 1892-1940, 80 min only on single transition days), Aruba 3.4 yr at 90 min plus 30 min for 1912-1965, Antigua 38.8 yr, Reykjavik 45 yr (winters 1908-1968, -60 min; the auditor's example date 1950-06-15 is wrong — Iceland was on +0:00 summer time then and ICU agrees). Chart impact reproduced through natalChart: Stockholm 1947-07-01 12:00 with +120 vs +60 moves the ASC by 9.47 deg and the MC by 13.82 deg; Moon 29.8 arcmin (auditor said 32.9 arcmin/h). The homepage FAQ (index.astro:25) and STRATEGY.md:156 do promise pre-1970 history from Intl; the statement is literally true (it IS the ICU history) so it is misleading rather than false. Receipt slot runtime.tzdbVersion (receipt.js:454-457) exists and the site passes provenance null (portable.test.ts:49). Host: Ubuntu 24.04 tzdata 2025b (not Debian), Node 22.22.2 ICU 78.2 tz 2025c. Browser ICUs remain untested.
- claim: resolveLocalToUtc (site and SDK) reads offsets from Intl, i.e. the host ICU's tzdb, which is built with the tzdb default that links many zones to a neighbour with identical post-1970 data and drops their real pre-1970 history (backzone). Measured on this machine against its own backzone-built TZif files (Debian tzdata 2025b; Node 22 ICU 78.2 with tz 2025c): over 1850-2036, 98 of 418 zones disagree somewhere, 87 have a >=15-minute disagreement after 1900, 60 have a >=60-minute disagreement lasting >=30 days after 1900, totalling about 7,270 zone-years. European examples: Europe/Stockholm (57 zone-years, up to 120 min: 1947-07-01 12:00 resolves with +120 (Berlin CEST) where Sweden had +60; 1947-06-15 resolves with +180, Berlin's double summer time), Europe/Amsterdam (90.8 zone-years, up to 80 min: 1938-06-15 12:00 gets +60 where the legal Dutch offset was +20/+80), Atlantic/Reykjavik (99 zone-years; 1950-06-15 gets 0 where Iceland was -60/0), Europe/Oslo (52 zone-years; 1960 summer gets +60 where Norway had +120), Europe/Copenhagen (46.7), Europe/Luxembourg (65), Europe/Monaco (43.8), Guernsey/Jersey (WWII double summer time). Elsewhere: America/Aruba and Curacao -4:00 vs -4:30 (1912-1942) and -4:00 vs -5:30 (1942-45), America/Antigua -4 vs -5 (1912-1951), Africa/Niamey up to 120 min, Pacific/Majuro up to 180 min, Asia/Bahrain -30/-38 min (1920-1943). The homepage FAQ (src/pages/index.astro:25) promises 'Historical clock changes, including pre-1922 local mean time where available, use the IANA/ICU history', and docs/STRATEGY.md:156 promises 'pre-1970 shifts'; neither the 'lmt' flag, the receipt nor any warning marks these zones, and no tzdb version is recorded (receipt slot runtime.tzdbVersion exists but the site passes provenance null). Browser ICU builds were not tested here but ship the same tzdb default form (unverified on this machine).
- evidence: Code: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/geo.js:145-282 (timezone.ts: offsetFormatter 153-165, offsetAt 189-199, resolveLocalToUtc 237-282); src/lib/time/localToUtc.ts:20-69, 121-183; receipt slot node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/receipt.js:454-457 never populated (src/lib/engine/portable.test.ts:49 `provenance: null`). Measurement: audit/time/zone-history-divergence-to-2037.mjs compares tf(ms) (TZif v2 64-bit transitions, /usr/share/zoneinfo/<zone>) with Intl longOffset at daily steps 1850-2037, bisecting change instants to the minute; output audit/time/zone-history-divergence-to-2037.json and .log ('zones differing anywhere: 98 of 418; >=15 min lasting >=1 year after 1900: 73; >=60 min after 1900: 60'). The 1850-2100 run (zone-history-divergence.json, 203 zones) over-counts because my TZif parser ignores the POSIX footer after 2037 — use the to-2037 file. Highlights and chart impact: audit/time/zone-divergence-highlights.txt ('Stockholm 1947-07-01: 60-min offset error -> dASC 9.47 deg, dMC 13.82 deg; Moon 32.9 arcmin/h'; 'ICU Europe/Stockholm 1947-07-01 12:00 -> 1947-07-01T10:00:00Z offset 120'; 'ICU Europe/Amsterdam 1938-06-15 12:00 -> offset 60'; 'ICU Atlantic/Reykjavik 1950-06-15 12:00 -> offset 0'). Cross-check of the skew list by zone: audit/time/tz-scan-report.json `dataSkew` (491 TZif transitions with no ICU offset change, 136 zones). Compute: 138 s (1850-2100) + ~120 s (1850-2037).
- impact: For births before 1970 in the affected countries (Sweden, Norway, Denmark, Netherlands, Iceland, Luxembourg, Monaco, Channel Islands, much of West/East Africa, the Dutch and British Caribbean, Bahrain, parts of Indochina and the Pacific) the UTC instant is wrong by 20-180 minutes: rising signs, houses and the Moon's sign are frequently wrong outright. Swiss Ephemeris itself ships no timezone data, but every Swiss-based product uses an atlas with real history; a stack that aims to exceed Swiss cannot be worse than the tzdb default here, and its published promise about pre-1970 history is currently unsound.
- fix: Stop depending on the host for history: compile tzdata with backzone (PACKRATDATA=backzone PACKRATLIST=zone.tab) into a versioned, compact per-zone transition table shipped with the engine (whole tzdb with backzone is ~400 KB as raw transitions; lazy-load per zone shard like the GeoNames shards), and make resolveLocalToUtc consult it, using Intl only as a cross-check and as fallback for zones the table lacks. Record tzdb version, data form ('backzone') and the transition actually applied (offset, abbreviation, isdst, LMT/legal kind) in LocalTimeResolution and the receipt. Add a 'zone-history-uncertain' flag where tzdb's own comments mark pre-1970 data as approximate. Keep the existing 1.68 M-case round-trip scan as a CI test against the new table. Longer term, allow an atlas override (ACS-style) for the birth-time profession.
- verifier: verify/time/stockholm.out (SDK and site copies via the auditor's esbuild bundle), python3.11 zoneinfo on the host tzdata (Stockholm 1947 +1:00, Amsterdam 1938-06-15 +1:20, Reykjavik 1950-06-15 +0:00, Oslo 1960-06-15 +2:00, Aruba 1930 -4:30, Antigua 1930 -5:00), zdump showing no Stockholm transitions 1946-1949 while Berlin has CEST/CEMT, Intl Stockholm==Berlin day-by-day 1900-1979 (0 differing days), verify/time/zone-div.json + zone-div.out (independent script storing every divergence segment per zone, with the stats above), verify/time/stk-impact.out (natalChart ASC/MC/Moon deltas). The auditor's own JSON only kept the top-3 sample segments, which is why its >=60-min count (60) undercounts and why the headline zone-years cannot be read as hours-level exposure.

## time-3 — No Julian-calendar input path: Julian-era birth dates are silently read as proleptic Gregorian
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 2 d
- corrected claim: The shipped input path (SDK resolveLocalToUtc/wallMilliseconds via Date and Intl calendar 'gregory', site civil-date.ts Gregorian-only leap rule, receipt CONVENTIONS.calendar 'proleptic-gregorian', share codec 1800-2199, MCP EPOCH 1800-2199) is proleptic Gregorian only and offers no calendar field or Old-Style warning. Reproduced exactly: Sun 83.564 vs 93.093 deg (1500-06-15 J/G, 9.53 deg), 340.978 vs 351.954 (1700, 10.98 deg), 211.507 vs 224.514 (1917, 13.01 deg) with astronomy-engine, and 13.01 deg again through natalChart for Petrograd 1917-10-25 as typed vs 1917-11-07 (Moon 324.9 vs 141.9 deg); Swiss julday JUL-GREG = 10.0 d and revjul/date_conversion behave as stated. Two corrections: (1) the repo is not without a Julian path — docs/phase5/people-pilot/tools/compute-astro.mjs:140-160 has a JDN-based julianToGregorian, the manifest carries calendarModel/calendarConversion, and the pilot's screening.json records 'Option A — Julian-dated births are excluded from the Phase 5A pilot' with the Option B treatment specified in build-proofs.mjs, so the people pilot is NOT affected as the impact statement says; the gap is that this converter is internal tooling, not wired into the SDK, site form, MCP or share codec. (2) Every UI date input (BirthFields.tsx:60 and all islands) is min 1800-01-01 / max 2199-12-31, so the 1500 and 1700 examples are reachable only by calling the SDK directly (natalChart accepts 1500; verified); the user-facing exposure is Old-Style records from 1800 onward (Russia to 1918-02-14, Greece to 1923, Bulgaria 1916, Serbia/Romania 1919, Turkey 1926 etc.).
- claim: parseCivilDate/wallMilliseconds accept only proleptic-Gregorian YYYY-MM-DD and every layer (Date, Intl with calendar 'gregory', astronomy-engine, receipt CONVENTIONS.calendar = 'proleptic-gregorian') treats the typed fields as Gregorian. A user with a Julian-calendar record (all of Europe before 1582-10-15; Britain and colonies to 1752-09-14; Russia to 1918-02-14; Greece to 1923; Bulgaria 1916; Serbia/Yugoslavia 1919; Romania 1919) gets a chart for a different day with no warning: 1500-06-15 typed gives Sun 83.564 deg where the Gregorian equivalent 1500-06-25 gives 93.093 deg (9.53 deg); 1700-03-01 Julian (England) typed gives 340.978 vs 351.954 (10.98 deg); 1917-10-25 Julian (Russia, the October Revolution date) gives 211.507 vs 224.514 (13.01 deg). The birth-share window (1800-2199) and the MCP epoch window (1800-01-01..2199-12-31) both admit the Julian-era Russian and Balkan dates. Swiss provides swe_julday/swe_revjul with SE_JUL_CAL/SE_GREG_CAL and swe_date_conversion('j'|'g'); the stack has no equivalent, no UI affordance, and no adoption-date warning.
- evidence: src/lib/time/civil-date.ts:17-29 (Gregorian-only leap rule), node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/geo.js:216-236 (wallMilliseconds, Gregorian via Date), :158-160 and :171-176 (Intl calendar 'gregory'), node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/receipt.js:137-147 (CONVENTIONS.calendar), src/lib/share.ts:113 (1800-2199 window), src/mcp/bounds.ts:26-27 (EPOCH 1800-2199). Measurements in audit/time/calendar-edges.txt: 'Sun lon 1500-06-15 (as typed) 83.5640; Sun lon 1500-06-25 93.0935'; 'England 1700-03-01 Julian ... [340.978, 351.954]'; 'Russia 1917-10-25 Julian ... [211.507, 224.514]'. Swiss: 'swe_julday 1500-06-15 JUL 2269099.0 GREG 2269089.0 difference days 10.0; revjul(jd_jul, GREG) = (1500, 6, 25, 12.0)'; 'date_conversion 1500-06-15 j -> (True, 2269099.0, (1500,6,15,12.0))'. No rg hit for 'julian calendar|old style' anywhere in src/ (only julianCenturies helpers).
- impact: Whole-sign-level errors (10-13 deg for the Sun, up to 180 deg for the Moon over the 10-13 day shift) for any Old-Style record entered as written; historical/famous-person charts (the people pilot) and users with Russian, Greek, Serbian or pre-1752 British ancestry are affected. Swiss has had this for decades; it is a hard capability gap.
- fix: Add `calendar: 'gregorian' | 'julian'` to the birth input (SDK resolveBirth, site form, MCP schema, share codec, receipt), convert Julian civil fields to a Julian Day Number (Meeus algorithm, or the alpha's own JD arithmetic) and then to the proleptic-Gregorian Date the rest of the stack expects; store both the typed fields and the converted ones in the receipt; add an adoption-date table keyed by country (from the GeoNames row) that prompts 'this date is before <country> adopted the Gregorian calendar — was the record Old Style?' when applicable; extend the people-pilot manifest's calendarModel to carry the source's calendar.
- verifier: verify/time/julian.out and julian-swe.out (astronomy-engine Sun/Moon for the three date pairs; pyswisseph julday/revjul/date_conversion), verify/time/stk-impact.out (natalChart Petrograd 1917 as typed vs converted, and natalChart accepting 1500-06-15), cited lines read: src/lib/time/civil-date.ts:17-29, geo.js:158-160/171-176 (calendar 'gregory') and :216-236 (wallMilliseconds via Date), receipt.js:137-147, share.ts:113, mcp/bounds.ts:26-27; grep for julian/old style/calendarModel across src, docs, scripts, examples found the pilot converter and the Option A rule (docs/phase5/people-pilot/screening.json:4,403; tools/compute-astro.mjs:140; tools/build-proofs.mjs:436-461) and nothing in src/ or the SDK. Severity stays major: silent whole-sign-level wrong answers for a definable class of users with no way to declare the calendar, a capability Swiss exposes; not blocking because no documented guarantee is contradicted and the affected inputs are a narrow, historical class.

## verification-honesty-1 — In-suite Horizons gate is 12x-3600x looser than measured residuals and the reference set is not reproducible
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 0.5 d
- corrected claim: engine.test.ts gates positions at 180/540/720 arcsec while measured residuals against its own literals are 0.019-14.77 arcsec, so the gate is 12x (Neptune) to about 9400x (Mercury) looser than the residual and would pass a ~3 arcminute regression in any planet. The header records the Horizons query parameters (QUANTITIES='31', CENTER='500@399', apparent true-of-date) but not the API version, source kernels or retrieval date, so the reference set cannot be re-derived byte-for-byte from the tree.
- claim: src/lib/engine/engine.test.ts gates positions at 0.05 deg (180 arcsec) for planets, 0.15 deg (540 arcsec) for the 2020 Moon and 0.2 deg (720 arcsec) for the 1907 Moon, while the measured residuals against the same literals are at most 14.78 arcsec (Neptune 2020) and as small as 0.02 arcsec (Mercury 2020); a regression of about 3 arcminutes in any planet would pass. The literals carry no provider version, source-kernel names or query strings, so the reference cannot be re-derived from the tree.
- evidence: engine.test.ts:200-208 (HORIZONS_2020 literals), :215-216 (tol 0.15/0.05), :224-231 (0.05/0.2). Measured residuals (node scratch/residuals.mjs, artifact residuals.log): 2020 Sun 0.050, Moon 0.507, Mercury 0.019, Venus 0.524, Mars 1.337, Jupiter 4.059, Saturn 1.933, Uranus 3.065, Neptune 14.778, Pluto 0.743; 1907 Sun 0.666, Moon 2.349, Mars 6.246 arcsec. Re-fetch 2026-09-22 (horizons/fetch.py, horizons/queries.log, horizons/rows.json): API VERSION 1.2, Sun/Moon/Earth source DE441, Neptune nep098_merged, Mars mar099, Jupiter jup365_merged; six of seven literals byte-identical, Neptune 2020 literal 346.2645218 vs 346.2645207 now (0.0000011 deg = 0.004 arcsec). docs/engine-validation/README.md concedes the unrecorded version but not the gate ratio.
- impact: The only position gate CI runs cannot detect an arcsecond- or even arcminute-level regression in astronomy-engine or the reduction; a claim of 'tested against JPL Horizons' (src/pages/methodology, hero copy in es/fr/it/pt/ru) rests on it. Exceeding Swiss requires a gate that would actually fail if the engine got worse than Swiss's roughly 1-2 arcsec agreement.
- fix: Commit src/lib/engine/fixtures/horizons-reference.json with the seven-plus rows re-fetched today (query string, API version, source kernels, retrieval date) and a per-body gate of max(3 x measured residual, 2 arcsec): Sun 2, Moon 2, Mercury 2, Venus 2, Mars 4, Jupiter 12, Saturn 6, Uranus 9, Neptune 45, Pluto 2.5 arcsec at 2020; Mars 19, Moon 7, Sun 2 at 1907. Margin justified because the engine is deterministic and the reference drifted 0.004 arcsec over 15 months, so 3x leaves room for a Horizons kernel update without masking a real change. Add stations (Mercury 2026-03-20 19:32 UT) and one far epoch (2150) from Horizons in the same file.
- verifier: Reproduced (verify/verification-honesty/residuals.log): engine vs the test's own literals at 2020-01-01 and 1907-07-06 gives max 14.774 arcsec (Neptune; 14.778 vs the re-fetched literal), Sun 0.050, Moon 0.507, Mercury 0.019, Venus 0.524, Mars 1.337, Jupiter 4.059, Saturn 1.933, Uranus 3.065, Pluto 0.743; 1907 Sun 0.666, Moon 2.349, Mars 6.246. Gates in engine.test.ts are 0.05/0.15/0.2 deg (lines 201-212 literals, 217-218 and 226-231 tolerances; the auditor's 200-208/215-216 are off by one or two lines). Gate/residual ratio range is 12.2x (Neptune) to 9391x (Mercury), not 12x-3600x (3578x is the Sun). The claim that the literals carry 'no query strings' is overstated: engine.test.ts:4-5 records QUANTITIES='31', CENTER='500@399', apparent true-of-date; what is missing is the API version, source kernels, retrieval date and full URL, exactly as docs/engine-validation/README.md:211-214 concedes. Horizons re-fetch artifacts (horizons/rows.json, API VERSION 1.2, Neptune 346.2645207 vs literal 346.2645218 = 0.004 arcsec) checked, not re-requested. Public copy is honest about the gate (methodology: 'tolerances of a few hundredths of a degree'; es hero 'Posiciones verificadas con NASA JPL Horizons'), so severity rests on the gate not protecting the arcsecond-level Swiss-agreement figures published on the same page, not on an overclaim.

## verification-honesty-2 — Shipped Delta-T model is 2-6 s off IERS today and nothing tests it; Swiss is within 0.4 s
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 2 d
- corrected claim: The shipped engine converts UTC to TT with astronomy-engine's default Espenak-Meeus polynomial (no SetDeltaTFunction override), which is +1.5 s (2017), +2.2 s (2020), +4.8 s (2024) and +6.3 s (2026-09-22) ahead of IERS TT-UT1 and diverging about 1.2 s/yr, while Swiss 2.10.03 is within 0.4 s; no test asserts any Delta-T value. This is a systematic 1.2-3.5 arcsec Moon-longitude component today, partly masked at present by an opposite-sign error in astronomy-engine's Moon geometry (e.g. 0.72 arcsec total vs Swiss at the 2024 eclipse instant). The 2024 eclipse TT residual of 4.18 s is ephemeris geometry, not Delta-T, and should not be cited as evidence for this finding.
- claim: The production engine's UTC-to-TT conversion is astronomy-engine's own Delta-T polynomial. Against IERS finals2000A (TT-UT1 = 32.184 + 37 - (UT1-UTC)) it reads 70.320 s vs 68.844 s on 2017-08-21, 71.578 vs 69.361 on 2020-01-01, 74.007 vs 69.200 on 2024-04-08 and 75.497 vs 69.196 on 2026-09-22 (+1.5, +2.2, +4.8, +6.3 s), growing about 1.2 s per year because the polynomial assumes a decelerating Earth. Swiss 2.10.03 gives 68.847, 69.361, 69.075, 68.821 s (within 0.0-0.4 s). No test in src/lib/engine or src/lib/time asserts any Delta-T value.
- evidence: residuals.log (AE DeltaT lines), swiss-anchors.log (swe.deltat_ex), anchors/finals2000A.all (IERS, downloaded 2026-09-22; UT1-UTC 2017-08-21 +0.3399, 2020-01-01 -0.1772, 2024-04-08 -0.0159, 2026-09-22 -0.0117 predicted). Implied Moon longitude error at 0.549 arcsec/s: 1.2 arcsec (2020), 2.6 (2024), 3.5 (2026). Visible in the 2024 eclipse anchor: engine TT residual 4.18 s vs alpha 0.68 s and Swiss 0.69 s (anchors.json). grep of *.test.ts for DeltaT/deltaT: none.
- impact: A systematic, currently avoidable arcsecond-scale Moon error that Swiss does not have, and the single largest lever on far-future disagreement (109.5 s at 2100, 420 s at 2300 where it explains 231 of the 262 arcsec Moon residual measured here). The benchmark attributes 93% of the 2100 residual to this but treats it as a convention; today's values are not a convention, they are measured by VLBI.
- fix: Ship a Delta-T table: IERS/USNO historic TT-UT1 to the present, Bulletin A prediction for the next year, then a documented long-term model (Stephenson-Morrison-Hohenkerk 2016 or Swiss-compatible) beyond it; expose the source in the receipt CONVENTIONS block; commit a fixture of 12 IERS-derived values with a 0.2 s gate and a yearly refresh job like transits-monthly.yml. Overlaps the time auditor's area; the gate belongs here.
- verifier: All Delta-T numbers reproduce to 0.001 s: astronomy-engine MakeTime tt-ut = 70.320 (2017-08-21), 71.578 (2020-01-01), 74.007 (2024-04-08), 75.497 (2026-09-22), 202.652 (2100), 717.161 (2300); IERS finals2000A Bulletin A UT1-UTC +0.3400 (I), -0.1772 (I), -0.0159 (I), -0.0117 (P) -> TT-UT1 68.844, 69.361, 69.200, 69.196; pyswisseph 2.10.03 deltat_ex 68.847, 69.361, 69.075, 68.821 (0.003-0.375 s from IERS). Cause confirmed: @zodiacs/engine dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js calls MakeTime(date) at lines 39/47/51/125 and never calls SetDeltaTFunction, and astronomy-engine's default DeltaT is DeltaT_EspenakMeeus (astronomy.js:1023,1141). No engine or time test asserts a Delta-T value; the only match is a comment at engine.test.ts:114. Correction: the finding's corroboration 'visible in the 2024 eclipse anchor: engine TT residual 4.18 s' is a misattribution. eTT = UTC_min + DeltaT_AE is exactly the TT at which the engine's ephemeris was evaluated, so eTT minus canon TT is Delta-T-independent ephemeris geometry (the engine's Moon-Sun separation minimum is 4.18 s late in TT, about 2.3 arcsec of relative longitude). The Delta-T error shows up in the UT residual, where it cancels against that geometry error to +0.87 s (anchors.json engine_UT_minus_canon_UT_s). Consistently, my venus-swiss.log check at 2024-04-08 18:17:18.3 UT finds the engine Moon only 0.72 arcsec from Swiss calc_ut despite a 4.9 s Delta-T difference (2.7 arcsec expected from Delta-T alone). The 1.2-3.5 arcsec figures are therefore the Delta-T component, not the total Moon error at any given instant.

## verification-honesty-3 — First independent anchors: shipped engine misses NASA Venus-transit greatest instants by 17-21 s where the alpha and Swiss hit within 1 s
- severity: major · status: confirmed · effort: 1 d
- claim: Against published NASA/Espenak geocentric greatest-transit and greatest-eclipse instants (an independent implementation on JPL DE405/DE406 plus its own reduction, corroborated by observed contact timings, not a raw observation), minimising apparent geocentric Sun-body separation gives, in TT: Venus 2004-06-08 engine +16.76 s, alpha +0.74 s, Swiss +0.87 s; Venus 2012-06-06 engine +21.07 s, alpha +0.69, Swiss +0.89; eclipse 2017-08-21 engine +1.51, alpha +1.15, Swiss +1.14; eclipse 2024-04-08 engine +4.18, alpha +0.68, Swiss +0.69. Minimum separations: engine 627.25/553.92 arcsec vs canon 627/554 and alpha 626.89/554.37. Along-track Venus-Sun relative rate about 0.067 arcsec/s puts the shipped engine 1.1-1.4 arcsec off at inferior conjunction, both transits in the same direction.
- evidence: anchors.mjs -> anchors.json/anchors.log (engine via @zodiacs/engine natalChart in UTC then TT = UTC + AE Delta-T; alpha via alpha-copy/src/node.mjs openPackFile on data-toolchain-packaging/D.v2.zeph, CORRECTED options, TT input); swiss-anchors.py -> swiss-anchors.json (swe.calc in ET, FLG_SWIEPH confirmed by return flag, .se1 files). Canon values from anchors/SE2017Aug21Tpath.html (18:26:40 TD, 18:25:31.8 UT), SE2024Apr08Tpath.html (18:18:29 TD, 18:17:18.3 UT), SE2001-2100.html catalog, transit04.html (08:19:44 UT, 627 arcsec), transit12.html (01:29:36 UT, 554 arcsec). Canon rounded to 1 s; UT-to-TT for the transits used TAI-UTC 32/34 s with UT1-UTC ignored (<0.9 s). Swiss agreeing with canon within 1.2 s on all four confirms the apparent-separation convention is the canon's.
- impact: This is the first check in the project against something other than a JPL-descended vector table, and it discriminates: the shipped engine is measurably below both Swiss and the alpha on a published, reproducible quantity, while the alpha matches the canon to its rounding. It also establishes that the D.v2.zeph pack plus CORRECTED reduction is fit for full-chart positions of Sun, Moon and Venus at the 0.05 arcsec level over 2004-2024. What it does not establish: absolute accuracy better than the canon's own DE405-era model (about 0.1-0.5 arcsec), or anything about the outer planets.
- fix: Commit the four events (plus the 1999-08-11 and 2009-07-22 eclipses and the 2016-05-09 and 2019-11-11 Mercury transits from the same canon) as src/lib/engine/fixtures/canon-events.json with TT residual gates: 2 s for the alpha, 25 s for the shipped engine until Venus improves; run in vitest for the engine and in tier-b for the alpha. Record the canon URLs and the rounding.
- verifier: Re-ran anchors.mjs (0.35 s) and swiss-anchors.py in my scratch: every number matches the auditor's to 0.01 s. TT residuals engine/alpha/Swiss: 2017 eclipse 1.51/1.15/1.14; 2024 eclipse 4.18/0.68/0.69; Venus 2004 16.76/0.74/0.87; Venus 2012 21.07/0.69/0.89; min separations engine 627.25/553.92, alpha 626.89/554.37, Swiss 626.89/554.37 vs canon 627/554. Swiss return flag asserted FLG_SWIEPH and not MOSEPH. Canon values verified in the cached NASA pages: SE2017Aug21Tpath.html 'Greatest Eclipse: 18:25:31.8 UT', SE2024Apr08Tpath.html '18:17:18.3 UT', SE2001-2100.html catalog 18:26:40 and 18:18:29 (TD), transit04.html 'Greatest 08:19:44' and '627 arc-seconds', transit12.html 'Greatest 01:29:36' and '554 arc-seconds', with transit12.html defining greatest transit as closest approach to the Sun's centre from Earth's centre. Independent cross-check (venus-swiss.log): at the canon UT instants the engine's Venus-minus-Sun relative longitude differs from Swiss calc_ut by +1.168 arcsec (2004) and +1.272 arcsec (2012), same sign, which at the measured -0.066 arcsec/s relative rate is 17.7 s and 19.3 s -- agreeing with the auditor's 16.35/19.49 s UT residuals and '1.1-1.4 arcsec, both in the same direction'. Note for readers: the eclipse TT residuals are pure ephemeris geometry; in UT the shipped engine lands -0.61 s and +0.87 s from canon UT because its Delta-T error cancels its Moon geometry error, so the Venus transits, not the eclipses, are the discriminating anchors for the shipped engine.

## verification-honesty-4 — '42 families each confirmed by a mutation that made it fail' is recorded for 13 families and one recorded mutation survives
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 2 d
- corrected claim: PARTITION-RESULTS.md:324-331 states forty-two test families across four files were each confirmed by a mutation that made it fail. The four files do contain 42 tests, but only the 11 in partitioned-search.nodetest.mjs name their mutation; the 31 in domain-partition, partition-enclosure-intervals and partition-enclosure-lighttime carry no mutation record anywhere in the tree, and reviewer A's committed report shows 8 of 16 mutations in the enclosure-construction steps surviving. One recorded mutation is not caught: replacing the `&& unprocessedTotal === 0` conjunct of completeOverRequest (partitioned-search.mjs:541) with `&& true` passes 42/42, because the preceding `exhaustiveOverAdmissible` conjunct already requires status === 'finished' (:520) and the named test (:252) only asserts on unfinished runs; the comment at :248-251 and the header at :12-14 are therefore false for that case. The code is not wrong (the conjunct is redundant); the statement about the tests is.
- claim: examples/precision-alpha/PARTITION-RESULTS.md:324-331 states forty-two test families across four files were each confirmed by a mutation that made it fail. Only test/tier-a/partitioned-search.nodetest.mjs (13 tests) names its mutation per test; domain-partition (20), partition-enclosure-intervals (6) and partition-enclosure-lighttime (5) carry no mutation record anywhere in the tree, and reviewer A's committed report shows 8 of 16 mutations in exactly those enclosure-construction steps surviving. Sampling six recorded mutations in a scratch copy: five caught, one survived: replacing the '&& unprocessedTotal === 0' conjunct of completeOverRequest with '&& true' passes 42/42, although partitioned-search.nodetest.mjs:248-251 says that mutation is caught and the file header (:12-14) says each was confirmed to fail.
- evidence: mutate.sh, mutations.log, mut-*.log under scratch/verification-honesty (alpha-copy at examples/precision-alpha, worktree untouched). Results: F1 isNativePlan disabled CAUGHT (2 fails), F3 tolerance read from plan.request CAUGHT (1), F6 drop status==='finished' CAUGHT (1), F6b drop unprocessedTotal===0 SURVIVED (0 fails, 42 pass), F7 spend() unenforced CAUGHT (2), F5 starved time pushed to excluded CAUGHT (4). Baseline alpha-copy npm test 423 pass in 23.1 s. grep -ci mutat on the three other files: 0. docs/platform/evidence/precision-partition/review/reviewer-a-domain-mathematics.md:215-240 (M5, M6, M7, M8, M10, M11, M17 survive).
- impact: The partition's soundness argument rests on these families as corroboration; a corroboration claim that is unverifiable for 29 of 42 families and false for one recorded case is exactly the kind of overclaim the tree elsewhere avoids. The surviving conjunct is redundant with notFullyExamined and the tiling check, so the code is not wrong, but the statement about the tests is.
- fix: Add examples/precision-alpha/tools/mutate.mjs that applies a committed ledger (mutation id, file, sed, expected failing test names) and commit its output as test/MUTATIONS.md; cover the 29 unrecorded families or reword PARTITION-RESULTS to 'thirteen'; correct the partitioned-search header and either remove the redundant conjunct or write a test that isolates it (a run with unprocessed time but notFullyExamined empty).
- verifier: Survivor reproduced in a fresh copy of examples/precision-alpha under verify/: baseline of the four partition files 42 tests / 42 pass; with `&& unprocessedTotal === 0` -> `&& true` at src/core/partitioned-search.mjs:541, still 42/42 (mut-F6b.log). Control: the auditor's F6 sed replacing `const exhaustiveOverAdmissible = status === 'finished'` with `= true` fails exactly test 38 'no completeness flag survives a run that stopped early' (41 pass / 1 fail), so the harness detects mutations. Mechanism: completeOverRequest (:538-544) starts with `exhaustiveOverAdmissible`, which itself requires `status === 'finished'` (:520), so for every unfinished run that the test at :252 asserts on the flag is already false and the unprocessedTotal conjunct is unreachable-redundant; the comment at :248-251 claiming the test catches this mutation is false and the header at :12-14 is false for that case. Counts corrected: node --test reports partitioned-search 11 tests, domain-partition 20, partition-enclosure-intervals 6, partition-enclosure-lighttime 5 = 42, matching PARTITION-RESULTS.md:324-331 ('forty-two ... twenty ... eleven ... eleven'). All 11 partitioned-search tests carry a 'Mutation this catches' comment; the auditor's '13' is the count of lines matching 'mutat' (grep -ci = 13), not tests. So recorded families are 11 of 42 and unrecorded 31, not 13 and 29. grep -ci mutat on the other three files = 0. Reviewer A's '16 semantic mutations run, 8 caught, 8 survived' with M5/M6/M7/M8/M17 in enclosure construction confirmed at reviewer-a-domain-mathematics.md:213-242.

## verification-honesty-5 — CI enforces only the literal gate and frozen Swiss fixtures; tier-b, the benchmark, Delta-T and every anchor are documentation only
- severity: major · status: adjusted · effort: 3 d
- corrected claim: CI exercises the engine only through 'npm test' (vitest run, Node 22) and the alpha's tier-a node:test suite (site-check.yml:145-146 and :153-155); no workflow runs tier-b (85 tests, needs PRECISION_PACK; 84/85 pass in an out-of-tree copy here, the one failure being an import of a docs/ path that exists only in the full tree, 85/85 in the toolchain auditor's in-tree run), the 180-case Swiss benchmark, any Delta-T check, or any observational anchor. The methodology page's Swiss-agreement figures are therefore never re-established on a change. The vitest engine.test.ts load failure seen in this audit is a symlinked-node_modules artifact of the scratch worktree, not a CI defect, but nothing asserts that the accuracy vectors actually executed.
- claim: In .github/workflows/site-check.yml the engine is exercised by 'Engine accuracy vectors' (npm test = vitest run, Node 22 only) and 'Precision alpha tier A' (node --test tier-a, synthetic packs). Nothing runs tier-b (85 tests, needs PRECISION_PACK; passed 85/85 in 59 s against the compiled D.v2.zeph in this audit), the 180-case Swiss benchmark, any Delta-T check, any observational anchor, or the server/browser ephemeris parity beyond the vitest file. The accuracy story that exceeds Swiss (0.16 arcsec prototype) is therefore never re-verified on a change.
- evidence: site-check.yml:139-149 (the two engine steps), package.json 'test': 'vitest run'; examples/precision-alpha/package.json 'test' vs 'test:data'; test/tier-b/README.md (no kernel committed); docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/RESULTS.md (one-off, measured 2026-09-20); scratch/audit/data-toolchain-packaging/tier-b.log and compile-D.log (pack compiled from de440s.bsp in 24.5 s, 1.66 MiB, sha256 258cd562...). Also: in this worktree vitest ran 605 tests green while engine.test.ts failed to load (symlinked node_modules outside vite's fs.allow, vitest-engine-time.log) and the run was saved only by the non-zero exit; there is no assertion that the accuracy suite executed N vectors.
- impact: An accuracy regression in astronomy-engine, the reduction, or the alpha's real-pack path can merge with CI fully green; the benchmark numbers on the methodology page cannot be re-established automatically.
- fix: Minimal additions: (1) commit the Horizons reference JSON from finding 1 and the canon events from finding 3, run in vitest; (2) commit a small compiled synthetic pack plus a 1-2 MiB real DE440s excerpt pack (1990-2030) under examples/precision-alpha/test/fixtures with its sha256 and run tier-b real-pack against it in CI (pack-versus-kernel stays local); (3) a Delta-T fixture gate (finding 2); (4) a quarterly workflow that re-runs the Swiss benchmark corpus with cached .se1 files and diffs report-measure.json p50/p95/max within 0.05 arcsec; (5) assert in engine.test.ts that the reference set has >= N rows so a load failure cannot pass silently.
- verifier: Workflow read: the two engine steps are 'Engine accuracy vectors: npm test' at .github/workflows/site-check.yml:145-146 and 'Precision alpha tier A' (working-directory examples/precision-alpha, npm test) at :153-155 -- the auditor's 139-149 is off by six lines. package.json 'test' = 'vitest run'; examples/precision-alpha/package.json 'test' = node --test tier-a only, 'test:data' = tier-b. grep -rni over all 18 workflow files for tier-b, test:data, PRECISION_PACK, swiss, benchmark, deltat = 0 hits, so nothing in CI runs tier-b, the Swiss benchmark, a Delta-T check or any anchor. tier-b/README.md:14-15,21-23 confirms no pack or kernel is committed; RESULTS.md is headed 'measured 2026-09-20'. Tier-b re-run in my scratch copy with PRECISION_PACK=D.v2.zeph PRECISION_KERNEL=de440s.bsp: 85 tests, 84 pass, 1 fail in 58.6 s; the failure is pack-versus-kernel.nodetest.mjs importing ../../docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/src/spk2.mjs via tools/spk-backend.mjs, a path outside examples/precision-alpha that does not exist in an out-of-tree copy (the toolchain auditor's in-tree log shows 85/85). The vitest load failure in the auditor's vitest-engine-time.log (engine.test.ts could not resolve astronomy-engine through the symlinked node_modules; 605 other tests green; non-zero exit) is confirmed as an artifact of the scratch worktree: the repo has no vitest config, so vitest's default include picks up engine.test.ts and CI's real node_modules would load it. The absence of any 'reference set has >= N rows' assertion is confirmed by reading the file.

## alpha-reduction-math-3 — Interval allowances are covered by slack, not by the stated analysis, in three places (no counterexample found)
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: (a) frame-of-date.mjs forms the combined nutation arguments ARG_A[j], ARG_B[j] as double sums of five products and then pads them RELATIVE TO THE RESULT (I.iv / I.scale, 8u); for a cancelling sum the formation error exceeds that pad. Exact-rational check: term j=23 (multipliers [1,0,2,-2,2], A = -86773.3098") has formation error 1.164e-10" against a pad of 7.71e-11" (ratio 1.51), leaving 3.9e-11" = 1.9e-16 rad of argument uncovered; ARG_B worst ratio is 0.20. Containment survives only because sinCosInterval's ABS_ERR (4e-15) has room, and the module comment attributes that room to a different rounding. (b) Ephemeris.raw()/seriesAt() promise |tau| <= 1 after the record-selection fix, but for a pack whose initEt/intervalSec are not integers the computed tau at a record start is -1.0000000000000002 (reproduced at 6 of 7 edges of a synthetic pack); |T_k| then exceeds 1 by about k^2 * 2.2e-16, which is inside clenshawRoundoff's 4n^2u only because that allowance is generous (measured worst actual Clenshaw error is 6.35 per cent of it). (c) seriesBounds uses sum|d_k| of the floating-point derivative() coefficients as the Lipschitz constant with no pad; measured under-statement is at most 6.98e-17 relative (below u), again absorbed by the roundoff term. The interval primitives themselves (add, sub, mul, div, scale, sqrt, norm) passed a 60,000-case exact-rational containment fuzz with zero failures, and the 4n^2u Clenshaw bound held with 16x margin over 3,102 exact evaluations across five coefficient families including tau = 1+ulp.
- evidence: R/examples/precision-alpha/src/core/frame-of-date.mjs:118-131 (ARG_A/ARG_B), 164-174 (the comment about what covers the rounding); ephemeris.mjs:233-251 (record selection, tau); cheb.mjs:45-55 (derivative), 86-90 (clenshawRoundoff), retarded.mjs:147-159 (mean-value enclosure using bnd.maxAbsFirst unpadded). Artifacts: S/enclosures/argb-rounding.mjs and interval-fuzz.mjs (ARG_A worst term JSON in the output), boundary.mjs (tau = -1.0000000000000002 at 6 edges; plain floor() wrong at 3/10 probes, the while-loops correct it; one ulp past the last inclusive end refused), clenshaw-bound2.mjs (worst ratio 6.350e-2; derivative understatement 6.976e-17; (n-1)/2 growth claim verified for n = 8, 20, 32, 64).
- impact: Not a wrong answer today: no input interval was found whose enclosure excludes the true value, in seven families tried (primitive ops with exact rationals; derived-constant formation; record-boundary tau; Clenshaw with all-ones, alternating, random, geometric and ephemeris-like coefficients at tau = +-1, +-(1-1e-12), 1+ulp; sinCos quadrant boundaries via the tree's own BigInt test; mean-value half-width construction). But a completeness theorem whose padding is right by luck is weaker than Swiss's plain numbers only in the sense that it claims more; the claim should be made true by construction.
- fix: Pad ARG_A/ARG_B by an absolute allowance equal to 5u times the sum of |multiplier * constant| (the term magnitudes, not the result), or compute them once as exact rationals and store the double plus its residual; in ephemeris.mjs clamp the computed tau into [-1, 1] only when the record has already been proven to contain et (and document that the residual is a tau-rounding term of at most 2u); add an explicit tau-argument term n^2 * u * sum|c| and a (n/2) u relative pad on sum|d_k| to the cheb.mjs analysis so the allowance is justified rather than measured. Add the two exact-rational scripts as tier-A tests.

## alpha-reduction-math-4 — Five documentation statements in the core contradict the code or each other
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: (1) reduce.mjs:244-248 says ERFA's eraLdsun clamp 'silently caps the deflection at roughly a tenth of an arcsecond'; deflection.mjs:70-75 measures the actual peak under dlim=1e-6 at 5.7586" and says the reduce.mjs comment is wrong but leaves it. (2) CONTRACT.coordinates calls distKm 'the geometric geocentric distance' while reduce.mjs:230-231 returns |target(emission) - observer(reception)|, the light-time-corrected distance (the quantity Swiss also returns; the label is what is wrong). (3) CONTRACT.corrections and TIME_MODEL call TDB-TT a 'two-term' form; tdbMinusTt has three terms (0.001658 sin g + 0.000014 sin 2g + 0.0000224 sin(L-LJ)). (4) trig.mjs:170 states the cosine truncation term as (pi/4)^20/20! <= 4e-21; the polynomial stops at z^8 = r^16 so the first omitted term is (pi/4)^18/18! = 2.0e-18 (still 2,000x under ABS_ERR). (5) trig.mjs:196 says PIO2_HI has its 'low 33 bits clear'; 0x3FF921FB54400000 has 22 trailing zero bits (fdlibm's 'first 33 bits of pi/2' means 33 significant bits); exactness of k*PIO2_HI for |k| <= 5 holds either way. Also CONTRACT.notModelled omits the Shapiro delay, which deflection.mjs's profile does list.
- evidence: File:line references above; the TDB-TT term count is visible in reduce.mjs:149-153 and frame-of-date.mjs:413-418; cos polynomial coefficients trig.mjs:248-256 end at 1/16! = 4.7794773323873853e-14.
- impact: None on numbers; each one misleads a reviewer of the error analysis, and (1) understates a 5.8" effect by a factor of 58 in the shipped reducer's own comment.
- fix: Correct the five comments/labels and add 'Shapiro delay (<= ~2e-4 s of light-time, < 1e-7 arcsec)' to CONTRACT.notModelled.

## alpha-search-partition-4 — Record-wide Lipschitz constants make a double root of f′ catastrophic: 794,201 evaluations and 101,850 unmerged unresolved slivers (geometric), 4,000,001 and zero events (retarded rungs)
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: searchPiece uses M1 = max|f′| and M2 = max|f″| over the WHOLE record (validated-search.mjs:181-190, 256) for every sub-cell, so where f and f′ both vanish (a triple root, or a station lying on the target level) the undecided band shrinks only as w^{1/3} (exclusion) and √w (monotone) rather than with w; the run bisects to the 1e-4 s floor across ±4 s and emits one unresolved entry per floor cell without merging (line 466). The retarded rungs, with the same global bounds plus ~20 evaluations per cell, exhaust the 4,000,000 budget and return nothing.
- evidence: Fixture 'triple-root' (p(t) = (t−r0)³/3600², observer at rest, X0 = 1e8 km): geometric → found 0, established false, 101,850 unresolved entries, 794,201 evaluations; retarded/aberrated/of-date(identity) → status budget-exhausted, 4,000,001 evaluations, found 0. Contrast 'tangency' (double root of f, simple root of f′): 186 evaluations, 11 unresolved. Artifact: …/audit/alpha-search-partition/fixtures/results.json (run.mjs, 172 s total for 20 fixtures × 6 rungs).
- impact: An exact triple root does not occur in nature, but a station within ~1e-3° of the target level produces the same regime for the cells near it; the A2 Uranus contract case ('two passes near a station') already shows brackets of 5,495 s in the empirical mode. Cost near stations is what a station finder (finding 3) would meet head-on.
- fix: Compute local derivative bounds per cell: either re-expand the derivative series on the sub-interval (Chebyshev economisation / Clenshaw-Curtis on [lo,hi]) or evaluate the derivative series by interval arithmetic over the cell; both make M1, M2 shrink with w so the undecided band is O(rounding). Merge adjacent unresolved spans as retarded-search.mergeSpans does. Add a fixture with f′ double root to tier-a asserting a bounded unresolved count.

## alpha-search-partition-5 — partitionKey omits every numerical constant whose change would move spans, so an imported plan from a different build passes the key check
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: partitionKey (domain-partition.mjs:143-162) hashes contract id, profile id, pack digest, pack structure, observer, body, window, boundaryToleranceSec, relightWidthRatio, maxTauWidenings and tauPadFloorSec. Not included, although each changes the classification: MIN_ELONGATION_RAD and the two guard constants (deflection.mjs:111-150, covered only by the profile-id string), PAD (interval.mjs:18), clenshawRoundoff's 4n²u (cheb.mjs:87-90), C_KM_S, the solveTau contraction tolerance 0.5 (domain-partition.mjs:414), the crude emission window pad 1.2·half+1 (line 419), the widening factor 0.25 (line 465), the free light-time tightening rule (lines 359-364), the record-boundary seeding rule, the trig ABS_ERR, and the package version.
