The partitioned path refuses non-native plans by default, so this matters only under acceptImportedPlan, which is the documented cross-process path.
- evidence: Key emitted by the F2 Moon partition: 'zodiacs-domain-partition/1|zodiacs-deflected-of-date/1|d21783461f…|907402c97fbb91ef|0,0,0,0,0,0|Moon|tdb|-711201535.816|-685281535.816|60|8|6|0.000001' (phase-share.json). Reviewer B's finding 2 (review/REVIEW.md) already moved the budget question out of the key; nothing there covers algorithm identity.
- impact: A cache of serialized plans accepted with acceptImportedPlan would silently keep proofs computed under a previous build's constants; the result already labels such runs CONDITIONAL, so this is a hygiene gap rather than an unsound verdict.
- fix: Add a `numericsPolicy` object collecting every constant named above plus package.json version, hash it (the existing FNV fingerprint is fine), and include the hash as a key component; expose it in plan.request.identity so a consumer's cache can key on it.

## alpha-search-partition-6 — tauPadFactor is a dead option: accepted, validated, typed and documented, never read
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: RETARDED_DEFAULTS.tauPadFactor = 64 (retarded-search.mjs:229) is validated as positive (line 765) and declared in types/experimental.d.ts:36 with the description 'first half-width of the candidate light-time interval, as a multiple of the solver's own error bound', but no code reads it; retardedCell derives T from tauSlope·half + errorSec + tauPadFloorSec (lines 379-383).
- evidence: grep -rn tauPadFactor examples/precision-alpha/src → exactly one hit (the default). Passing tauPadFactor: 1e-9 or 1e9 changes nothing.
- impact: A consumer tuning it gets no effect and no refusal; the type docs describe a behaviour that does not exist.
- fix: Remove the option from RETARDED_DEFAULTS and the .d.ts (an unknown key is then refused, which is the existing contract), or implement it as the multiplier on rough.errorSec.

## alpha-search-partition-7 — Endpoint-root ownership fires only on exact zeros, so window-edge and record-boundary roots cost the proven claim in every rung
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.75 d
- claim: validated-search.mjs:297-299 reports an 'endpoint' root only when the fitted polynomial evaluates to exactly 0 at the cell edge; on fitted data the value is a few ulps, |f| ≤ RHO, and lines 323/362 file the cell as unresolved. The retarded rungs have no ownership rule at all: an endpoint enclosure straddling zero is filed unresolved (retarded-search.mjs:1062-1066). EVALUATION-RESULTS.md:183 records that the plan's boundary convention C2 'never triggered', so this path was untested on real data.
- evidence: Fixtures (results.json, results-edges-retarded.json): geometric left-edge root → found 0, unresolved 1, 6 evaluations; geometric root within |δ| ≤ 1e-8 s of a record boundary → found 1, established false; retarded reception root at either window edge → found 0, unresolved 1; retarded reception root within 1e-6 s of a record boundary → found 0, unresolved 1, while at 1e-5 s → found 1, proven. No wrong answer in any case.
- impact: Negligible probability per root on real data (~1e-11 to 1e-13), but it means splitting a window at an instant that happens to be a root turns two proven halves into two unresolved ones, and the 'endpoint' event kind is effectively unreachable on packs.
- fix: When a cell is proven monotone and one endpoint value lies within RHO (or its enclosure straddles zero), report an 'endpoint' event with bracket [edge − reach, edge + reach], reach = RHO/slopeFloor (or the enclosure half-width divided by mig(F′)), owned by the [lo, hi) convention, and keep `established`: monotonicity already proves at most one root in the cell and locates it within reach of the edge. Add the edge fixtures above to tier-a.

## alpha-search-partition-8 — The of-date and deflected rungs' projection weights enter as point values whose declared 4e-15 error is not carried into the enclosure
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: retarded-search.mjs:583-585 takes wL = sinCos(L) and forms dF = [wL.s, −wL.c, 0]; proj (line 504) multiplies interval components by these plain numbers via I.scale, which widens by PAD = 8·u ≈ 8.9e-16 relative (interval.mjs:18-23). trig.mjs declares an absolute error bound of 4e-15 on sinCos (OF_DATE_CONTRACT.evaluation cites it as the reason Math.sin is not used), which exceeds PAD by ~4.5x for |s| near 1; the enclosure is therefore covered only by the MEASURED worst error (1.484e-16, trig.nodetest) and not by the declared bound the contract rests on. Unconfirmed as a counterexample: no root can be moved by it at any realistic margin (≤ 4e-15·|Q| ≈ 4e-7 km ≈ 1e-8 s).
- evidence: Code reading of retarded-search.mjs:504, 583-585; interval.mjs:18-23; trig.mjs header; OF_DATE_CONTRACT.evaluation string (retarded-search.mjs:139).
- impact: Proof hygiene only: the 'no unspecified host function enters this mode' claim is true, but the chain is sound by measurement rather than by the stated analysis.
- fix: Use I.around(wL.s, ABS_ERR) and I.around(wL.c, ABS_ERR) (or sinCosInterval at a point) for the projection weights so the declared bound travels with the value.

## alpha-search-partition-9 — zodiacs-partitioned-search/1 has no invariant gate: a self-inconsistent result is reported, not refused
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: buildResult enforces eight invariants for zodiacs-precision-search/2 (result.mjs:83-158) but searchDeflectedOverPartition assembles its object by hand (partitioned-search.mjs:546-708) with no equivalent check: coversRequestExactly is computed and returned even when false (lines 650-652); nothing asserts isExactTotalOverRequest ⇒ exhaustiveOverAdmissible ⇒ every admissible span in spanResults is finished and established, that eventCount.found === events.length, that no 'established' event lies outside plan.admissible, or that eligibilityEstablished + eligibilityAmbiguous === found.
- evidence: Code reading; the fields are set from the same local variables today, so they agree by construction, which is exactly the situation result.mjs's header says a gate exists to protect.
- impact: A future edit can ship a contradictory partitioned result silently; the released contract cannot.
- fix: Add buildPartitionedResult() with the invariants above (throwing 'unsupported-option' like buildResult), route the one return through it, and pin it with the same style of test result.mjs has.

## alpha-search-partition-10 — compile.mjs treats --help as a full compile into the package tree from a machine-specific default kernel path
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: tools/compiler/compile.mjs has no --help handling (argOf at lines 47-50 ignores unknown flags), defaults `--kernel` to the absolute path <swisslab>/de440s.bsp (line 342) and `--out` to tools/compiler/packs/<candidate>.zeph inside the vendored package (line 343). Running `node tools/compiler/compile.mjs --help` therefore compiled a 1.7 MB pack from that kernel into the worktree.
- evidence: Observed during this audit: the command emitted {candidate: 'D', out: '…/examples/precision-alpha/tools/compiler/packs/D.zeph', bytes: 1737632} in under 60 s and left an untracked directory; moved to …/audit/alpha-search-partition/pack/ and the tree re-verified clean (git status empty). The vendored SOURCE.md describes the tool as 'a tested route from their own kernel to a local pack' with no mention of the default path.
- impact: A consumer following SOURCE.md gets a surprising write into node_modules or the repo, and CI drift checks on the vendored tree could trip; harmless to correctness.
- fix: Add --help/-h handling, refuse unknown flags, require --kernel explicitly (no absolute default), default --out to the current working directory, and add tools/compiler/packs/ to .gitignore.

## angles-houses-aspects-3 — Placidus refused at a hard |lat| > 66 while Swiss computes to 90 - eps (66.56): Rovaniemi gets a whole-sign fallback chart
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 0.5 d
- corrected claim: placidusCusps refuses at a hard |lat| > 66 while Swiss computes Placidus up to |lat| < 90 - eps_true (66.53 in 1800, 66.56 today, 66.59 in 2200); in the 66.0-66.56 band the engine returns a flagged whole-sign fallback although its own iteration converges there and matches Swiss cusps to 0.0085 arcsec given Swiss inputs. The fallback is documented and flagged and ASC/MC stay correct, so this is a narrow, documented conservative limit; note that widening it without first fixing finding 1 would expose 2.2 deg cusp errors at 66.5.
- claim: placidusCusps returns null for |lat| > 66 (chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:46) and computeHouses substitutes whole-sign cusps flagged 'polar-fallback' (:91-107). Swiss computes Placidus for |lat| < 90 - eps_true (about 66.56 deg today) and only then fails to Porphyry. The band 66.0 < |lat| <= 66.56 (Rovaniemi 66.50 N, Salekhard 66.53 N, Grimsey 66.54 N) therefore gets a different house system from Swiss although the engine's own iteration converges there. The fallback is confirmed to be whole sign and flagged; it is not Porphyry, so even above 66.56 a Swiss-comparing user sees a different chart.
- evidence: Fine grid 5,616 cases (|lat| 55.0..66.6 step 0.1, both hemispheres, 1800/2000/2200, hours 0..21 step 3): engine null 288 (all at |lat| >= 66.1), Swiss failure 48 (all at |lat| = 66.6, its minimum failing latitude), 240 cases where the engine refused but Swiss returned Placidus; whole-sign fallback vs Swiss Placidus cusps max 108.7 deg (66.1) to 146.4 deg (66.5). fallbackIsWholeAndFlagged = true for all 288. No engine null at |lat| <= 66.0 and the |arg| >= 1 bail-out (:56) is unreachable there (tan(phi) tan(delta) < tan(phi) tan(eps) < 1 iff |phi| < 90 - eps). Concrete: 1995-06-15T12:00Z Rovaniemi 66.5034 N 25.7294 E: engine houses.system 'whole', flags ['polar-fallback'], cusps 180,210,...; Swiss houses_ex 'P' status 0, cusp11 147.039, cusp12 172.825. Swiss per-system polar table (swiss.json 'systems'): P, K, G, i fail within the polar circle (pyswisseph raises 'within polar circle, switched to Porphyry'); O, R, C, E, A, W, V, X, H, T, B, M, U, Y, L, Q, I, D, F, N, S all compute at 69.65 and 85 deg; R, C, T, Y, I flip the MC by 180 deg at polar latitude when it is below the horizon (mc 72.911 vs 252.911 at Tromso 2001-12-21T09:30Z). Artifacts: engine-vs-swiss.json (summaryB, byLat_66plus), gridB-rows.json, swiss.json.
- impact: Users inside the 66.0-66.56 band receive a chart Swiss-based software will not reproduce, and the README's '66 deg' wording misstates Swiss's limit (90 - eps). A per-system, obliquity-aware polar policy is the prerequisite for adding Koch/Gauquelin (which share the limit) and for an explicit fallback contract that can exceed Swiss's silent Porphyry substitution.
- fix: Replace the constant with |lat| >= 90 - obliquityTrue (strict, in degrees) computed from the same obliquity used for the cusps; return {houses, fellBack, fallbackSystem} and add a 'porphyry' system so the fallback can be Swiss-compatible (Porphyry is a closed form of ASC/MC); document the policy per system; add fine-grid tests at 66.0-66.6 against the Swiss P and O arrays already frozen in swiss-node-polar.fixture.json (placidusRequest arrays).
- verifier: Phenomenon and numbers confirmed. chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:46 is the hard constant (|lat| > 66 -> null) and :91-107 substitutes whole-sign cusps with fellBack=true. Rovaniemi 1995-06-15T12:00Z 66.5034N 25.7294E: shipped computeChart -> houses.system 'whole', flags ['polar-fallback'], cusps 180,210,240; Swiss houses_ex 'P' (SWIEPH) succeeds with cusp11 147.0393, cusp12 172.8253, ASC/MC agreeing with the engine to 1.5 arcsec. Swiss ladder (8 hours x lat 66.0..67.0, four epochs): Placidus fails first at 66.55 in 1800 (90 - eps_true = 66.5326), at 66.57 in 1995 and 2000 (limit 66.5625/66.5620), at 66.6 in 2200 (66.5892) - i.e. exactly |lat| >= 90 - eps_true, confirming the auditor's dynamic limit. Copying the dist chunk to scratch with the constant replaced by |lat| >= 90 - input.obliquity and running the 336-case 66.05-66.55 ladder (both hemispheres, 1800/2000/2200, 8 hours): status agrees with Swiss in 336/336 (16 nulls = the 16 Swiss failures), and with Swiss ARMC + true obliquity the engine's iteration matches Swiss cusps to 0.0085 arcsec, so convergence is not the obstacle; the shipped code refuses all 320 cases Swiss computes; whole-sign fallback vs Swiss Placidus differs by up to 147.5 deg. Two corrections: (a) pyswisseph 2.10.03 raises only 'swisseph.houses_ex: error' - the 'within polar circle, switched to Porphyry' text is the C library's serr and is not surfaced by the wrapper; (b) severity. The behaviour is documented (STRATEGY.md 'hard fallback to Whole Sign above 66 deg', README section 2), flagged in the result, leaves ASC/MC correct, and affects a band of at most 0.53-0.59 deg of latitude, so it is a small real defect rather than a gap that keeps the stack materially below Swiss.

## angles-houses-aspects-5 — House placement ignores ecliptic latitude: differs from Swiss house_pos in 2.75% of placements (Pluto 12.75%, Moon 3.25%)
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: houseOf brackets a body's longitude between cusp longitudes (chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:108-120): start-inclusive, end-exclusive, wrap-safe, degenerate zero span returns that house. This is consistent with Swiss house_pos for zero latitude in every sampled case, but Swiss's Placidus house_pos uses the body's ecliptic latitude (semi-arc position), and the two disagree for bodies with latitude near a cusp.
- evidence: 400 random charts (1850-2150, |lat| <= 60), 4,400 placements of Sun..Pluto + true node with Swiss lon/lat and Swiss 'P' cusps: engine bracketing vs swe.house_pos(armc, lat, eps_true, (lon, lat), 'P') differs in 121 (2.75%): Pluto 51/400, Moon 13, Venus 12, Saturn 10, Mars 9, Mercury 8, Neptune 8, Jupiter 5, Uranus 5, Sun 0, Node 0; vs house_pos with latitude 0: 0/4,400 differ. Example 1910-04-20T04:22Z lat 39.56: Pluto lon 85.155 lat -6.758 -> engine house 6, Swiss house_pos 5.996 (house 5). Artifact: swiss.json 'housepos' (swiss_dump.py, part of the 1.6 s run).
- impact: Not an error (longitude bracketing is the dominant chart-drawing convention) but a capability Swiss has and the engine lacks; a body 'in house 6' by longitude that is in house 5 by semi-arc is exactly the kind of disagreement practitioners notice.
- fix: Add housePosition(system, armc, lat, eps, lon, bodyLat) returning a fractional house (Swiss-compatible semantics for P/K/R/C, longitude for W/E/O), keep houseOf as the default, and flag bodies within 1 deg of a cusp with the signed distance.

## angles-houses-aspects-6 — Declination derived with mean obliquity (or fixed J2000 23.4392911 when no instant is known): up to 44.5" (1900) / 8.4" error; the transformation formula itself is correct
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: declinationOf(lon, lat, eps) implements sin(dec) = sin(b) cos(eps) + cos(b) sin(eps) sin(l) correctly (ecliptic3d.ts:129-139). EclipticView passes meanObliquity(julianCenturies(utcMs)) (EclipticView.tsx:226-228) and DepthFigure the same or OBLIQUITY when utcMs is null (DepthFigure.astro:104); findDeclinationAspects defaults to OBLIQUITY (declination.ts:72). Mean obliquity omits nutation in obliquity; the fixed constant additionally omits the secular drift (46.8"/century).
- evidence: 36 Swiss rows (Sun, Moon, Pluto at 1900/1950/2050, Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct, Swiss lon/lat as input, Swiss FLG_EQUATORIAL declination as reference): fixed-J2000 error max 44.46" (Moon 1900-01-01), 43.26" (Pluto 1900-04-01), 31.2" (1950), 29.9" (2050); mean-of-date error max 8.37" (Sun 1950-07-01), Moon 8.10", Pluto 6.08"; true-of-date error 0.000" on every row (proves the formula). Artifact: swiss.json 'declination' (formula replicated verbatim in Python; the TS module could not be imported under strip-types because of extensionless imports, the Python port is line-identical). Orbs: DECLINATION_ORB 1 deg, luminary 1.5 deg (declination.ts:24-25).
- impact: At a 1 deg orb the 8" mean-obliquity error flips no verdicts, but the engine package exposes no declination or equatorial coordinates at all while Swiss returns them natively (FLG_EQUATORIAL) with true obliquity; parallels/contraparallels exist only as a site drawing feature.
- fix: Have the engine emit declination and right ascension per body from astronomy-engine's equatorial-of-date vector (no formula needed, true obliquity implicit), move findDeclinationAspects into the package with those values, and drop the OBLIQUITY default path (require an obliquity or an instant).

## angles-houses-aspects-8 — Chart-shape thresholds leave dead zones: even 10-planet spreads of 122-148 deg and 182-223 deg classify as nothing
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: measureChartShape declares bundle span <= 120, bowl 150-180, locomotive 225-255 (chart-shape.ts:62,67,68) with a +/-2 deg band; a spread between 120 and 150 or between 180 and 225 that is not a bucket or seesaw returns status 'no-clear', reason 'outside-conventions'. Jones's scheme treats bundle <= 120 and bowl <= 180 as contiguous, so 130-140 deg charts are bowls in the literature.
- evidence: Run of the real TS module (node --experimental-strip-types): even spreads span 110 -> bundle clear; 121 -> near-threshold; 130 and 140 -> outside-conventions (no candidate at all); 149/151/179/181 -> near-threshold bowl; 200 -> outside-conventions; 224/226/254/256 -> near-threshold locomotive; 300 -> near-threshold splash. The 150 lower bound is asserted in chart-shape.test.ts:57 (['bowl',150,false]) so the hole is deliberate but undocumented outside the test. Artifact: command output in this audit's transcript (script inline).
- impact: Not a Swiss comparison (Swiss has no chart shapes) but an 'advanced' feature that silently declines a large fraction of real charts; the CONTEXT_CONVENTION string should either document the holes or close them.
- fix: Either extend bowl to 120-180 (with the band) and locomotive to 180-255 per Jones, or state the two excluded ranges in the convention text and the UI 'absence' copy; add tests at 135 and 200 deg.

## angles-houses-aspects-12 — docs/engine-validation/README.md overstates angle validation ('1.57" worst angle, cusps exact') and misstates Swiss's polar limit as 66 deg
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: The summary table row 'Angles and houses | Swiss houses_ex, polar and ordinary | 1.57" worst angle, cusps exact' and section 2 ('Above 66 deg absolute latitude Swiss returns C status -1') generalise three polar whole-sign cases and five Placidus cases with 0.1/0.2 deg gates (engine.test.ts:386-406). The measured worst angle across the supported latitude range is 512" and Swiss's limit is 90 - eps (66.56 deg), measured here as first failing at 66.6.
- evidence: docs/engine-validation/README.md table row and section '2. Angles and houses'; engine.test.ts:400-404 gates 0.1 and 0.2 deg; engine-vs-swiss.json summaryA.all.asc_shipped.max 512.5"; summaryB.swissFail_min_abs_lat 66.6, engine refused 240 cases where Swiss succeeded.
- impact: A reader of the honest-summary page concludes angles are validated to the arcsecond everywhere; they are validated to 0.1 deg at five latitudes <= 60.2 and not at all at 60-66.
- fix: Replace the row with the grid distribution (p50/p95/max per latitude band), state the 90 - eps limit, and link the new 63-66 deg vectors once finding 1 is fixed.

## angles-houses-aspects-14 — Site horizon drawing uses mean sidereal time while the engine uses apparent: 0.005 deg internal inconsistency
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: DepthFigure.astro:113 computes the zenith from ramcOf({ gastHours: gmstHours(utcMs) }) - Greenwich MEAN sidereal time - while the chart's ASC comes from astronomy-engine's apparent sidereal time; the comment in ecliptic3d.ts:196-201 acknowledges the ~1.1 s (0.005 deg) offset.
- evidence: src/components/DepthFigure.astro:104,113; src/lib/wheel/ecliptic3d.ts:196-206 (gmstHours) and its comment; equation of the equinoxes measured in the grid as up to 1.98" of RAMC difference between apparent and mean-based paths.
- impact: Drawing-only, invisible at screen resolution, but it is a second sidereal-time implementation in the codebase that will drift from the engine's conventions.
- fix: Pass the engine's gastHours (already on the chart input) into DepthFigure instead of recomputing GMST.

## data-toolchain-packaging-2 — A pack compiled with the default --out ships inside the npm archive
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 0.25 d
- corrected claim: package.json `files` ships tools/compiler/ wholesale with no *.zeph exclusion and compile.mjs defaults --out to tools/compiler/packs/ and unconditionally creates that directory even with an explicit --out; a pack left there is included by `npm pack` (87 files, D.zeph included). Mitigations the auditor did not weigh: the package is private:true (npm publish refuses) and the documented command already uses --out=./D.zeph, so the exposure is a hand-shared tarball after deviating from the docs.
- claim: package.json `files` includes `tools/compiler/` wholesale; compile.mjs defaults --out to `tools/compiler/packs/<candidate>.zeph` (compile.mjs:343) and unconditionally mkdirs that directory inside the package (compile.mjs:562). There is no .npmignore. Any developer who runs the documented compile without --out and then `npm pack` distributes a derived JPL coefficient set, which every rights document says must not happen.
- evidence: Copied examples/precision-alpha to scratch, placed D.zeph at tools/compiler/packs/D.zeph, ran `npm pack --dry-run --json`: 87 files, `tools/compiler/packs/D.zeph included: true`. Artifact: <audit>/data-toolchain-packaging/npm-pack-packs-hazard.txt and npm-pack-dryrun.json. Also observed: my compile with an explicit --out still created tools/compiler/packs/ inside the read-only worktree (removed afterwards; `git status` clean).
- impact: Direct contradiction of the stated posture 'no pack is distributed while the rights question is unsettled'; a single careless pack turns the MIT archive into a redistributor of derived JPL data.
- fix: Add `!tools/compiler/packs/**` and `!**/*.zeph` to `files` (or a .npmignore), make --out default to process.cwd(), remove the unconditional mkdirSync of a package-internal directory, and add a tier-A test that runs `npm pack --dry-run --json` and asserts no *.zeph/*.bsp path is listed.
- verifier: Reproduced: copied examples/precision-alpha to scratch, placed D.zeph at tools/compiler/packs/, `npm pack --dry-run --json` → entryCount 87, tools/compiler/packs/D.zeph (1,737,632 B) listed (npm-pack-result.txt, npm-pack-dryrun.json). package.json files includes 'tools/compiler/' wholesale; no .npmignore; no .gitignore rule for *.zeph. The unconditional mkdirSync at compile.mjs:562 is also confirmed by execution: I deleted packs/ and it was recreated (22:28) by a compile that passed an explicit --out elsewhere. Two facts the auditor omitted lower the severity: (1) package.json:4 is `private: true`, so `npm publish` refuses — only a hand-shared `npm pack` tarball can carry the pack; (2) the documented compile command (examples/00-prepare-a-pack.md:18-19) already passes `--out=./D.zeph`, so the auditor's 'runs the documented compile without --out' misdescribes the docs — the hazard needs a developer to deviate from them. The defect (files glob + default --out inside the package + unconditional mkdir + no ignore rule) is real but small.

## data-toolchain-packaging-3 — RIGHTS.md claims the pack header carries the NAIF attribution obligations; it does not
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 0.5 d
- corrected claim: RIGHTS.md overstates one clause: the compiled header does record source kernel identity+sha256, compiler version/settings, coverage, conventions, and names 'JPL DE440s SPK kernel' (Caltech/JPL) as the only source of position data, but it has no explicit producer name-and-institution field (only 'zodiacs precision compiler' as compiler.name) and no literature citation for DE440; the NAIF modified-kernel attribution-replacement obligation is therefore not met by the header as it stands.
- claim: RIGHTS.md states 'Every pack header carries the obligations anyway: source kernel identity and hash, compiler version and settings, coverage, conventions, and the producer named as Zodiacs rather than JPL, with the DE440 reference cited as the source of the underlying data.' The compiled header has no producer field, no DE440 citation (Park et al., doi:10.3847/1538-3881/abd414), no attribution-replacement text and no reference to the kernel's own NIO2SPK/author comment block. NAIF's modified-kernel rule (quoted in RIGHTS.md) requires replacing the producer attribution with the modifier's name and institution, annotating and renaming; the header satisfies only the annotate/rename part.
- evidence: grep of the 16,187-byte header of the compiled D.zeph for 'Park','DOI','10.3847','producer','Zodiacs','attribution','NIO2SPK','Folkner','DE440 ' — all false; top-level keys: format,formatVersion,candidate,compiler,input,settings,coverage,conventions,derived,dependencies,bodies,payloadSha256,payloadEndOffset. Artifact: scratch/audit/data-toolchain-packaging/header-attribution.txt. RIGHTS.md §'What follows operationally' bullet 2; compile.mjs:499-537 (header construction), :591-616 (dependencies()).
- impact: A documented compliance claim is false. If packs are ever distributed (finding 11's path), the header as-is does not meet the conditions the project itself identified as the conservative course.
- fix: Add a `provenance` block: producer {name:'Zodiacs', institution, contact}, sourceEphemeris {name:'JPL DE440', citation, doi, kernelFile, kernelSha256, kernelCommentExcerpt}, modification {what:'Chebyshev refit and quantisation', compilerVersion}, attributionNote quoting the NAIF rule. Bump COMPILER_VERSION to 1.2.0 and demonstrate payload digest unchanged as METADATA-CORRECTION.md did. Correct RIGHTS.md until then.
- verifier: Re-grepped the 16,187-byte header of the auditor's D.zeph (header-grep.out). Absent, as claimed: 'producer', 'institution', 'Park', 'DOI', '10.3847', 'attribution', 'NIO2SPK', 'Folkner'. But the auditor's greps for 'Zodiacs' (capitalised) and 'DE440 ' (trailing space) were false negatives: the header contains compiler.name 'zodiacs precision compiler', input {file:'de440s.bsp', sha256:c1c7feea...}, conventions.frame '(DE440s segment frame id 1)', and dependencies[0] {what:'JPL DE440s SPK kernel', role:'the only source of position data', licence:'...Produced by Caltech/JPL under contract to NASA; NAIF grants permission...see RIGHTS.md'}. Against RIGHTS.md:139-142's five-part sentence, 'source kernel identity and hash', 'compiler version and settings', 'coverage', 'conventions' are all present, and 'the DE440 reference cited as the source of the underlying data' is met in the sense of naming the DE440s kernel (not the Park et al. paper); only 'the producer named as Zodiacs' has no dedicated field — and NAIF's rule (RIGHTS.md:34-37) asks for name AND institution of the last modifier, which the header lacks. So the RIGHTS.md sentence is partly overstated, not false as titled; the auditor's 'no DE440 citation' is wrong. Nothing is distributed, so the compliance consequence is conditional.

## data-toolchain-packaging-6 — Tree commits ~20,000 per-instant Swiss output values while stating bulk per-instant values are not committed
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: METADATA-CORRECTION.md says Swiss output is 'treated as a measurement, not redistributed in bulk' and 'bulk per-instant values are not committed'; LICENSING.md says 'Nothing Swiss is committed'. Yet docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/raw/t1-swiss.json (380,798 B, ~7,856 decimal values) and numerics/verify/v1-swiss.json (570,916 B, ~11,780 values) are raw Swiss dumps, and src/lib/engine/fixtures/swiss-node-polar.fixture.json (184 values) and swiss-eight-cases.fixture.json (164 values) are Swiss outputs used as test oracles. The legal exposure is probably small (program output), but the project's own statement is inaccurate.
- evidence: stat/grep counts in transcript; docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/METADATA-CORRECTION.md 'The rights picture, by artifact' table and second bullet; docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/LICENSING.md:15-19.
- impact: Inconsistency between stated and actual licensing posture; a reviewer checking the claim finds it false, which weakens the (otherwise careful) rights record.
- fix: Either amend the sentence to 'Swiss outputs are committed only as frozen measurement fixtures and never as fitting targets' and list the files, or move the two raw dumps to an artifact store and keep only their digests in-tree.

## data-toolchain-packaging-7 — Cross-runtime bit-identity holds for the partitioned path only; the released reducer relies on Math.sin/cos/atan2
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: The tree's bit-identical claim (Node/Chromium/Firefox, one digest-verified file) is for the domain plan/partitioned search, which uses trig.mjs (Cody-Waite + Taylor, +,-,* only, ABS_ERR=4e-15 justified term by term and measured at 1.48e-16 over 40,656 arguments), sqrt-of-squares instead of Math.hypot, cosine comparisons rather than acos, and a key built from decimal strings and FNV hashes. The released `apparent()` reducer, the nutation series, the R1/R2/R3 frame matrices and the two released validated modes' projection weights still call Math.sin/Math.cos/Math.atan2 (implementation-defined in ECMAScript); agreement there is observed on V8/SpiderMonkey, not guaranteed, as frames.mjs:130-133 admits. Interval widening (PAD = 8·u) covers missing directed rounding; sinCosInterval pads by ABS_ERR; both justified in comments. No remaining Math.hypot in src/core.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/src/core/frames.mjs:33-35,114-115,138-140; nutation.mjs:84-123; validated-search.mjs:168-169; reduce.mjs:152 (Math.sin in TDB-TT); retarded-search.mjs:572-582 (explicitly keeps Math.sin in released modes); trig.mjs header and :46-51; interval.mjs:19-20 (PAD); domain-partition.mjs:142-161 (partitionKey), :196-209 (FNV fingerprint); docs/platform/evidence/precision-partition/cross-runtime/README.md.
- impact: A chart produced by the released reducer on one browser is not guaranteed bit-identical on another; only the experimental partitioned search is. Swiss (C, libm) has the same weakness, so this is parity, not a deficit — but the tree's headline determinism claim must be read as scoped.
- fix: Route frames.mjs rotations and nutation.mjs through trig.mjs (angles are reduced to |x|≤8 already or reducible in arcseconds), replace eclipticLonLatDeg's atan2 with a documented two-argument arctangent built from a correctly-rounded primitive or accept and document the last-ulp scope; re-pin the released mode roots when doing so.

## data-toolchain-packaging-8 — Receipt CONVENTIONS strings are truthful but 'apparent' omits deflection and the receipt does not identify the ephemeris model by default
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: receipt.js CONVENTIONS: planetPositions 'apparent-geocentric-ecliptic-of-date' — GeoVector(body,time,true) applies light-time and aberration (astronomy.js:3843-3855 BackdatePosition) but no gravitational deflection, so 'apparent' is overstated by ≤0.1″ beyond 5° elongation and up to 1.75″ at the limb; moonPosition 'astronomy-engine-ecliptic-geo-moon' honestly names the implementation (no light-time/aberration — conceded in CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md); moonNodes 'instantaneous-geocentric-moon-orbit-plane' matches trueNodeLongitude (GeoMoonState angular momentum rotated by Rotation_EQJ_ECT); angles 'gast-and-mean-obliquity' matches computeChart (SiderealTime = GAST, meanObliquity) and records an internal inconsistency (apparent sidereal time paired with mean rather than true obliquity; Swiss pairs GAST with true obliquity); speed 'central-difference-plus-minus-0.25-day' matches longitudeSpeed. Gap: receipt.provenance.ephemeris {name:'astronomy-engine', version} is optional caller-supplied (receipt.js validateProvenance), so a receipt alone does not pin the positional model; two engines with the same ENGINE_VERSION but different astronomy-engine minor versions would emit indistinguishable receipts.
- evidence: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/receipt.js CONVENTIONS/COVERAGE blocks and validateProvenance; dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:35-41,43-46,47-60,66-75,113-122; node_modules/astronomy-engine/esm/astronomy.js:3843 GeoVector, :2781-2789 EclipticGeoMoon doc, :1881 SiderealTime (GAST).
- impact: Small semantic overclaim; the larger issue for a Swiss-class engine is that the receipt cannot prove which ephemeris model produced it unless the caller volunteers it.
- fix: Rename to 'aberrated-geocentric-ecliptic-of-date;no-deflection' or add deflection; make `ephemeris: {name, version}` a required CONVENTIONS-adjacent field populated by the engine from its own dependency at build time (tsup define), and add `moonPosition: 'no-light-time;no-aberration'` qualifier text.

## data-toolchain-packaging-9 — Container validates geometry but not numerical conventions; a pack declaring a different Chebyshev convention decodes silently wrong
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: container.mjs validates magic, extents, widths, offsets, coverage monotonicity and EMRAT plausibility, but `conventions.chebyshev` (c0 not halved), `conventions.units` and `formatVersion` are free text/unchecked (validateConstants only inspects emrat and that conventions is an object). The evaluator (ephemeris.mjs:122-140) always assumes SPK type-2 convention in km and km/s. A pack from a variant compiler that halves c0 or stores AU would pass parse+digest and return wrong positions with no error. Unconfirmed by execution; confirmed by code reading.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/src/core/container.mjs:281-292 (validateConstants), :128-130; ephemeris.mjs:122-140; compile.mjs:521-528 (conventions written as prose).
- impact: Integrity ≠ interpretation: the digest proves bytes, not that the runtime reads them under the compiler's convention. Low probability today (one compiler), but it is a latent silent-wrong-answer path.
- fix: Pin `formatVersion` to an allowed set and require `conventions.chebyshev === 'spk2-c0-unhalved'` and `conventions.units.position === 'km'` as enumerated tokens (compiler writes tokens, prose moves to a `notes` field); refuse otherwise with 'unsupported-version'.

## data-toolchain-packaging-12 — NOTICE gaps: shipped ERFA-derived vectors and verbatim ERFA C sources are not named; no JPL attribution text for user-compiled packs; site root has no LICENSE/NOTICE
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: examples/precision-alpha/NOTICE.md names the ERFA-derived nutation series, precession recipe and the astronomy-engine comparator, but not test/tier-a/_erfa-ld-vectors.json (358 eraLd output vectors, shipped via files:'test/tier-a/'), nor tools/measure/erfa-ab.c.pinned and tools/measure/erfa-deflection/{ld.c,ldsun.c,ab.c,ld_body.h} (verbatim BSD-3 ERFA C in the repo; PROVENANCE.md covers them but NOTICE does not). NOTICE.md carries no JPL/DE440 attribution sentence a user could copy into a pack they compile. The site repository root has no LICENSE or NOTICE file; the astronomy-engine MIT notice exists only inside the vendored tarball and at public/sdk/engine/media/NOTICE; whether the minified browser bundle retains the MIT header was not examined.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/NOTICE.md; package.json:44-63 files; test/tier-a/_erfa-ld-vectors.json head ('eraLd outputs from the pinned liberfa source'); tools/measure/erfa-deflection/PROVENANCE.md; `ls` of repo root shows no LICENSE*/NOTICE*; public/sdk/engine/media/NOTICE exists.
- impact: Compliance hygiene; ERFA clause 2 is met by LICENSE-erfa shipping, but the NOTICE inventory is incomplete and a pack producer has no ready attribution text.
- fix: Extend NOTICE.md with the ERFA test-vector and pinned-source entries and a 'If you compile a pack' JPL/DE440 attribution block; add a root THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md; add a check that the built browser chunk containing astronomy-engine retains its MIT comment (esbuild legalComments).

## data-toolchain-packaging-13 — Documentation drift in the shipped compile guide and frozen compiler RESULTS

Compile time '~20 seconds' vs 24.5 s measured is fine.

## production-event-search-5 — Two crossing solvers ship with different semantics: package includes an exact lower endpoint and throws on a 10,000-sample budget; the site's copy does neither
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 1 d
- corrected claim: The site (src/lib/engine/longitude-crossings.ts) and @zodiacs/engine ship two crossing solvers with different, but each documented, contracts: the package includes an exact root at `from`, drops an exact-zero sample not confirmed by a later sign change, and throws RangeError above 10,000 samples (all stated in dist/index.d.ts:21-27); the site excludes the lower endpoint, emits exact samples immediately and has no budget (stated by longitude-crossings.test.ts:19-31). No sign-change crossing differs between them; the only practically reachable divergence is that SDK users cannot run scans the site performs (e.g. Moon at the 0.25-day step beyond ~6.8 years, or transit-scan-core's Moon path). docs/engine-validation/README.md:23 ('the site runs the same package') is true for positions (full.ts takes them from @zodiacs/engine/internal) and imprecise only about the solver.
- claim: The site does not use @zodiacs/engine's findLongitudeCrossings; src/lib/engine/longitude-crossings.ts is a separate implementation. They differ observably: (a) an exact root at `from` is returned by the package and excluded by the site; (b) the package throws RangeError once ceil((to−from)/step)+1 or the running sample count exceeds 10,000 (a Moon scan at the 0.25-day step over ~2000+ days), the site has no budget; (c) the package's pendingExact logic drops an exact-zero sample not confirmed by a later sign change, the site emits it immediately. package-integration.test.ts pins versions, not solver parity, and docs/engine-validation/README.md says 'the site runs the same package'.
- evidence: out/s2-crossings.json: Sun target = bodyLongitude('Sun', 2026-03-01T00:00Z), window [03-01, 03-03], step 1 → package ['2026-03-01T00:00:00.000Z'], site [] (siteSameInputs.lowerEndpointExact). budget: package Moon 0° over 2100/2400/2600 days at step 0.25 → 'Crossing search exceeds the 10,000-sample budget.' each time; Saturn 1900–2100 at step 5 → same throw; site Moon 2600 days → 95 crossings, no budget. Code: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/index.js:56-112 (MAX_CROSSING_SAMPLES, pendingExact) vs src/lib/engine/longitude-crossings.ts:23-70. Test coverage of exact endpoints exists only for the site copy (longitude-crossings.test.ts:19-31).
- impact: SDK consumers reproducing a site result get a different crossing list or an exception on inputs the site accepts; an SDK transit scanner at the Moon step cannot cover more than ~5.5 years. Swiss has one implementation; a stack claiming to exceed it cannot ship two that disagree.
- fix: Make the site import the package solver (or vice-versa) and delete the copy; decide the interval convention ((from, to] per the site tests) and encode it in the package; replace the hard throw with a documented budget parameter and a typed 'refused' result; add a parity test running both on the s2 cases.
- verifier: s2 rerun byte-identical: package Sun exact-at-from returns ['2026-03-01T00:00:00.000Z'], site returns []; package throws 'Crossing search exceeds the 10,000-sample budget.' for Moon 0° at 0.25 d over 2100/2400/2600 days and Saturn 1900-2100 at 5 d; site Moon 2600 d returns 95 crossings. Code confirmed at dist/index.js:44-112 (MAX_CROSSING_SAMPLES, pendingExact) and longitude-crossings.ts:23-70. But the package's index.d.ts:21-27 documents the budget, the boundary-root inclusion and the tangency drop as its contract, so these are designed semantics, not defects; s3 shows identical pass counts in both copies for all nine real Saturn cases; grep of src/ finds no import of saturnReturn or findLongitudeCrossings from '@zodiacs/engine' (site imports natalChart/ENGINE_VERSION only). No wrong answer is produced by either copy, so the rules' 'major' is not met; it is a real consistency/documentation defect.

## production-event-search-6 — Transit-window budgets are 25–100× the measured residual and mark 14% of windows uncertain that Swiss would state plainly
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 2 d
- corrected claim: transit-window-core.ts:265 applies declared budgets of 0.10°/0.15°/0.20° (documented in docs/engine-validation/transit-windows/README.md:46-48 as moving 0.05° plus natal 0.05°/0.10°/0.15°); on ten real charts over 2026-2027 they label 88/641 windows (13.7%) membership-uncertain and 21 (3.3%) exact-uncertain, while a 0.003° budget labels none and the exact pass count (550) is unchanged at every budget. This is a conservative, documented calibration choice — the labels are honest hedges, not wrong answers — and the tree's own JPL comparison (methodology page: p95 12.1", max 18.6") puts the 0.05° model term at ~10x p95 rather than the 25-100x the finding derives from engine-vs-Swiss residuals. Re-deriving per-body budgets under a new contract version (RESULTS.md:75-83 forbids shrinking the tolerance retroactively) would improve decisiveness; it is not an accuracy gap below Swiss.
- claim: transit-window-core.ts fixes angular budgets of 0.10° (planets), 0.15° (angles), 0.20° (Moon) (line 265) and labels a window membership-uncertain or exact-uncertain when a turning point or threshold lies inside that allowance. The measured engine-vs-Swiss slow-planet residual is 3–15" (0.001–0.004°), so the labels are dominated by budget headroom, not by any measured error; users receive 'uncertain' verdicts a Swiss user would never see, and the Uranus D contract stays failed for the same reason.
- evidence: out/s6-window-uncertainty.json (12.4 s): ten real charts (1955–2010 births, Placidus, 40.7N) scanned 2026-01-01..2027-12-31 with default options → 641 windows: membershipStatus uncertain 88 (13.7%), exactTopologyStatus uncertain 21 (3.3%), peak.kind 'uncertain' 21. Same charts with angularBudgetDegrees 0.01° → 6 and 2; 0.003° → 0 and 0; 0.001° → 0 and 0; exactPassesTotal 550 in every run (no pass appears or disappears). Measured residuals: RESULTS.md §2 core−Swiss Uranus max 3.45" (0.00096°), Neptune median 11.5" (docs/engine-validation/README.md); RESULTS.md ladder: Uranus D certifies 2 passes at ε ≤ 0.044°. Code: src/lib/engine/transit-window-core.ts:265,280,294-297.
- impact: Decisiveness is a capability: Swiss-based software gives exact-pass counts; this product hedges 1 window in 7 on a budget it never measured. The residuals show the hedging is unnecessary at ~10× margin.
- fix: Replace the fixed budgets with per-body budgets derived from committed measurements (e.g., 3× the per-body p95 of docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark plus the natal term), record the derivation in docs/engine-validation/transit-windows/README.md, re-run the A–I fixtures with the new contract version (the frozen v2/v3 failure stays recorded), and when a DE-backed backend lands, drop to ε≈0.001° as RESULTS.md §6 already demonstrates.
- verifier: s6 rerun byte-identical (641 windows; production budgets 88 membership-uncertain, 21 exact-uncertain, 21 peak.kind 'uncertain'; 0.01° -> 6/2; 0.003° and 0.001° -> 0/0; exactPassesTotal 550 in every run; 12.4 s). transit-window-core.ts:257 accepts angularBudgetDegrees <= 0.2 and :265 sets the defaults as cited. RESULTS.md:10-12 confirms the Uranus D contract is failed-incomplete because its 0.044188° witness sits under the 0.05° budget, and :75-83 states the policy against post-hoc tolerance shrinking. transit-windows README:46-48 documents the budget composition. The finding's residual reference (Swiss, an instrument) is not the tree's error measurement; no shipped answer is wrong, so the severity rules give minor.

## production-event-search-9 — build-transits.mjs daily aspect scan claims 'Daily samples catch every crossing'; near a Mercury station a 74" band is blind
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: scripts/build-transits.mjs:166-211 scans fast×slow and slow×slow exact aspects at one-day steps with a plain sign-change test and asserts completeness in its comment. Two crossings inside one day around a Mercury/Venus/Mars station cancel and are dropped.
- evidence: out/s11-transits-blind.json: Mercury station 2026-02-26T06:47:01Z curvature −0.164 °/day² → blind dip 0.0205° (73.8") for a daily scan, 0.13" for hourly; Venus 18.3"; Mars 6.0". With 40 slow-planet aspect targets the per-Mercury-station chance of a silently dropped pair is ≈4.6e-3 (band 2·dip/360 × 40). Ingresses in the same script already use hourly brackets for exactly this reason (lines 105-107), so the inconsistency is internal.
- impact: Roughly one dropped exact Mercury aspect pair per few hundred station months in the horoscope event feed; unflagged.
- fix: Scan aspects hourly (12 pairs × 8 targets × 720 samples/month is trivial) or split each fast body's scan at its stations as transit-scan-core.ts does; delete the completeness comment or replace it with the measured blind band.

## production-event-search-10 — Saturn natalRetrograde uses a ±1-day difference while the chart uses ±6 h; they disagree inside a 40 s window around a station
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: returns.ts:83-87 (and the package computeSaturnReturns) derives natal direction from bodyLongitude(±1 day)/2, whereas computeBodies uses longitudeSpeed(±0.25 day). Neither is the analytic derivative; the two zero-crossings differ, so a birth at a Saturn station gets 'retrograde' from saturnReturns and 'direct' from its own chart.
- evidence: out/s4-natal-speed.json: Saturn longitude maximum 2026-07-26T19:57:40.372Z; ±1-day difference zero at 19:56:57.058Z, ±6-hour zero at 19:57:36.921Z, disagreement window 39.9 s; a birth at 19:57:16.990Z gives chart retrograde=false (speed 3.9e-7 °/day) but saturnReturns.natalRetrograde=true in both the site and the package.
- impact: Negligible in frequency (40 s per 4.5 months), but it is one product contradicting itself, and a stationary natal Saturn is exactly the case a careful user checks.
- fix: Compute natalRetrograde from the same longitudeSpeed the chart uses, or better from an analytic/central-difference derivative with a declared stationary deadband ('stationary' as a third state, as the eight-case policy's 0.02°/day deadband already implies).

## production-event-search-11 — Return and year scans compute far outside the declared 1800–2199 range without error or flag
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: saturnReturns/yearScan scan to birth+92 years with no range check; lunar-return.ts and transit-window-core.ts enforce 1800–2199 but returns.ts, solar-return.ts and year-scan.ts do not, so a 2190 birth is scanned to 2282 and a year-900 birth to 992, past astronomy-engine's validated span and on an extrapolated Delta-T, with results presented like any other.
- evidence: out/s4-natal-speed.json tail: birth 2150-01-01 → seasons at 2179/2208/2237, scan ends 2242-01-02, threw=false; birth 2190-06-01 → 2219/2248/2278, scan ends 2282-06-02; birth 0900-01-01 → 929/958/988, threw=false. docs/engine-validation/README.md §1 concedes natalChart bounds nothing; the return scanners are not mentioned.
- impact: Users can obtain Saturn-return dates for 2280 with the same presentation as 2030; Swiss refuses outside its file range and says so.
- fix: Apply the same 1800–2199 (or the eventual DE-backed pack) range check to returns.ts, solar-return.ts and year-scan.ts, clipping the scan window and setting a 'range-clipped' flag the UI renders.

## production-event-search-12 — Yearly sky/eclipse/ingress/birthday data have no refresh automation or coverage check
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Only build-transits.mjs has a cron (transits-monthly.yml). eclipses.json and sky.json (generated 2026-07-20, horizon 2031-01-01), ingresses.json (2026-07-06; fast planets to 2029-07-01), aura-moon-ingresses.json (2026-07-15, to 2029-01-01) and birthdays.json (NOW_YEAR hard-coded 2026) are manual yearly regenerations with no CI check that the display window is still covered.
- evidence: .github/workflows: only transits-monthly.yml references a data builder (cron '41 5 25 * *'); src/data/{eclipses,sky,ingresses,aura-moon-ingresses}.json generatedAt/from/to as listed; scripts/build-birthdays.mjs:28 NOW_YEAR = 2026.
- impact: Not an accuracy defect today; a missed yearly run silently empties the eclipse/retrograde pages in 2031 and leaves birthday receipts on 2026.
- fix: Add a yearly workflow (or extend transits-monthly.yml) that regenerates and PRs the yearly data each July, plus a site-check assertion that each dataset's `to` exceeds today + 12 months.

## production-positions-7 — Nutation keeps 5 of the 77 IAU 2000B luni-solar terms: 0.2685" max longitude error (1900-2100), present in every shipped longitude
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: astronomy.js iau2000b evaluates only the five largest terms (Omega, 2(F-D+Omega), 2(F+Omega), 2Omega, l'). Against the published 77-term series (alpha's nut00b) over 73 050 daily TT instants 1900-2100: max |dpsi| error 0.2685" (JD 2452355.5), mean 0.061", max |deps| error 0.086". Against Swiss's nutation in 2025: p50 0.054", p95 0.167", max 0.216". The shipped chunk applies this via Rotation_EQJ_ECT (planets, node) and e_tilt/nutation (Moon), so the error enters every longitude 1:1; the alpha's replica nutAstronomyEngine matches the real astronomy-engine to 0 (verified). Residual-subtraction on shipped output is inconclusive because series errors dominate (p50 moves < 0.05"), so confirmation is by code path plus the measured dpsi gap.
- evidence: astronomy.js:1316-1353 (iau2000b), :1363 (e_tilt), :6638 (Rotation_EQJ_ECT), :2855 (EclipticGeoMoon nutation call); nutation-series.json; compare.json key T2_dpsi_swiss_minus_ae_arcsec_daily2025; committed docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/README.md:35 (0.2677").
- impact: A 0.27" floor under every body and the angles; Swiss uses the full model. Becomes the dominant error once the series are replaced.
- fix: Use examples/precision-alpha/src/core/nutation.mjs (77-term 2000B, or 2000A for a further 0.0025") in the of-date rotation and in the true obliquity; if astronomy-engine stays, wrap its rotations rather than calling Rotation_EQJ_ECT.

## production-positions-8 — Gravitational light deflection absent: 1.14" measured at Jupiter's 2025 conjunction; receipt's 'apparent' label is therefore not the IAU apparent place
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: Swiss apparent minus Swiss NOGDEFL over 3285 body-days in 2025: max -1.140" (Jupiter 2025-06-25, elongation 0.26 deg), +0.758" (Jupiter 06-24), 0.467" (Uranus 05-17, 0.89 deg); 116 body-days (3.5%) >= 0.05", 35 >= 0.1", 2 >= 0.5". The engine tracks the NOGDEFL variant (Jupiter residual stays at its -1.5..-1.7" series bias vs NOGDEFL while swinging -0.48..-2.31" vs apparent), confirming absence rather than assuming it. Swiss's speed includes the deflection derivative near conjunction (up to 8.57"/day for Uranus 2025-05-18, 1.83"/day Jupiter), which the engine lacks. The receipt declares planetPositions 'apparent-geocentric-ecliptic-of-date' although deflection is missing and nutation truncated. Committed CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md:19 states 32.87% of days 1900-2100 have a body inside the 5-degree floor.
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js eclipticOfDate (GeoVector(body,time,true) only); receipt.js:140; compare.json key T4_deflection_daily2025 and T5 Jupiter/Uranus max rows.
- impact: Up to 1.7" at the solar limb, ~0.1-1" for a few days around every superior conjunction; Swiss applies it by default.
- fix: Port examples/precision-alpha/src/core/deflection.mjs (validated against ERFA) into the planet chain; reword the receipt to enumerate the corrections actually applied.

## production-positions-9 — +-6 h central-difference speed is off by up to 7.5"/day for the Moon; station times differ from Swiss by up to 38 min but the derivative contributes at most 1.1 min
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: Daily 2025, TT pinned: shipped speed minus Swiss FLG_SPEED for the Moon p50 1.80"/day, p95 5.33"/day, max 8.72"/day, of which the pure discretisation error (shipped minus h=0.001 d derivative of the same longitude) is p50 1.79, p95 5.25, max 7.47"/day; Mercury discretisation up to 1.70"/day near perihelion; outer planets < 0.005"/day. Retrograde flag: 0 disagreements with Swiss in 3650 body-days. Over the 54 stations of Mercury..Pluto in 2024-2026 (Swiss speed=0 bisected to 1e-6 d), the shipped station time differs from Swiss by median 0.06 h and max 0.63 h (Pluto 2024-10-12), 0.44 h Neptune, 0.26 h Uranus, <= 0.11 h Mercury/Venus, and the shipped minus tight-derivative station difference is <= 0.018 h; so a wrong retrograde flag is confined to a <= 38 min window whose width is set by the series error, not the derivative. Only longitude speed is produced (no latitude or distance rate; Swiss gives all three).
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js longitudeSpeed (stepDays = 0.25); compare.json keys T5_speed_daily2025_pinnedTT, T5b_retrograde_flag_disagreements_daily2025; stations.json, stations-swiss.json.
- impact: Small today; it becomes the limiting error for the Moon's speed once positions reach 0.1", and speed-dependent products (void-of-course, applying/separating, station lists) inherit it.
- fix: Derive velocities analytically from the Chebyshev state (position and derivative) through the reduction (the alpha already carries derivative enclosures), and emit lon, lat and distance rates; until then shrink the step to ~0.02 d, which removes the Moon error at negligible cost.

## production-positions-10 — No validity span or out-of-range flag: the package computes any year and silently degrades (Moon 303" and Pluto 33" at 2400)
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: computeBodies accepts any Date; astronomy-engine's Pluto table spans years 0-4000 and crawls beyond, VSOP87 and the ILE Moon are evaluated without limit. Measured production residuals vs Swiss at 2399-12-01: Moon 302.6" (20.3" with TT pinned), Pluto -33.2", Venus 26.8", Sun 22.0"; at 1800-02-01: Moon 5.0"/7.6" pinned, Neptune -16.1", Pluto 18.6". No flag is raised and the receipt COVERAGE says only 'finite-reference-cases-only'; the 1800-2199 limit is a site rule. Swiss returns an error or a flagged Moshier fallback outside its file span.
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js computeBodies/computeChart (flags only 'polar-fallback'/'no-time'); receipt.js:148; compare.json key T8_epochs rows epoch-1800, epoch-2200, epoch-2400.
- impact: Callers of the public npm package cannot tell a 0.5" chart from a 300" one; Swiss can.
- fix: Declare a validity span per source (pack coverage, fallback series) in the receipt, add a flag such as 'outside-reference-span' and, for the DE pack, refuse or fall back explicitly outside coverage.

## swiss-parity-3 — The 'DE440 vs Swiss DE431' advantage does not exist and a committed record misattributes the Moon residual to it
- severity: major → minor · status: adjusted · effort: 0.2 d
- corrected claim: docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/RESULTS.md lines 231 and 346-347 name DE431 as the source of Swiss's semo_18.se1; the pinned files (hashes in CONFIGURATION.md, headers dated 2026/05/26) are DE441-based, as the tree's own engine-validation READMEs and fixtures already state. The Moon's epoch-dependent 0.0107 arcsec residual is measured here to be the DE440-vs-DE441 lunar difference (Swiss .se1 agrees with Horizons's DE441 Moon to 0.002 arcsec rms once the frame term is cancelled; the DE440s alpha differs by up to 0.010 arcsec, correlated -0.87 with the .se1-vs-DE440 difference). No tree document asserts an ephemeris-generation advantage over Swiss; the correction is to the one record's parenthetical and hypothesis wording.
- claim: The Swiss .se1 files actually used (sepl_18.se1 sha256 ca1393ce..., semo_18.se1 1ca07bd6..., the same hashes as docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/CONFIGURATION.md) carry the header 'Created for Astrodienst in Switzerland 2026/05/26, based on JPL Ephemeris DE441'. Measured Swiss-.se1 minus Swiss-DE440 (same Swiss code, FLG_JPLEPH on the JPL binary) is max 0.0104 arcsec (Moon), ≤0.0054 arcsec planets. docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/RESULTS.md line 231 states 'Swiss's semo_18.se1 is a fit to DE431' and line 346 keeps the DE431 hypothesis; the artifact says DE441 and the 0.0107 arcsec Moon residual is the DE440-vs-DE441 lunar difference (alpha vs Swiss both on DE440: Moon 0.0025 arcsec).
- evidence: strings <swisslab>/ephe/semo_18.se1 | head -3; results.json perBody['a-b'] and perBody['b-d_kernel']; RESULTS.md quoted lines.
- impact: Any superiority narrative resting on a newer JPL series is unsupported: Swiss's current data files are already DE441. The truthful ephemeris-generation gap is ≤0.01 arcsec.
- fix: Correct RESULTS.md lines 231 and 346 to 'DE441-based .se1; the Moon residual is the DE440/DE441 lunar difference (measured 0.010 arcsec)'; remove 'newer JPL series than Swiss' from any owner-facing claim.
- verifier: Facts confirmed: sha256 sepl_18.se1 = ca1393ceab3a44fb..., semo_18.se1 = 1ca07bd67c24374d..., matching docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/CONFIGURATION.md line 14; both file headers read 'Created for Astrodienst in Switzerland 2026/05/26, based on JPL Ephemeris DE441.'; RESULTS.md line 231 says 'Swiss's semo_18.se1 is a fit to DE431' and 346-347 'The DE431-vs-DE440 hypothesis'. Auditor's a-b (Swiss .se1 vs Swiss on the DE440 JPL binary) Moon max 0.0104 arcsec reproduced from their swiss.json. I went further on attribution using only cached Horizons data (Moon source DE441), cancelling the common IAU76/80 frame residual by subtracting the Sun's residual at each instant: Swiss .se1 Moon vs Horizons DE441 Moon rms 0.0018 / max 0.0042 arcsec, alpha-on-DE440s Moon vs Horizons rms 0.0043 / max 0.0103 arcsec, correlation of (a-b) with the DE440 residual -0.865, and a-b is V-shaped in time (0.0104 at 1851, under 0.001 over 1933-2012, 0.0085 at 2148) - the signature of DE440/DE441 lunar divergence away from the LLR span. So the 0.0107 arcsec Moon residual is the DE440-vs-DE441 difference, now measured rather than asserted. What is wrong is the severity and framing: RESULTS.md itself hedges at lines 232-233 ('a hypothesis consistent with the data, not a proven attribution') and 346-347 ('unexplained... not tested'); the rest of the tree correctly records DE441 (docs/engine-validation/README.md:33-34, swiss-node-polar/README.md:18, src/lib/engine/fixtures/swiss-eight-cases-policy.json:44); and no owner-facing tree document claims a DE440-over-DE431 advantage (FOUR-CONFIGURATIONS.md:36 explicitly disclaims superiority). The refuted 'premise' comes from the audit brief, not from the tree. One wrong factual parenthetical in a hedged evidence record, 0.2 days to fix, nothing shipped or claimed depending on it, is a minor defect by the rules, not a major unsupported claim.

## swiss-parity-6 — Production Moon is not the apparent place its receipt declares: 0.6 arcsec systematic today, 4.2 arcsec at 1851 with the clock pinned
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: receipt conventions declare planetPositions apparent-geocentric-ecliptic-of-date, but moonOfDate() uses EclipticGeoMoon with no light-time or aberration pass (conceded in CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md). Measured with the clock pinned: Moon 0.45-0.71 arcsec from Horizons over 1957-2026 (the omitted Moon-relative light-time/aberration term is ~0.7 arcsec) rising to 2.0-4.25 arcsec over 1851-1933 (the ILE-based series degrading backwards).
- evidence: results.json rows body=Moon, res['c_pinned-e']; chunk-GBH7JIYF.js moonOfDate.
- impact: Small for readings, but the receipt's convention statement is literally false for the Moon and the alpha already does it right (0.0025 arcsec).
- fix: Either apply light-time and aberration to the Moon in the shipped engine or amend the receipt conventions to say the Moon is geometric-of-date; adopting the alpha removes the issue.

## swiss-parity-7 — Speeds are a ±0.25-day central difference; Moon speed differs from Swiss's analytic speed by up to 3.4 arcsec/day
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: longitudeSpeed() differences longitudes at ±0.25 day. Against Swiss FLG_SPEED: Moon max 3.45 arcsec/day (p50 2.24), Mercury 2.12, Venus 0.61, others <0.14 arcsec/day. Impact on station timing is unconfirmed (not measured at a station): 2 arcsec/day of speed error near a Mercury station (acceleration ~0.03 deg/day^2) would move the station by ~30 minutes.
- evidence: zodiacs.json speed vs swiss.json configs.a lonSpeed; per-body table in the transcript ('speed' columns).
- impact: Minor for retrograde flags; matters if speeds are ever used for station timing or exposed as a precision quantity.
- fix: Use the pack's analytic derivative (the alpha already returns state derivatives) or reduce the step to ±0.01 day with a Richardson correction; document the speed convention in the receipt.

## swiss-parity-13 — No topocentric option; the geocentric-only Moon is up to ~1 degree from the observer's sky
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 4 d
- claim: Neither the engine nor the alpha offers topocentric positions, diurnal aberration or parallax (alpha README: 'The observer is the geocentre'). Swiss FLG_TOPOCTR with swe_set_topo gives it, with WGS84 geodesy. Measured at London 2026-09-22 17:00 UT the Moon moves +909 arcsec in longitude and -3180 arcsec in latitude between geocentric and topocentric. Astrologers conventionally use geocentric, so this is a completeness gap, not an error.
- evidence: Transcript (Swiss FLG_TOPOCTR probe); examples/precision-alpha/README.md 'Coordinates' paragraph.
- impact: Needed for local eclipses, occultations, rise/set and parallax-corrected Moon; a place to exceed Swiss with proper geodesy (WGS84 + geoid height + polar motion) at ~0.01 arcsec if done rigorously.
- fix: Add observer geodetic position -> ITRS -> GCRS (Earth rotation angle, polar motion optional) in the alpha reducer; validate against Swiss FLG_TOPOCTR to 0.05 arcsec.

## time-4 — UTC is fed where UT1 is meant (no leap-second or UT1-UTC treatment): up to 0.9 s, 13.5 arcsec RAMC, 44 arcsec in MC/ASC at 66 deg
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: AstroTime.ut is the JS Date's UTC scaled to days and is used as UT1 by era() inside sidereal_time (astronomy.js:1861-1882) and as the argument of DeltaT; there is no leap-second table and no UT1-UTC. Since 1972 |UT1-UTC| <= 0.9 s by definition (Horizons header lines 70-72 say the same), so the neglect is bounded: measured over a 4-date x 5-latitude x 3-longitude grid, a 0.9 s shift moves RAMC by 13.5 arcsec and MC/ASC by up to 44.45 arcsec (latitude 66), the Moon by 0.49 arcsec. Before 1972 civil time was GMT/UT-based, so treating it as UT is correct there. Swiss handles this through swe_utc_to_jd/swe_jdet_to_utc/swe_jdut1_to_utc with a leap-second table (demonstrated: 2016-12-31 23:59:60 accepted; 2026-01-01 UTC gives TT-UT1 = 68.8998 s, implying UT1-UTC = +0.284 s). Unconfirmed against actual IERS UT1-UTC values (not fetched); the figures are the definitional bound.
- evidence: node_modules/astronomy-engine/astronomy.js:1198-1202 (ut from Date.getTime), :1861-1882 (sidereal_time: theta = era(time) from ut; polynomial from tt), node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:125 (SiderealTime(MakeTime(input.utc))). Measurement: audit/time/ramc-sensitivity.txt ('UT1-UTC=0.9 s worst |dMC|,|dASC| arcsec: 44.45'), per-case rows in ramc-sensitivity.json (dRAMC_ut1_arcsec = 13.54 everywhere). Swiss demo output in the transcript (utc_to_jd rows).
- impact: Sub-arcminute but real for angles at high latitude; more importantly it is the missing half of a correct UTC->TT->UT1 chain: with an exact leap-second path TT would be known to microseconds and Delta-T's role would shrink to UT1 alone, which is what Swiss does.
- fix: Implement UTC->TAI (leap-second table, 1972-present, refreshed from IERS Bulletin C via the weekly cron) -> TT = TAI + 32.184 s exactly; then UT1 = UTC + (UT1-UTC) from IERS finals.all (observed 1973-present, 90-day predictions), else 0 with a +-0.9 s band; feed UT1 to sidereal time/ERA and TT to everything dynamical; report both offsets and their bands in the receipt. Share the data pipeline with time-1.

## time-5 — The 'lmt' and 'dst-*' flags are heuristics that misfire both ways
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.75 d
- claim: 'lmt' is set iff the resolved offset has a fractional minute (geo.js:277, localToUtc.ts:180). Whole-minute local mean times are not flagged (Pacific/Guam 1890-06-01 12:00 -> offset +579.0 min, flags []), while legal standard times that happen to carry seconds are flagged as LMT (Asia/Kolkata 1900 HMT +5:53:20 -> ['lmt']; Africa/Monrovia 1960 MMT -0:44:30 -> ['lmt']). 'dst-gap'/'dst-fold' fire for any offset change: the 1883-11-18 US railroad-time cutover in America/New_York yields ['dst-fold','lmt'], and the Samoa/Kwajalein date-line skips yield 'dst-gap'. The receipt validator hard-codes these flag names (receipt.js:183-184), so the mislabels are persisted.
- evidence: audit/time/calendar-edges.txt and the transcript run of the flag probe: 'Pacific/Guam 1890-06-01 12:00 -> ... off 579.0000 flags []'; 'Asia/Kolkata 1900-06-01 12:00 -> off 321.1667 flags ["lmt"]'; 'Africa/Monrovia 1960-06-01 -> off -44.5 flags ["lmt"]'; 'America/New_York 1883-11-18 12:02 -> flags ["dst-fold","lmt"]'; 'Pacific/Apia 2011-12-30 12:00 -> flags ["dst-gap"]' (calendar-edges.json). Code: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/geo.js:262-277; src/lib/time/localToUtc.ts:161-180; receipt.js:183-184 (FLAGS/TIME_FLAGS).
- impact: Users and receipts get the wrong explanation of what happened to their birth time; with the own-tzdb fix (time-2) the correct kind (LMT vs legal, DST vs legal change vs date-line) is available for free.
- fix: Derive flags from the transition record (tzdb type: LMT abbreviation, isdst bit, standard-offset change, calendar/date-line skip) rather than from fractional minutes; rename to 'gap'/'fold' with a `cause` field; keep 'lmt' only for true local mean time. Version the FLAGS list in the receipt schema.

## time-6 — House angles mix an apparent (true-equinox) sidereal time with the mean obliquity, evaluated at UTC centuries
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: computeChart builds angles from SiderealTime (GAST, includes the 5-term equation of the equinoxes) and meanObliquity(centuriesSinceJ2000(input.utc)) (chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:125-128). The UTC-vs-TT argument costs 1.7e-5 arcsec (negligible, measured), but mean-vs-true obliquity is a frame inconsistency of up to the nutation in obliquity (9.2 arcsec): measured over the same grid, switching to astronomy-engine's true obliquity moves the MC by up to 1.28 arcsec and the ASC by up to 128.3 arcsec (66 deg latitude). Swiss swe_houses uses the true obliquity of date with the apparent ARMC. Belongs to the houses area as well; recorded here because it is a time-argument/frame-of-date issue and is not conceded in docs/engine-validation.
- evidence: audit/time/ramc-sensitivity.txt: 'obliquity from UTC centuries instead of TT: worst arcsec: 1.72e-5'; 'mean vs TRUE obliquity in angles: worst dMC 1.28 dASC 128.34'; per-case rows in ramc-sensitivity.json. Code: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:115-117 (centuriesSinceJ2000 from UTC), :125-128; chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:9-13 (meanObliquity); receipt.js:143 (angles: 'gast-and-mean-obliquity').
- impact: Up to ~2 arcmin of ASC at high latitude relative to Swiss's convention purely from the obliquity choice; small at mid-latitudes but it is a systematic convention gap that the receipt currently enshrines.
- fix: Use true obliquity of date (mean + nutation in obliquity from the same nutation model that feeds GAST) for MC/ASC/houses, evaluate the polynomial at TT centuries, and update CONVENTIONS.angles to 'gast-and-true-obliquity'; when the alpha's 77-term IAU 2000B nutation replaces astronomy-engine's 5 terms, feed the same dpsi/deps to both the equation of the equinoxes and the obliquity.

## time-7 — Receipts never carry the tzdb/ICU version or any time-scale facts, and the site and SDK keep two divergent copies of the resolver
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: The receipt schema allows provenance.runtime {name, version, icuVersion, tzdbVersion} but the site passes provenance null and no code path reads process.versions.tz or a browser equivalent (browsers expose none), so a receipt cannot say which tzdb produced its UTC instant; CONVENTIONS has no Delta-T, UT1 or leap-second entry. The site's src/lib/time/localToUtc.ts duplicates the SDK's geo.js resolver with a subtly different offsetAt: the site regex is un-anchored and returns 0 silently on an unparseable name (localToUtc.ts:62-63) where the SDK throws (geo.js:194-197); both silently treat a missing timeZoneName part as 'GMT' (offset 0). Node 22 has no Temporal, so createNativeTemporalTransitionProvider returns null and local-date-intervals is 'provider-unavailable' in every Node run.
- evidence: node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/receipt.js:137-147, 454-457; rg 'tzdbVersion|icuVersion' over src/ and scripts/ returns nothing outside the validator; src/lib/engine/portable.test.ts:49 (provenance: null); src/lib/time/localToUtc.ts:57-69 vs node_modules/@zodiacs/engine/dist/geo.js:189-199; src/lib/time/local-date-intervals.ts:57-67 with `typeof globalThis.Temporal` = 'undefined' on node v22.22.2 (audit/time/tz-scan-report.json host block: node v22.22.2, icu 78.2, tz 2025c).
- impact: A receipt that cannot name its tzdb or Delta-T cannot be re-verified after the host updates, which undercuts the receipt's whole purpose; two resolvers invite drift.
- fix: Make the SDK the single resolver (site imports it); with the own-tzdb table (time-2) stamp tzdbVersion/dataForm into every receipt; add conventions.timeScale {deltaTModel, deltaTSeconds, deltaTSigma, ut1MinusUtc, leapSeconds}; anchor the regex and fail closed on a missing timeZoneName in both copies until consolidated.

## time-8 — Adapter contract states the UTC->TT split backwards
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: examples/precision-alpha/CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md:157 says 'UTC -> TT. Leap seconds before 1972, Delta-T after.' Leap seconds exist only from 1972; before 1972 (and before 1961's rubber-second UTC) civil time was UT and only Delta-T applies; after 1972 UTC->TT is exactly TAI+32.184 via the leap-second count, and Delta-T is needed only for UT1. The sentence, if implemented as written, would build the wrong chain.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md:157-158.
- impact: Documentation-only, but it is the specification a future chart adapter is told to follow.
- fix: Rewrite: 'UTC -> TT: after 1972-01-01 exact via the leap-second table (TT = UTC + (TAI-UTC) + 32.184 s); before that treat civil time as UT1 and apply Delta-T. UT1 (for sidereal time) = UTC + (UT1-UTC) from IERS, |value| <= 0.9 s.'

## verification-honesty-6 — Claims audit: four figures have no committed receipt; one transcribed figure is now reproduced
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: (a) The node/polar measured maxima 0.001685457 deg and 0.000434825 deg appear only as prose in docs/engine-validation/README.md and swiss-node-polar/README.md; no run output is committed. (b) CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md:19,244 states 32.87 per cent of days 1900-2100 (24,014 of 73,050) have a body inside the 5-degree floor; no data file or script output backs it. (c) The Swiss Delta-T 93.18 s at 2100 was transcribed (conceded); this audit reproduces it as 93.182 s with pyswisseph 2.10.03, so the concession can be closed with a receipt. (d) ACCEPTANCE.md F1 says '125 tests' tier A; the suite is 423 today (dated 2026-09-20, so stale rather than false). (e) methodology 'Moon median 1.1 arcseconds' checks out: 1.093 arcsec over 16 non-future Moon rows, 1.162 over all 18 in report-measure.json. (f) methodology 1.6/12.1/18.6 arcsec and 64.8/159.4 match the committed benchmark.
- evidence: grep -rln '0.000434825|0.001685457' docs src -> only the two READMEs; grep '32.87' -> CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md only; swiss-anchors.json swiss_deltat_seconds['2100-01-01'] = 93.182; alpha-copy-baseline.log 423 pass; report-measure.json byBody.Moon p50 1.162, rows filtered non-future median 1.093 (computed in shell, see transcript command).
- impact: Small individually, but the tree's credibility rests on 'every figure has a receipt'; two figures used to motivate architecture (the 32.87 per cent floor occupancy and the polar maxima) do not.
- fix: Commit the raw comparison JSON for the node/polar run (or re-run and commit), a floor-occupancy.json plus the script that produced 24,014/73,050, a swiss-deltat-receipt.json (93.182 s with binding version), and refresh the F1 count with a date.

## verification-honesty-7 — Pre-registration ordering of the Swiss packs is self-reported, not verifiable from history
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: The policies say declaredOnUTC 2026-09-05 and the fixtures say retrievedAtUTC 2026-09-05T12:26 and generatedAtUTC 2026-09-05T12:33; policy and fixture files for node-polar and eight-cases were all first committed in the same commit at 2026-09-07T09:31:45Z. The ordering 'policy before comparison' therefore rests on timestamps written by the same process that wrote the fixtures. All nine committed sha256 digests do recompute correctly.
- evidence: git log --diff-filter=A on src/lib/engine/fixtures/*.json (transcript); receipts/acquisition.json retrievedAtUTC values; sha256sum output matches engine.test.ts:48-50,106-108, lunar-return.test.ts:102-108, transit-window-independent.test.ts:65.
- impact: Does not change any number; it limits what a sceptical reader can conclude from 'declared before comparison'.
- fix: For future packs commit the policy in its own commit (or a signed tag) before acquisition; note the limitation in the READMEs for the existing ones.

## verification-honesty-8 — Moon speed from a +/-0.25 day central difference is 2-6 arcsec/day slow near perigee and untested
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Around the 2024-10-17 perigee the engine's Moon speed is 2.10 to 6.22 arcsec/day below Swiss's analytic speed at every 6-hour sample (max -6.22 arcsec/day at 15.21 deg/day), the sign and size of the h^2/6 truncation of a central difference with h = 0.25 day; longitude itself differs by 2.0-3.4 arcsec at the same instants. No test asserts a Moon speed against any reference.
- evidence: probes.json moonSpeed (engine positions()), swiss-probes.log Moon block.
- impact: Speed feeds applying/separating and retrograde flags and is printed in receipts; the bias is below Swiss. Cheap to remove because GeoMoonState already carries the velocity.
- fix: Use the analytic velocity for the Moon (and GeoVector differences at h = 0.01 d or state vectors for planets), then add frozen Swiss speed rows at perigee/apogee with a 1 arcsec/day gate.

## verification-honesty-9 — Placidus between 60 and 66 degrees is untested; residual vs Swiss grows to 9.2 arcsec with no flag before the hard 66-degree cut
- severity: minor · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: At 60.5, 63.0 and 65.9 N (three instants each) the engine returns Placidus with flags [] and cusps differing from swe.houses_ex 'P' by up to 3.9, 7.0 and 9.2 arcsec (cusp 3), ASC by up to 2.4 arcsec, MC 1.5 arcsec; the in-suite Swiss vectors stop at 60.17 N (Helsinki) and the gate is 720 arcsec. At 66.01 the same request silently becomes whole sign with polar-fallback, where Swiss keeps computing until its own failure. At exactly +/-90 the engine returns ASC 0.0000 deg with only the generic polar-fallback flag.
- evidence: probes.json placidus and pole_90/pole_-90; swiss-probes.log Placidus block.
- impact: Below Swiss in a populated band (Scandinavia, Alaska, Iceland) and unverified there; the degenerate pole answer is defensible geometry but carries no distinct flag.
- fix: Add fixture rows at 60.5/63/65.9 with a 15 arcsec cusp gate; add a 'placidus-near-limit' flag above 60; add an 'ascendant-degenerate' flag at |lat| > 89.99.

## alpha-reduction-math-5 — Reduction order and conventions match the ERFA apparent-place chain to 4.4e-7 arcsec (planets) with only the documented Klioner term missing
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Order is BCRS geometric -> Newtonian light-time (target at emission, barycentric observer at reception; reduce.mjs:215-229) -> eraLd with e = observer - Sun(reception), em at reception, q = target(emission) - Sun(emission) (reduce.mjs:237-254, the eraLd argument semantics) -> eraAb form with the observer's BARYCENTRIC velocity (EMB + Earth-rel-EMB analytic derivative, reduce.mjs:209-213) and bm1 = sqrt(1-v^2) but without ERFA's w2 = SRS/s potential term -> frame bias + IAU 2006 precession + IAU 2000B/P03 nutation as one fw2m matrix whose gamb/psib carry the bias once (frames.mjs:100-110) -> ecliptic via epsA + deps. Against an independent pyerfa + jplephem chain over the same DE440s (200 epochs x 10 bodies) the max separation is 4.37e-7" (Sun), 4.17-5.02e-7" (planets) and 1.34e-5" (Moon); the planetary constant is the omitted w2 term (ERFA bounds it at ~0.4 uas), and the Moon figure is rounding noise from EMB cancellation (finding 8), with light-time agreeing to 8e-9 s and distances to 2.7e-5 km. The frame bias direction is verified by the matrix match to eraFw2m(pfw06 + nut00b/P03) at 2.2e-16 and to eraPnm06a at 1.0e-8 rad (= the 2000A/2000B difference).
- evidence: S/erfa-chain/dump-alpha.mjs (Reducer over tools/spk-backend.mjs, raw DE440s), S/erfa-chain/erfa-chain.py -> erfa-chain-results.json (2.3 s). Reader agreement Spk2 vs jplephem: Moon-rel-EMB 4.7e-7 km, EMB 1.4e-5 km, Sun 6.8e-9 km (S/erfa-chain/dump-spk2.mjs).
- impact: The pointwise reduction is not what stands between the alpha and 'most accurate available'; the data (finding 1) and the missing variants (finding 2) are. This measurement should replace 'documented at 0.0107" against Swiss' as the statement of the reduction's correctness, since the 0.0107 is dominated by the instrument.
- fix: Add the optional Klioner w2 term (aberration.mjs already has withPotential) to reduce.mjs's 'full' aberration, and commit this ERFA-chain comparison as a tier-b test so the 4e-7" figure becomes a regression gate.

## alpha-reduction-math-6 — TDB-TT three-term form is within 21.8 us of eraDtdb; a full series would remove the last time-scale term
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
