- claim: tdbMinusTt (reduce.mjs:149-153) differs from ERFA's eraDtdb (Fairhead & Bretagnon, geocentric u=v=0) by at most 2.181e-5 s, rms 6.2e-6 s, over 20,001 epochs 1850-2150, so the '<30 us' claim holds. At the Moon's 0.55"/s this is at most 1.2e-5" of longitude, comparable to the Moon's numerical floor (finding 8) and the only remaining model-side term above 1e-6" for the Moon.
- evidence: S/erfa-chain/erfa-chain.py output: 'TDB-TT: alpha 3-term vs eraDtdb, n=20001: max |diff| 2.181e-05 s rms 6.212e-06 s'.
- impact: Opportunity: Swiss's handling of TDB-TT was not measured here; ERFA-level (sub-us) time scale is cheap and would make the Moon's time argument better than any 30-us convention.
- fix: Port the 787-term eraDtdb series or its 20-term truncation (error ~1 us) into reduce.mjs and frame-of-date.mjs (the interval version needs the same terms as sine intervals; reducedRadians already handles this).

## alpha-reduction-math-7 — IAU 2000A would move longitudes by at most 0.00215 arcsec on the corpus; Swiss uses 2000B by default too
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: nut00b matches erfa.nut00b to 6.7e-12" (dpsi) / 1.2e-12" (deps) over 601 epochs 1850-2150; adjustToP03 is bit-for-bit eraNut06a's fj2/0.4697e-6 rule. Replacing 2000B/P03 with eraNut06a (2000A) changes apparent longitude by 0.00215" max on the 2,000-row corpus, 0.00225" max in dpsi over the grid, rms 0.00074". The tree's <=0.0025" is confirmed. Swiss's own nutation departs from published 2000B by up to 0.00153" on the same rows (Pluto residual vs Swiss is 0.895-correlated with that departure), so 2000A is a step past Swiss, not a step to it.
- evidence: S/erfa-chain/erfa-chain-results.json 'frames' and '2000A minus 2000B' lines; S/swiss/ decomposition output (dpsi diff max 0.00153").
- impact: 2 mas is below the centre/barycentre item by 40x and below the lunar-series item by 5x; worth doing after those, when nutation becomes the limiting term (the numerics track says the same).
- fix: Carry the already-parsed 1,365-term table (docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/src/nutation-series.json) behind a nutation:'2000a' switch; cost measured there at 77 us per evaluation.

## alpha-reduction-math-8 — Geocentric Moon is formed by cancelling two 1.5e8 km barycentric vectors at two epochs: a 1.3e-5 arcsec numerical floor
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: Reducer.apparent computes geo = [EMB(et-tau) + MoonRelEMB(et-tau)] - [EMB(et) + EarthRelEMB(et)] in doubles (reduce.mjs:209-229 via backend.state). The EMB terms are at different epochs so they do not cancel algebraically, and their rounding (1.4e-5 km, i.e. 1e-13 relative on 1.5e8 km) does not cancel numerically; against an independent reader/chain the Moon shows 1.34e-5" and 2.45e-5 km worst, 40 of 200 epochs above 1e-6", while planets sit at 4e-7". The EMRAT derivation itself is right: the pack stores Moon relative to EMB (segment 301/3) and Earth = EMB - MoonRelEMB/EMRAT (compile.mjs:357-364, ephemeris.mjs:350-351), EMRAT is taken from the coefficient ratio and verified over every coefficient, and the validated search merges the weights so the geocentric Moon is one series with weight 1 + 1/EMRAT (validated-search.mjs:112-137) rather than two independent errors added.
- evidence: S/erfa-chain/erfa-chain-results.json (per-body worst), S/erfa-chain/dump-spk2.mjs reader comparison (EMB 1.437e-5 km, Moon 4.657e-7 km).
- impact: Irrelevant against Swiss (mas level) but it is the alpha's true Moon precision floor and would cap a future sub-1e-5" claim.
- fix: Form the geocentric Moon as MoonRelEMB(et-tau) - EarthRelEMB(et) + [EMB(et-tau) - EMB(et)], computing the bracket from the EMB Chebyshev polynomial difference within a record (or velocity x tau plus a second-order term when tau spans records), which keeps every term at the 4e5 km scale.

## alpha-reduction-math-9 — Re-measured against Swiss on 2,000 fresh rows: 0.0104 arcsec max, and the residual is the instrument, not the reduction
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Alpha (raw DE440s) vs Swiss 2.10.03 with sepl_18/semo_18.se1 (flag 258 = SWIEPH confirmed on every call), TT input, 200 epochs 1852-2148 x 10 bodies: |dlon| max 0.01041" (Moon), p50 0.00041", p95 0.00283"; Sun 0.00245, Venus 0.00704 (one 1850-1900 row), Mars 0.00298, Jupiter 0.00188, Pluto 0.00151. Decomposition: the Moon residual is U-shaped, mean -0.0003" in 1950-2000, -0.0049" in 1850-1900 and -0.0070" in 2100-2150 (max 0.0104 at 2100-2150), which is the signature of two lunar ephemerides (Swiss's DE431 fit vs DE440) diverging on both sides of the LLR data span, and REFINES the numerics track's 'grows backwards in time' hypothesis (it grows forwards too). Pluto's residual correlates 0.895 with Swiss's own departure from published IAU 2000B nutation (<=0.00153"), leaving 0.00064"; the Sun's remainder after removing that term is <=0.00145". Since the alpha matches the ERFA chain to 4e-7" on the same data, the alpha's reduction is more than three orders of magnitude closer to the IAU/ERFA chain than Swiss's.
- evidence: S/swiss/swiss-vs-alpha.py -> swiss-vs-alpha.json; decomposition output in the transcript (swe.calc ECL_NUT per row). Committed 160-row figure: docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/numerics/RESULTS.md headline 0.010730".
- impact: Supports the 'exceeds Swiss' claim on the reduction and, if DE440's lunar orbit is the better one (it carries newer LLR), on the Moon by up to 0.01" outside 1950-2000; a DE431 kernel or DE441 Horizons vectors would settle attribution.
- fix: Cite this decomposition instead of the bare 0.0107"; test the lunar attribution with two Horizons VECTORS requests (DE441 Moon geocentric, 1850 and 2140) against the pack, which needs no frame conventions.

## alpha-reduction-math-10 — Harness premise failed: Swiss cannot read de440s.bsp; FLG_JPLEPH silently answers with Moshier (flag 4)
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: swe.set_ephe_path('<swisslab>'); swe.set_jpl_file('de440s.bsp') (or the absolute path) followed by calc(..., FLG_JPLEPH) returns flags 4 (MOSEPH) with no exception; positions then differ from the alpha by up to 2.82" (Pluto), 1.64" (Moon), which is Moshier's accuracy, not a reduction difference. Swiss's JPL reader takes the asc2eph binary format, not SPK/DAF. Consequently the 'Swiss-with-DE440s vs Swiss-with-.se1 isolates the series' and 'Swiss-with-DE440s vs alpha isolates the reduction' experiments proposed to all nine auditors are impossible on this machine, and any auditor who did not read the returned flag has compared against Moshier.
- evidence: S/swiss/swiss-vs-alpha.py first block ('returned flags [260]'), and the follow-up probe in the transcript: 'set_jpl_file(de440s.bsp): returned flags 4 -> MOSEPH=True lon=327.9627403' vs se1 327.9627295 vs Moshier-forced 327.9627403 (identical). Also S/centre/swiss-centre.json 'de440s_jpleph' rows carry flags 260.
- impact: Affects the audit, not the engine: the reduction-vs-series isolation has to be done with pyerfa + jplephem (as here) or with a de440 file in JPL's binary format.
- fix: For future measurements obtain JPL's ascii/binary DE440 (linux_p1550p2650.440 via asc2eph) for Swiss, or rely on the ERFA/jplephem chain; add a flag assertion to every Swiss harness script (tools/measure/swiss.py already reads flags).

## alpha-reduction-math-11 — Horizons QUANTITIES=31 is an IAU76/80 ecliptic-of-date frame: 0.049 arcsec from the alpha at J2000 for Mars, so it cannot arbitrate at the mas level
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: Horizons' legend for ObsEcLon/ObsEcLat says 'IAU76/80 ecliptic-of-date'. At JD 2451545.0 TT Horizons gives Mars 327.9627159 / -1.0677844 while the alpha (IAU 2006/2000B, ICRF bias) gives 327.9627294 / -1.0677946: +0.0487" / -0.0367", the size of the 1976 vs 2006 obliquity constant (84381.448 vs 84381.406 = 0.042") and the FK5/ICRF bias. The site's engine.test.ts JPL gate uses the same quantity; at its 0.05-degree gates that is harmless, but no sub-0.05" claim about the alpha can be made against quantity 31. Horizons remained useful for frame-free differences: 599-vs-5 and 999-vs-9 in the same frame reproduced my kernel-derived centre offsets to 0.3 mas.
- evidence: S/horizons/q1-mars-2451545.txt lines 83-86 (legend) and the $$SOE row; alpha value from the node one-liner in the transcript; S/horizons/q-*.query record the exact query strings (5 requests total).
- impact: Prevents a false 'disagrees with JPL by 0.05"' conclusion about the alpha and prevents using q31 as the mas arbiter the audit brief hoped for.
- fix: For a mas-level arbiter use Horizons VECTORS (geometric ICRF states, DE441) against the pack, and apparent RA/Dec (quantity 2) only after confirming its precession/nutation model in the legend; keep the ERFA chain as the reduction arbiter.

## alpha-reduction-math-12 — No output carries a sky-referenced error bound; the pointwise apparent() carries none at all
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 3 d
- claim: The validated/experimental searches enclose the PACK POLYNOMIAL rigorously but explicitly exclude the pack's proven compression bound ('a pack's own declared bound is not evidence', validated-search.mjs:558-571) and everything external (result.mjs EXTERNAL_UNCERTAINTY), and Reducer.apparent() returns lon/lat/distKm with no uncertainty field. The compiler does prove a fit bound (0.0224 km geocentric, cited in reduce.mjs:70-89) and this audit shows the reduction is within 5e-7" of ERFA, so a per-result bound of the form 'polynomial enclosure + proven fit bound/distance + model-omission budget (Klioner 4e-7", 2000B-vs-2000A 2.3e-3", TDB-TT 1.2e-5" Moon, centre offset for barycentre bodies)' is derivable today. Swiss cannot offer any bound; this is the clearest path to being provably, not just measurably, more accurate.
- evidence: validated-search.mjs:543,558-571; result.mjs:191-203; reduce.mjs:288-302 (return shape). Bound inputs: this audit's measurements (findings 1, 5, 6, 7) and docs/platform/evidence/precision-2026-09-20/compiler/RESULTS.md candidate D.
- impact: Without it the 'rigorous proven error bounds on every output' differentiator from Swiss is a property of the search internals, not of anything a chart consumer receives.
- fix: Add an `uncertainty` object to apparent(): numerical (Clenshaw + rounding, from cheb.mjs), data (pack's proven fit bound propagated through 1/distance, plus the centre-offset bound for barycentre bodies), and model (the itemised omissions above), each labelled proven/measured/estimated exactly as result.mjs already distinguishes.

## alpha-search-partition-11 — Evaluations undercount wall time by 16–25 %: frame, aberration and deflection phases charge zero evaluations
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: The budget currency counts one unit per stored-series record evaluation (retarded.mjs:129 and 183 via spend), which is sound and counted in exactly one closure per operation (validated-search.mjs:394-398; retarded-search.mjs:798-805; domain-partition.mjs:644-649; partitioned-search sums the two). The frame rotation, aberration and deflection arithmetic run outside any spend and cost real time, so evaluation-based ratios (P-4, P-5, the 47–831x) are not wall ratios.
- evidence: Instrument sink on the real pack: Saturn F7 window, 63,945 evaluations, 3,978 cells, 886 ms wall; zero-evaluation phases frame 168.5 ms + deflection 32.8 ms + aberration 7.8 ms = 209.1 ms = 25.0 % of attributed time (23.6 % of wall). Mars F5: 285,731 evaluations, 3,934 ms; zero-evaluation phases 614.6 ms = 16.3 % of attributed. domain-test itself 3.8 ms / 4.8 ms (negligible, as PARTITION-EVALUATION claims). Artifact: …/audit/alpha-search-partition/partition/phase-share.json.
- impact: None on soundness; the preregistered cost targets are honestly stated in evaluations and the wall figures are labelled secondary. Worth knowing when the fix in finding 2 shifts the balance toward frame cost.
- fix: Report `execution.wallMs` and the zero-evaluation share alongside evaluations when a sink is installed, or charge a fixed 'frame unit' per frame call so the currency approximates wall.

## alpha-search-partition-12 — Bracket claim near a noise-level endpoint is technically a tolerance, not a containment (unconfirmed)
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.2 d
- claim: In the different-sign branch of searchPiece (validated-search.mjs:302-348) an endpoint whose |value| ≤ RHO is trusted for its sign, and the bisection clips the bracket to [lo, hi] (lines 344-345). If rounding flipped that sign the true root would lie up to RHO/slopeFloor outside the reported bracket, contradicting the comment 'the bracket is real, not a tolerance'. The same-sign branch already declines such cells (line 323). Unconfirmed: constructing a sign flip inside the rounding allowance is not controllable, and the realised Clenshaw error is far below RHO, so no reproduction exists; the bound on the discrepancy is RHO/slopeFloor ≈ 1e-8 s on the fixtures.
- evidence: Code reading; fixtures at record-edge offsets 0, ±1e-9, ±1e-8 s all came back unresolved rather than mis-bracketed (results.json), consistent with the same-sign guard catching them.
- impact: Negligible numerically; matters only for the wording of the theorem.
- fix: When |flo| ≤ RHO or |fhi| ≤ RHO in the different-sign branch, widen the final bracket by reach = RHO/slopeFloor toward that endpoint (into the neighbouring cell), or state the bracket as 'root within reach of this bracket'.

## alpha-search-partition-13 — Chart-adapter contract omissions: nutation model versus frame, nodes, coverage window, houses
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md is careful and largely right, but (a) §1 says the of-date frame is 'the same frame' as production's Rotation_EQJ_ECT and 'the one place the two stacks already agree' — it is the same frame DEFINITION with a different nutation MODEL (astronomy-engine keeps 5 of the 77 IAU 2000B terms; the alpha uses all 77 + P03), so a longitude comparison will see a model difference the contract does not budget; (b) §2.3 says to refuse the nodes, but a chart cannot refuse two of its twelve bodies and the pack's Moon state gives the true node by the same angular-momentum construction production uses; (c) the pack covers 1849–2150 while production has no such limit and the contract names no out-of-coverage policy; (d) houses and angles need GAST/obliquity of date from the same nutation model as the longitudes, and the alpha exposes no such API; (e) the proposed pointwise DeflectedPlace carries no error interval although the search layer's whole advantage is verified enclosures — a pointwise place with a proven interval would be the thing that exceeds Swiss.
- evidence: CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT.md:66-71, 165-179, 345-381; the task brief's description of astronomy-engine's nutation truncation; pack coverage from the compiled header (1849-12-25 … 2150-01-21).
- impact: An integration built to this contract would attribute a nutation-model difference to implementation, would have no answer for nodes, and would fail outside 1849–2150.
- fix: Amend §1 to separate frame from model and quote the 5-term vs 77-term difference; specify node computation from the pack's Moon state (true node = direction of r × v of the geocentric Moon, with its own provenance field); add an explicit out-of-coverage fallback policy; add a GAST/obliquity-of-date requirement; define the pointwise place as value plus proven half-width.

## alpha-search-partition-14 — Empirical mode: request endpoints are silently rounded to whole milliseconds and reported as if requested
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: search.mjs:159-160 does a = Math.round(fromTtDays·MS_PER_DAY) and intervalOf reports requestedTtDays as a/MS_PER_DAY (line 460), so a caller's window is moved by up to 0.5 ms at each end without a note; a root in that sliver is neither reported nor listed as unprocessed. The validated and experimental modes work in real seconds and do not have this.
- evidence: Code reading; the empirical mode's brackets are ≥ 1 ms and its completeness is conditional anyway, so no proven claim is affected.
- impact: Cosmetic; mention here because the plan's C1 partition invariant compares windows that could differ by this rounding.
- fix: Report the caller's original TT days in interval.requestedTtDays and the rounded ms grid separately, or refuse non-integer-ms endpoints with a note.

## angles-houses-aspects-4 — Placidus iteration and angle formulas are numerically equivalent to Swiss when given the same inputs (0.004" cusps, 1e-5" angles) with no convergence failures
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Given Swiss's ARMC and true obliquity, the engine's computeAngles reproduces swe.houses_ex ASC/MC to 1.03e-5" max and the 30/60/120/150 x (1/3, 2/3) fixed-point Placidus iteration (64 iterations, 1e-9 deg tolerance) reproduces all twelve Swiss cusps to 0.0041" max over 8,456 cases including 5,328 at 55-66 deg; convergence never failed below the polar circle. This is the documented-convergence path the owner wants: the iteration contracts because |d(AD)/d(RA)| x multiplier < 1 for |phi| < 90 - eps.
- evidence: engine-vs-swiss.json summaryA.all.asc_swissArmc_trueObl max 1.03e-5", mc 4.2e-8", cusp_formula_check p95 0.00064" max 0.0041"; summaryB.formulaCheck_as max 0.0034" (n 5,328), engineNull_lat_le_66 = 0; the 0.004" floor is Swiss's own ~1e-9 deg stopping tolerance. Code: chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:45-90.
- impact: Positive: the geometry core is sound, so the angle fixes are input fixes, not rewrites. To exceed Swiss, publish the contraction bound and a proven iteration count (Swiss documents neither), and expose cusp speeds (houses_ex2) which follow from differentiating the same closed forms.
- fix: Add a docs note with the contraction argument and a test that asserts iteration count <= N(phi) on the 55-66.5 grid; expose iterations and residual in a debug receipt field.

## angles-houses-aspects-9 — Classical dignity table is correct against the Ptolemaic scheme but stops at domicile/exaltation/detriment/fall
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: All 28 cells in dignities.ts:18-26 match the standard table (Sun Leo/Aries/Aquarius/Libra; Moon Cancer/Taurus/Capricorn/Scorpio; Mercury Gemini+Virgo/Virgo/Sagittarius+Pisces/Pisces; Venus Taurus+Libra/Pisces/Scorpio+Aries/Virgo; Mars Aries+Scorpio/Capricorn/Libra+Taurus/Cancer; Jupiter Sagittarius+Pisces/Cancer/Gemini+Virgo/Capricorn; Saturn Capricorn+Aquarius/Libra/Cancer+Leo/Aries). Precedence exaltation > fall > domicile > detriment is documented (:36-41). Missing: triplicity rulers, Egyptian/Ptolemaic terms (bounds), faces (decans), exaltation degrees, peregrine and mutual reception, and outer-planet policy is explicitly 'none'.
- evidence: Table read line by line; runtime check with the TS module: dignityFor('Mercury','virgo') = exaltation, dignitiesFor = [domicile, exaltation]; ('Mercury','pisces') = fall; ('Pluto','scorpio') = null; ('Sun','aries') = exaltation. Tests exist (dignities.test.ts, 5 cases).
- impact: No deviation to fix; Swiss has no dignity model, so a complete essential-dignity scheme (five dignities with a documented scoring convention and mutual reception) is a clean way to exceed it.
- fix: Add triplicities (day/night/participating), Ptolemaic and Egyptian terms, faces, exaltation degrees, peregrine and mutual reception with a selectable table and tests per cell.

## angles-houses-aspects-10 — Finite-difference speed (+/-0.25 day) places planetary stations 1-4 minutes from Swiss's analytic speed; retrograde flag correct outside that window
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: longitudeSpeed is a central difference over +/-6 h (chunk-GBH7JIYF.js:74-82) and retrograde = speed < 0. Around a station the average speed over 12 h differs from the instantaneous speed, shifting the sign change.
- evidence: Swiss station instants by bisection on swe.calc_ut speed (FLG_SPEED, SWIEPH): Mercury 2024-04-01T22:14Z, Mars 2024-12-06T23:33Z, Venus 2025-03-02T00:36Z. Engine sign change by bisection on longitudeSpeed: -0.90 min, +0.84 min, -3.79 min respectively; engine speed at the Swiss station instant -7.4e-5, +7.9e-6, -1.07e-4 deg/day. Artifacts: stations-swiss.json, stations-engine.json.
- impact: Below Swiss's analytic speeds by a few minutes at stations only; affects the applying test's inputs too. Easy to exceed: astronomy-engine already returns state vectors.
- fix: Compute d(lon)/dt analytically from the geocentric state vector rotated to the ecliptic of date (GeoMoonState / BaryState-derived velocity), keeping the finite difference only as a test oracle.

## angles-houses-aspects-11 — The engine's sidereal time is closer to the IAU 2006/2000A standard than Swiss's default: 0.19" vs 1.9" in RAMC outside 1850-2050; obliquity time argument (UTC vs TT) is negligible
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: Engine RAMC (astronomy-engine SiderealTime, 5-term nutation) vs ERFA gst06a: max 0.188" across 1800-2200. Swiss swe.houses_ex ARMC vs the same arbiter: <= 0.001" from 1850 to 2025 but 1.908" at 2050, 1.79" at 2100, 0.68" at 2200 and 0.35" at 1800, consistent with Swiss switching to its long-term sidereal-time model outside 1850-2050. Using UTC instead of TT in centuriesSinceJ2000 for the mean obliquity changes it by at most 6.6e-6" (year 2200, deltaT 442 s).
- evidence: gridA-rows.json per-year maxima (arcsec): 1800 swiss-erfa 0.353 engine-erfa 0.033; 1850-2025 swiss-erfa <= 0.001, engine-erfa 0.017-0.188; 2050 1.908 vs 0.076; 2100 1.793 vs 0.154; 2200 0.680 vs 0.070. e_tilt nutation in obliquity vs Swiss max 0.030" (summaryA.all.dObl_aeTrue_swissTrue). oblTT table in engine-vs-swiss.json: dObl 2.0e-7" (1800) .. 6.6e-6" (2200). astronomy-engine iau2000b carries 5 terms (esm/astronomy.js:1303-1349).
- impact: Once finding 1 is fixed, the engine's angles would sit within ~7" of the IAU arbiter across 1800-2200 (0.36" for |lat| <= 45) while Swiss's default departs by up to 68" at lat 66 after 2050. This is a measurable, honest 'exceeds Swiss' claim for angles, provided DUT1 (not applied by either; <= 0.9 s = 13.5" of RAMC) is either applied from IERS data or disclosed.
- fix: Keep the IAU 2006 GMST + equation of equinoxes path; optionally upgrade to the full 77-term IAU 2000B (or the alpha's nutation module) for a sub-0.05" RAMC; add an optional IERS DUT1 table (finals2000A, compact) to convert UTC to UT1 for the sidereal time, with a receipt flag.

## angles-houses-aspects-13 — Polar ASC quadrant rule matches Swiss on 1,152 sweep points; exact pole and DUT1 remain out of scope
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: computeAngles' rule 'if normalize(asc - mc) >= 180 then asc += 180' (chunk-H7RJKCBO.js:34) selects the rising intersection exactly as swe.houses_ex does at 66.6, 70, 78.22, 85 and 89.9 deg in both hemispheres across a full sidereal day; at exactly +/-90 the ASC degenerates to 0/180 and whole-sign cusps differ from Swiss in 35 of 96 steps because a 1e-6" difference flips the sign boundary.
- evidence: Grid C (2001-12-21, 96 x 15 min, lon 15.6267, 'W'): ASC max residual 15.5" (66.6, obliquity effect of finding 1), 0.60" (70), 0.13" (78.22), 0.044" (85), 9e-7" (90); MC max 0.029"; ASC formula residual with Swiss inputs <= 1.6e-9"; engineAscEastOfMc and swissAscEastOfMc true for every latitude; swappedVsSwiss 0; wholeMismatch 0 except 35 at +/-90. Artifacts: engine-vs-swiss.json summaryC, gridC-rows.json.
- impact: Confirms the polar angle behaviour; the remaining gap to a stronger-than-Swiss polar story is an explicit, per-system documented policy (which intersection, MC flip or not, fallback system) instead of one silent rule plus one flag.
- fix: Document the rising-intersection choice and the exact-pole degeneracy in the receipt CONVENTIONS block; reject |lat| = 90 with a clear error or return angles with a 'degenerate-horizon' flag.

## data-toolchain-packaging-10 — Production tarball rebuilds byte-identically from the pinned commit; pins, hashes and lock integrity all agree
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: The vendored @zodiacs/engine 0.1.1-rc.6 is fully auditable despite living in another repository: vendor/zodiacs-engine-0.1.1-rc.6.sha256 = sha256(tgz) = sha256 of the raw.githubusercontent artifact at carrier commit 51129a19 = sha256 of a fresh `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile; pnpm run build; npm pack` at source commit fb57af7a (09c3e63432f8ba2e9df05af137c42f65ab039740a207a89418d9e6470ea3db3e), and package-integration.test.ts additionally pins the package-lock sha512 integrity and ENGINE_VERSION==='0.1.1-rc.6'. Swiss Ephemeris has no equivalent reproducible-build attestation for sweph or the .se1 files. Gap: the rebuild is not performed in CI, so the sourceCommit→artifact link is asserted in prose (vendor/README.md) rather than checked.
- evidence: scratch/audit/data-toolchain-packaging/sdk-install.log, sdk-build.log, measurements.json (engineRebuild: wall 8 s); vendor/README.md:8-12; src/lib/engine/package-integration.test.ts:51-68,75-76; package.json:152 file: pin.
- impact: Opportunity to exceed Swiss on provenance: a CI job that rebuilds from sourceCommit and diffs against vendor/*.sha256 makes the pin verifiable rather than declared.
- fix: Add a scheduled/PR CI job that performs the rebuild above and fails on hash mismatch; record sourceCommit in a machine-readable candidate.json the test reads (scripts/platform-engine-candidate.mjs already validates such a schema).

## data-toolchain-packaging-11 — Integrity verified end to end; authenticity absent by design — what an Ed25519 manifest would add and cost
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 2 d
- claim: ZODEPH02 = 16-byte prologue (magic, uint32 headerLen, uint32 payloadOffset) + JSON header + 16-aligned body blocks + 32-byte SHA-256 trailer over everything before it. Measured on a sealed synthetic pack (198,055 B): flipping intervalSec 86400→86401, a body name, a payload byte, a trailer byte → 'corrupt'; emrat digit → 'bad-header'; headerLen+1 → 'bad-header'; magic ZODEPH01 → 'unsupported-version'; size-cap tripwire refuses before any read. A header edit followed by re-sealing OPENS with selfConsistent=true (integrity cannot see it); the same file with expectDigest of the original → 'mutated'. LIMITS checks precede allocation (safeExtent overflow guard). Swiss .se1 files carry no digest or signature at all.
- evidence: scratch/audit/data-toolchain-packaging/flip.log and flip.mjs; container.mjs:44-52 LIMITS, :63-72 safeExtent, :83-138 parseContainer, :299-321 integrity policy; tools/seal.mjs; tier-b 'an out-of-band digest that matches opens, and one that does not is refused' pass.
- impact: Exceed-Swiss opportunity: a signed manifest {packDigest, kernelSha256, compilerVersion, compilerSourceSha256, coverage, producedAt(omit for determinism), keyId, signature} with Ed25519 (WebCrypto: Node 22, Chrome ≥113, Firefox ≥130, Safari 17) binds a pack to the producer's key so a re-sealed edit is detected without an out-of-band digest per pack; it does not solve key distribution (publish the public key at zodiacs.org/.well-known and in the repo, pin in the runtime). SLSA-style provenance (GitHub attestations of the compile job with kernel hash and compiler commit) adds build-integrity for ~1–2 more days.
- fix: Add `tools/sign.mjs` and `openPack*({manifest, trustedKeys})`; refuse when a manifest is supplied and fails; keep expectDigest as the unsigned fallback; add tier-A tests with a test key.

## data-toolchain-packaging-14 — Coverage refusal verified: typed out-of-coverage errors, no silent fallback; usable window is coverage minus max light-time
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: On the real pack, apparent('Sun', ±73050 d) throws PrecisionError code 'out-of-coverage' with detail {et}; ±54700/54800 d inside coverage succeed; apparent('Pluto', start+0.01 d) is refused because the 24,168 s light-time lookback leaves coverage, while start+0.5 d succeeds. raw() and seriesAt() refuse rather than clamp (ephemeris.mjs:104-112,168-176). The production engine, by contrast, computes any year with no flag (docs/engine-validation/README.md concedes this; astronomy-engine's Pluto table spans -730000..+730000 days TT and integrates one-way beyond it).
- evidence: scratch/audit/data-toolchain-packaging/coverage-probe.log; ephemeris.mjs:104-112; reduce.mjs light-time lookback refusal message; astronomy.js:2994-2999,3238.
- impact: Behaviour is correct and better than Swiss's silent Moshier fallback when .se1 files are missing (Swiss returns a different-model answer with only a flag change). A production integration must still define the boundary policy (CHART-ADAPTER-CONTRACT §5.2) and should expose `usableCoverage = coverage minus max light-time (~0.28 d for Pluto)`.
- fix: Add `runtime.usableCoverage(body)` and document; in the adapter, refuse or downgrade with an explicit per-body flag rather than mixing models silently.

## production-event-search-13 — Lunar-return Moon path lacks the −0.7" light-time term the README concedes; sized at +1.3 s of return time
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: bodyLongitude('Moon') goes through EclipticGeoMoon (CalcMoon rotated into the true ecliptic of date) and never through GeoVector, so no light-time/planetary-aberration correction is applied. Swiss's apparent-minus-geometric Moon difference is a steady −0.67 to −0.74" across 1850–2140, worth ≈1.3 s of lunar-return time (Moon at 0.51–0.57"/s). The README's 'not identical' concession is thus a 1.3 s effect, an order of magnitude smaller than the Delta-T bias in finding 3.
- evidence: out/s5b-swiss-returns.json moonEpochs: swiss_apparent_minus_geometric_arcsec −0.7191 (1850), −0.677 (1950), −0.669 (2020), −0.6736 (2026-09-22), −0.6797 (2140); the term is entirely light-time (swiss_lightTimeOnly_effect equals it; Swiss's FLG_NOABERR for the Moon changes a different ±17" quantity). engine − Swiss apparent Moon: 0.16–4.3" modern, 10.2" (2050), 64.8" (2100), 98.8" (2140). Code: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js moonOfDate → EclipticGeoMoon; astronomy.js:3849-3850 GeoVector returns GeoMoon for the Moon unconditionally.
- impact: Small, but a stack aiming past Swiss must apply it: with finding 3 fixed, this becomes the largest remaining Moon-event bias.
- fix: Backdate the Moon by its light-time (distance/c ≈ 1.28 s) before CalcMoon, or take the Moon from the DE-backed reducer that already applies light-time and aberration (examples/precision-alpha/src/core/reduce.mjs), and record the change in docs/engine-validation/swiss-lunar-return/README.md.

## production-event-search-14 — Void-of-course: definition, precision and coverage stated; matches the modern 'last exact Ptolemaic aspect' convention
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1 d
- claim: void-of-course.ts implements the void as the interval from the Moon's last exact conjunction/sextile/square/trine/opposition to any of Sun, Mercury–Pluto (default) or Sun–Saturn (option) until the next sign ingress, with no orb, no nodes/asteroids/angles/parallels and no Lilly-style 'applying within orb' rule; ingresses use a 6-hour scan with 24 bisections (1.3 ms), aspects a 3-hour scan with 26 bisections. The relative Moon−planet motion is monotonic (Moon ≥11.8°/day vs Mercury ≤2.2°/day) so the 3-hour scan cannot miss an aspect; the page text matches the code.
- evidence: src/lib/engine/void-of-course.ts:15-33,74-141,148-169; src/pages/void-of-course-moon/index.astro:66,74,243-249; tests void-of-course.test.ts (7 cases incl. a 30-minute brute-force sweep and a cross-check against aura-moon-ingresses to 1 s). Moon ingress instants vs Swiss (out/s9): n=481, median 5.9 s, max 10.1 s, all early — the Delta-T bias of finding 3, not a solver defect. Mainstream references: Astrodienst/Solar Fire default to the ten-planet exact-aspect rule; traditionalists (Lilly) use an orb-based applying rule, which is not offered.
- impact: At parity with mainstream software; Swiss has no VOC function. With finding 3 fixed the boundaries would be within ~1 s of Horizons.
- fix: Optional: add the Lilly/orb variant and an 'applying to the next-sign aspect' flag as options; publish the definition string with each API window.

## production-event-search-15 — Progressions: tropical-year day-for-a-year (365.2422 mean solar days per civil day), planets only, no alternative rates
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 2 d
- claim: progressions.ts maps elapsed time in units of 365.2422 × 86400 s onto civil days from birth (PROGRESSION_DAYS_PER_YEAR = 365.2422) and computes bodies only; progressed angles (Naibod 59'08"/yr, solar-arc MC, mean quotidian) and the Q2 'true solar arc' day-for-a-year variant are not offered. The methodology page states the convention accurately. Solar Fire's default and most Swiss-based apps use the same 365.2422 mapping; 365.25 and Q2 are user options there.
- evidence: src/lib/engine/progressions.ts:11,20-23; src/pages/methodology/index.astro:162-165; progressions.test.ts covers the mapping against a constructed JPL fixture (1 ms gate) and the 12–15°/yr progressed Moon rate.
- impact: Correct and honest but thinner than Swiss-based software; no accuracy issue.
- fix: Add year-length option (365.25 Julian) and progressed angles via Naibod and solar-arc MC with the convention stamped in the receipt; the solar-arc variant needs a progressed-Sun search which the crossing solver already supports.

## production-event-search-16 — Half-day transit-window sampling is sound for the slow bodies: 12-hour motion ≤0.121° and stations ≥117 days apart
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: transit-window-core's 12-hour grid cannot lose a 3° orb entry/exit (a body would have to traverse 6° in 12 h) and cannot hide a crossing pair, because a pair requires a turning point inside the cell and turns are bracketed by the grid's slope-sign test with at least 117 days between them. The Moon and fast bodies never enter this scanner (transitBodies restricted to Jupiter–Pluto); transit-scan-core scans Sun/Mercury/Venus/Mars by default and the Moon when the TransitSearch UI selects it (0.25-day step, station-split irrelevant), the calendar API scans the defaults over −31/+183 days.
- evidence: out/s7-slow-bounds.json (37.8 s, 292,194 half-day samples per body 1800–2200): max |Δλ| per 12 h Jupiter 0.12126° (1892-03-21), Saturn 0.0652°, Uranus 0.0316°, Neptune 0.0190°, Pluto 0.0203°; minimum station spacing Jupiter 117.5 d, Saturn 133.5 d, Uranus 149 d, Neptune 156 d, Pluto 156 d. Code: transit-window-core.ts:57-61,108-131,176-194; transit-scan-core.ts:95-106 (STEP_DAYS incl. Moon 0.25), TransitSearch.worker.ts:41 includeMoon, TransitSearch.tsx:46-50 (1/5/10-year spans), api/calendar/transits.ts:10-11.
- impact: No sampling defect in the window scanner; the remaining weakness there is the budget policy of finding 6.
- fix: Record these bounds in the transit-windows README as the sampling argument (they are currently asserted, not shown).

## production-event-search-17 — Path to exceed Swiss: certified completeness from the alpha's derivative-enclosure search behind every production crossing
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 15 d
- claim: Swiss Ephemeris offers no completeness guarantee for any event function (solcross/mooncross return the next root by iteration; users find stations by speed sign and everything else by their own scans). The alpha already proves completeness for geometric longitude crossings in a fixed J2000 frame (searchGeometricLongitude: exclusion and monotone cells from Chebyshev-coefficient Lipschitz bounds) and has of-date/aberration/retarded ladders with interval enclosures; the deflection layer failed its usefulness rule and the partition is partial. Production uses none of it.
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/src/core/validated-search.mjs:1-55,372 (proof sketch and entry point); RESULTS.md §3-6 (typed verdicts crossing/no-crossing/stationary-touch/multiple-crossings/boundary-event/unresolved-interval; Uranus D certified at ε=0.001° on DE440s); this audit's findings 4 and 5 show what the fixed-step scan drops. Swiss API surface enumerated from pyswisseph 2.10.03: solcross(_ut), mooncross(_ut), mooncross_node(_ut), helio_cross(_ut), sol_eclipse_when_glob/loc/where/how, lun_eclipse_when/when_loc/how, lun_occult_when_glob/loc/where, rise_trans(_true_hor), heliacal_ut/heliacal_pheno_ut, nod_aps(_ut), pheno(_ut) — none carries a completeness contract.
- impact: This is the one dimension where 'more advanced than Swiss' is achievable with existing repository code: a return, transit or ingress result that says 'these are all the crossings, proven' or 'this cell is unresolved' instead of a list that may be short.
- fix: (1) Compile a DE440s pack (tools/compiler) and ship a fixed-frame geometric certification alongside each production crossing list as a count check; (2) extend the interval enclosures through the of-date frame and aberration (both ladders exist) to certify apparent tropical crossings, declaring the empirical curvature bound where the proof does not reach; (3) route findLongitudeCrossingsWith/saturnReturns/yearScan/transit-scan-core through it with typed verdicts surfaced in the UI; (4) leave deflection out until it passes its own rule.

## production-positions-11 — DE431 (Swiss .se1) vs DE440s is 0.003" or less: moving to DE440/441 gains nothing against Swiss by itself; the premise that Swiss can read de440s.bsp is false
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Swiss geometric ICRS positions from sepl_18/semo_18 (DE431 excerpts) versus jplephem on de440s.bsp, daily 2025: all ten bodies max 0.000-0.003" angular separation; at the 1900/2000/2100 epochs 0.000-0.006" (Moon 2100 largest). Horizons (DE441) vs Swiss in 2025: +0.046..0.048" (a constant that is the practical ceiling of 'agreement with Swiss'); Pluto 2200: Swiss -0.50" vs DE441. Attempting swe.set_jpl_file('de440s.bsp') with FLG_JPLEPH returned retflag 260 (FLG_MOSEPH set, FLG_JPLEPH clear): Swiss 2.10.03 does not read NAIF SPK files and silently fell back to Moshier, so the 'Swiss with DE440s' leg requested by the task does not exist on this machine and was replaced by reading the kernel directly. Saturn centre vs system barycentre (Horizons 699 vs 6) differs by 0.024" lon / 0.010" lat; Swiss reports barycentres.
- evidence: compare.json keys T8b_daily2025_geometric_series_only (swissICRS_geom_vs_DE440s_sep), T8_epochs (geom_swissICRS_vs_DE440s_sep), T8c (frame bias 0.022"); swiss-jpl-probe.txt; horizons/arbiter.json.
- impact: Sets expectations: the path to exceeding Swiss is series replacement plus a fuller reduction and independent arbitration (Horizons/DE441, SOFA/ERFA), not a newer DE alone; a claim of 'DE440 therefore better than Swiss' would be unsupported at the 0.01" level.
- fix: Frame the roadmap around (a) full-precision series, (b) complete IAU reduction, (c) planet centres via satellite kernels, (d) Horizons/DE441 arbitration; record in the evidence tree that Swiss cannot consume .bsp files.

## production-positions-12 — Frame-chain nits: FK5 obliquity 84381.448" in the VSOP rotation vs P03 84381.406" in precession, no ICRS frame bias (0.022"), heliocentric aberration velocity, UTC taken as UT1
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: VsopRotate (astronomy.js:2956) rotates VSOP87 ecliptic to the equator with cos/sin of the IAU 1976 obliquity while precession_rot (:1794) uses eps0 = 84381.406" (P03), a 0.042" internal inconsistency; the J2000/FK5 to ICRS frame bias is not applied (Swiss J2000 vs ICRS output differs by 0.0221" on every 2025 row); BodyPosition.Position (:3750-3780) uses the heliocentric, not barycentric, Earth velocity for aberration (<= 0.01"); MakeTime treats UTC as UT1 (|UT1-UTC| <= 0.9 s, <= 0.5" Moon) exactly as swe_calc_ut does, so no Swiss gap; centuriesSinceJ2000 for the mean obliquity uses UTC not TT (negligible). Rotation order (precession then nutation then obliquity), longitude atan2(y,x) and latitude asin(z/r) in eclipticOfDate are correct. None of these is measurable beneath the series error today; all become relevant at the 0.01" level.
- evidence: astronomy.js lines cited; compare.json key T8c_swiss_J2000_vs_ICRS_frame_bias_sep.
- impact: Sub-0.05" today; needed to exceed Swiss (which applies frame bias and full reduction) once the series are fixed.
- fix: When adopting the DE pack, start from ICRS, apply the IAU 2006/2000A bias-precession-nutation matrix (the alpha's frames.mjs already does), use the barycentric observer velocity, and accept UT1-UTC from an optional input.

## production-positions-13 — Capability gaps versus Swiss in the body set and outputs
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 4 d
- claim: The package returns lon/lat/lon-speed for Sun, Moon, eight planets and the true nodes only. Absent relative to swe_calc: geocentric distance, latitude and distance rates, mean node, mean and osculating apogee (Lilith), Chiron and asteroids, Earth/heliocentric and barycentric frames, topocentric positions (FLG_TOPOCTR; parallax moves the Moon up to ~1 deg), sidereal zodiac/ayanamsas, equatorial output, J2000/mean-of-date outputs, Delta-T access, and planet-centre vs system-barycentre handling. Unconfirmed: whether any site surface needs these today; the site wrappers are another auditor's area.
- evidence: chunk-GBH7JIYF.js PLANETS list and position() return shape; index.d.ts public API (natalChart/transits/synastry/saturnReturn/moonPhase/findLongitudeCrossings).
- impact: Users migrating from Swiss expect these; some (distance, topocentric Moon) are prerequisites for features such as parallax-corrected charts that would exceed Swiss's default geocentric output.
- fix: Extend the body/position model to carry distance and full state, add mean node and mean apogee from standard mean elements, add topocentric and sidereal modes as options with receipt fields; asteroids/Chiron need additional SPK segments.

## swiss-parity-4 — No Zodiacs configuration is measurably closer to Horizons than Swiss; the residual is Horizons's IAU76/80 frame
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Against Horizons apparent ecliptic-of-date (QUANTITIES=31, header: 'IAU76/80 ecliptic-of-date'), Swiss .se1, Swiss DE440, alpha pack and alpha kernel all give exactly max 0.384 / p50 0.098 arcsec versus planetary barycentres and identical per-row values to 0.01 arcsec. The residual is +0.369 arcsec at 1851, +0.047 at 2000-2026 and -0.346 at 2148 for every body: the IAU 1976 precession-rate difference (~-0.3 arcsec/century) plus EOP pole offsets, i.e. Horizons's older frame model, not ephemeris. Consequently 'more accurate than Swiss' in positions is unmeasurable with any available arbiter: Swiss, DE440s and Horizons agree to within convention differences. The only body over 0.4 arcsec (Uranus, 1.017) is a Horizons-internal inconsistency: Horizons Uranus(799, source ura184_merged) minus Uranus barycentre(7, DE441) reaches 0.671 arcsec although the physical centre offset is 0.003 arcsec.
- evidence: results.json perEpoch and perBody for a-e, b-e, d_pack-e, d_kernel-e, a-e_bary, d_kernel-e_bary, e_centre-e_bary; horizons/Uranus.txt and horizons/UranusBary.txt headers.
- impact: Sets what 'substantially better than Swiss' can truthfully mean: not positional accuracy (parity is the ceiling), but rigor, bounds, integrity, determinism and completeness semantics.
- fix: State parity explicitly in docs/engine-validation/README.md and in the owner brief; never quote Horizons agreement below 0.4 arcsec as evidence of superiority; if Horizons is used again as arbiter, query barycentres for the outer planets and note the IAU76/80 frame.

## swiss-parity-5 — Swiss's centre-of-body correction is exact; satellite kernels are a 0.075 arcsec catch-up for the alpha, not a lead
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 2 d
- claim: With the sepm9599/9699/9799/9899/9999.se1 files, Swiss FLG_CENTER_BODY reproduces Horizons's centre-minus-barycentre offset to ≤0.001 arcsec on all 24 instants for Jupiter (max 0.059), Saturn (0.041) and Pluto (0.075), and ≤0.003 for Uranus/Neptune. The alpha returns system barycentres for Mars..Pluto (labelled isSystemBarycentre). Swiss's DEFAULT (no sepm files, no flag) is also barycentric, so the effect is a ceiling of 0.075 arcsec either way.
- evidence: results.json perBody['a_centre-a'] vs ['e_centre-e_bary'] and the per-row Jupiter/Saturn/Pluto table in the transcript; sepm files downloaded to <scratch>/audit/swiss-parity/ephe/.
- impact: Item (ii) of the owner's list is not a place to exceed Swiss; it is a small parity item.
- fix: Add centre-relative-to-barycentre segments from jup348/sat480/nep097/plu060 to the pack compiler as optional low-degree series and expose centre vs barycentre as a convention flag in the receipt.

## swiss-parity-8 — Delta-T has no published uncertainty anywhere; three respected models spread 256 s (140 arcsec of Moon) at 2148
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 3 d
- claim: At F3 (2148-12-30) Horizons's ΔT is 51.8 s below Swiss and astronomy-engine's is 204.7 s above it; at E1 (2055) the spread is 35 s (19 arcsec Moon). None of Swiss, Horizons or astronomy-engine reports an uncertainty; Swiss's swe_deltat returns a bare number. This is an opportunity to exceed Swiss: a ΔT with a stated band, propagated to Moon longitude and to event times in the receipt.
- evidence: deltat-three-models.json (implied Horizons ΔT from the Moon_UT vs Moon TT requests; Swiss deltat_ex; astronomy-engine MakeTime).
- impact: A genuine, demonstrable advantage over Swiss in honesty rather than in a number.
- fix: Model ΔT as (IERS table to present) + (long-term extrapolation with a 1-sigma band growing ~±(0.3 s/yr) past the table end); surface 'Moon ±x arcsec from ΔT uncertainty' in receipts for dates outside the observed record.

## swiss-parity-9 — Swiss cannot read SPK kernels: set_jpl_file('de440s.bsp') silently answers from Moshier
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: swe.set_ephe_path('<swisslab>'); swe.set_jpl_file('de440s.bsp'); swe.calc_ut(..., FLG_JPLEPH) returns retflag 260 (MOSEPH|SPEED), identical to a nonexistent file name. Swiss reads only the classic JPL binary export; the real configuration (b) needed linux_p1550p2650.440 (102 MB), which then answered JPLEPH (257). The audit brief's stated method for (b) would have compared Moshier and called it DE440.
- evidence: Transcript probe output; jpl/linux_p1550p2650.440 sha256 29915576d0a6...; swiss.json backendsObserved {b: [JPLEPH]}.
- impact: Any future Swiss-with-DE440 comparison in the repo must use the JPL binary format and read the return flag, as dump_swiss.py already does.
- fix: Record this in docs/platform/evidence/swiss-benchmark/CONFIGURATION.md as a pitfall; keep the return-flag check mandatory in every Swiss harness.

## time-9 — Gap/fold resolver verified: 1,675,757 wall minutes around all 42,861 ICU transitions in 418 zones, zero failures in both copies
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: Both resolveLocalToUtc implementations round-trip every tested wall minute around every offset transition the host ICU knows for 1850-2100, with the documented policies (unique -> itself; fold -> earlier instant + 'dst-fold'; gap -> shift by the pre-transition offset + 'dst-gap') matching a brute-force expectation built from the complete offset set of the transition list (independent of the +-36 h sampling). No two ICU transitions fall within 72 h of each other anywhere in 1850-2100, so the +-36 h candidate sampling has structural headroom; 24-hour date-line skips (Pacific/Apia 2011-12-30, Pacific/Kwajalein 1993-08-21) and the Sitka 1867 repeat resolve correctly; every longOffset name (including 'GMT-04:56:02'-style LMT) parsed by a strict anchored regex; year 0000-02-29, 0000-01-01 in Pacific/Honolulu (UTC in year 0) and 9999-12-31 23:59 in Pacific/Kiritimati resolve; negative and 5-digit years are refused with a clear RangeError. This is a positive result and is stronger than the committed tests (which cover 12 hand-picked cases).
- evidence: audit/time/tz-scan.mjs, output audit/time/tz-scan-report.json and tz-scan.log: {zones:418, transitions:42861, tested:1675757, failures:0, pairs72h:0, short:0, tzifOnly:0, unparseable:0, seconds:285.9}; audit/time/calendar-edges.txt for the edge cases. Tests compared: src/lib/time/localToUtc.test.ts (12 cases).
- impact: The algorithm is not where the timezone risk lies; the data (time-2) is. This scan should become a CI fixture once the engine ships its own tzdb table.
- fix: Adopt tz-scan.mjs as a test (sampled per CI run, full nightly) and extend its ground truth to the shipped tzdb table.

## time-10 — Alpha time scales verified: TDB-TT within 22 us of ERFA, integer-ms TT exact, compiler and runtime agree on TDB
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.25 d
- claim: reduce.mjs tdbMinusTt (0.001658 sin g + 0.000014 sin 2g + 0.0000224 sin(L-LJ)) differs from ERFA's full Fairhead-Bretagnon dtdb (geocentric) by at most 22.01 us (rms 6.2 us) over 109,573 daily samples 1850-2150, confirming the stated |error| < 30 us; the topocentric diurnal term it omits is 0.11 us. et = ttDays*86400 + tdbMinusTt has the correct sign (TDB = TT + delta). Integer-millisecond TT (search.mjs) is exact in doubles (|ms| <= 4.7e12 < 2^53); the 'TT seconds past J2000' double has a 1.05 us ULP at 150 years and JD-TT a 48 us ULP, i.e. <= 2.6e-5 arcsec for the Moon. compile.mjs writes coverage in TDB seconds (startEtSecTdb) and its header states the runtime is fed TT; tools/compiler/runtime.mjs indeed uses et = tt*86400 (1.7 ms TDB-TT ignored, 0.9 mas for the Moon) while the core Reducer applies the correction under timescale 'tdb' — consistent with what each documents. The search's conversionApproximation reports only the frame-argument effect of the 30 us model error, which is correct since the pack is evaluated at TDB directly. Delta-T never enters the alpha; a chart adapter must supply TT (see time-1/time-4 for what it needs).
- evidence: examples/precision-alpha/src/core/reduce.mjs:148-153, 196-197; src/core/frame-of-date.mjs:394-473 (TIME_MODEL, tdbMinusTtInterval, ttCenturiesInterval); src/core/search.mjs:36, 87, 159-160; tools/compiler/compile.mjs:81, 512-517; tools/compiler/runtime.mjs:312-315; src/core/retarded-search.mjs:1280-1289. ERFA check (pyerfa in audit/time/erfa-venv): 'n 109573 max|alpha-erfa| us 22.013 rms us 6.213 max|ref| ms 1.697; topocentric diurnal term max us 0.114' (transcript; venv at audit/time/erfa-venv). ULP figures: audit/time/ramc-sensitivity.txt last line.
- impact: None negative; the alpha's time handling is sound and its stated bounds hold. The gap is entirely upstream (UTC->TT).
- fix: When building the chart adapter, take TT from the leap-second path (time-4) and pass Delta-T with its band alongside so the adapter can report uncertainty.timeScale for UT-derived quantities too.

## time-11 — Year range and Delta-T model extremes: what the stack accepts and returns outside 1800-2199
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.1 d
- claim: Inputs are limited to 0000-9999 by the civil parsers (share window 1800-2199; MCP 1800-2199); JS Date spans -271821..+275760; Intl renders BCE years by era (year -1 shown as '2 BC'), handled correctly by wallStringAt; astronomy-engine's Delta-T for far years is the Morrison-Stephenson parabola (year 0: 10,584 s; -500: 17,204 s; 3000: 4,435 s; 5000: 32,339 s) with no uncertainty, and its VSOP87/NOVAS-Moon series are nominally valid only for about +-4000 yr around J2000. Swiss's data files here cover 1800-2400 (sepl_18/semo_18), but Swiss carries Delta-T and calendar arithmetic for -13000..+17000. Not a defect within the declared window; recorded so the receipt's 'broadDateRange: not-certified' has numbers behind it.
- evidence: audit/time/calendar-edges.txt ('AE DeltaT at year 0 and -1000 and 5000 (s)', 'Date extremes', 'Intl wall for year -1'); src/lib/time/civil-date.ts:17-29; src/lib/share.ts:113; src/mcp/bounds.ts:26-27; node_modules/astronomy-engine/astronomy.js:1037-1041, 1129-1135.
- impact: Informational; supports scoping the Delta-T uncertainty band (time-1) to grow rapidly outside 1620-present.
- fix: Publish the supported window with the Delta-T band and refuse (or flag) dates where the band exceeds a stated threshold.

## verification-honesty-10 — Far dates measured: at 2300 and 2399 the engine differs from Swiss by 262-288 arcsec (Moon) and 4-35 arcsec (planets), mostly Delta-T
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 0.5 d
- claim: natalChart returns charts for -500, 900, 2300, 2399 and 3500 with flags []. Against Swiss .se1 at 2300-01-01: Moon 262.2, Sun 17.9, Mercury 21.0, Venus 19.6, Mars 10.5, Jupiter 4.3, Saturn 0.7, Uranus 8.8, Neptune 6.4, Pluto 26.9 arcsec; at 2399-12-31: Moon 287.5, Sun 22.1, Mercury 35.1, Venus 27.7, Pluto 33.1. Delta-T is 717 s (engine) vs 297 s (Swiss) at 2300, which alone is 231 arcsec of the Moon and 17 arcsec of the Sun residual.
- evidence: probes.json far_* entries; swiss-probes.log far-dates block; AE DeltaT 2300 717.16 s (transcript).
- impact: Extends the README's concession (edges touched, no flag beyond range) with a measurement; both Delta-T values are extrapolations so this is not an accuracy verdict, but the residual after Delta-T (about 30 arcsec Moon, 10-30 arcsec Pluto/Mercury) is real series truncation.
- fix: Set an 'outside-validated-range' flag in natalChart outside 1801-2150 and gate it in a test; report Delta-T source in the receipt.

## verification-honesty-11 — Edge cases probed and found sound but uncommitted as tests: poles, natal-instant transits, identical synastry, station flag, leap second, receipt fuzz
- severity: note · status: unverified · effort: 1.5 d
- claim: Run against the real package: transits(natal, natalUtc) returns 42 aspects with 24 orb-0 conjunctions and no NaN/null; synastry(a, a) returns 42 inter-aspects, no NaN; Mercury retrograde flag agrees with the sign of Swiss's speed at all 97 hourly samples across the 2026-03-20 19:32:50 UT station; resolveLocalToUtc('2016-12-31','23:59:60','UTC') rejects with RangeError (leap seconds unsupported by design); DST gap combined with LMT is already covered (localToUtc-precision.test.ts historicalGaps carry both flags); 20,000 seeded mutations of a 4,822-byte natal envelope through parseNatalEnvelope produced 0 throws, 0 prototype pollution, 0 disagreements with JSON.parse on the 847 accepted inputs, rejections by code invalid_json 7485, invalid_shape 5470, complexity_limit 3298, invalid_value 1051, unsupported_feature 926, inconsistent_result 753, unsupported_version 170. Still untested and not run here: interior tangencies in findLongitudeCrossings (conceded), unknown-time charts across a date boundary beyond the civil-date suite, negative years vs any oracle.
- evidence: probes.mjs, probes.json, probes.log; swiss-probes.log Mercury block.
- impact: None negative; these are cheap regressions to lock in, and the fuzz is the only adversarial check of the custom parser that exists anywhere.
- fix: One-line proposals: (1) engine.test.ts 'transits at the natal instant yield orb-0 conjunctions and no NaN'; (2) synastry self-pair invariants; (3) frozen Swiss station instant with retrograde flag agreement at +/-1 h; (4) a seeded 2,000-iteration fuzz in portable.test.ts asserting no throw, no pollution, JSON.parse agreement; (5) a pole test asserting the degenerate flag from finding 9; (6) a tangency test with a synthetic body whose longitude touches the target without crossing.


# Swiss gap (all areas)

- [alpha-reduction-math] Light-time: Swiss — Newtonian iteration, target at emission (no Shapiro delay) · Zodiacs — Same, to a caller-set tolerance (1e-11 s), with a Banach a-posteriori bound in the search; agrees with an independent chain to 7.8e-9 s · exceed — Add the Shapiro term (<= ~2e-4 s, < 1e-7") for completeness and put the light-time bound on every pointwise result
- [alpha-reduction-math] Annual aberration: Swiss — Relativistic form with the observer's barycentric velocity · Zodiacs — eraAb form with barycentric analytic velocity, without the Klioner potential term: 4.2-5.0e-7" from ERFA · exceed — Switch on withPotential (already implemented in aberration.mjs) in reduce.mjs; then the chain is ERFA-complete
- [alpha-reduction-math] Gravitational deflection: Swiss — Solar deflection only · Zodiacs — Solar deflection, eraLd with finite-distance q and observer/Sun at reception, target/Sun at emission; matches ERFA fixtures bit-for-bit; released reducer uses dlim 1e-14 so the limiter never binds · exceed — Add planetary deflectors (Jupiter up to ~16 mas, Saturn ~6 mas, Earth for the Moon negligible) using the same eraLd form with body GM and positions already in the pack; ERFA's eraLdn is the template
- [alpha-reduction-math] Precession: Swiss — IAU 2006 short term, Vondrak 2011 long term · Zodiacs — IAU 2006 Fukushima-Williams only; matches pfw06 to 2.9e-12"; adequate over the pack's 1850-2150 · exceed — Needed only with DE441 full range: implement Vondrak-Capitaine-Wallace 2011 with a switch beyond +-2 centuries
- [alpha-reduction-math] Nutation: Swiss — IAU 2000B (its own evaluation departs from the published series by <=0.00153" on the corpus) · Zodiacs — Published IAU 2000B, 77 terms, P03-adjusted; matches eraNut00b to 6.7e-12" · exceed — Carry IAU 2000A (1,365 terms, table already parsed in the research track): 0.00215" max on the corpus, 17x cost
- [alpha-reduction-math] Frame bias ICRS -> dynamical: Swiss — Applied for DE-based positions · Zodiacs — Applied once through the pfw06 angles; matrix matches eraPnm06a family to 2.2e-16 · exceed — None needed
- [alpha-reduction-math] TDB-TT: Swiss — Not measured here · Zodiacs — Three-term form, 21.8 us max vs eraDtdb · exceed — Port eraDtdb (or its 20-term truncation, ~1 us)
- [alpha-reduction-math] Body centres for Mars outward: Swiss — Barycentres by default; centres with SEFLG_CENTER_BODY and sepm*.se1 files · Zodiacs — Barycentres only, flagged; up to 0.093" (Pluto), 0.071" (Jupiter), 0.053" (Saturn) off the centre · exceed — Compile centre-rel-barycentre Chebyshev deltas from NAIF satellite kernels into the pack (~2.6 MB per planet) and apply at emission via the offsetKm hook
- [alpha-reduction-math] Topocentric place, diurnal aberration, refraction: Swiss — FLG_TOPOCTR with geodetic lon/lat/altitude; refraction in rise/set · Zodiacs — None (observer is the geocentre) · exceed — WGS84 geodetic observer to GCRS with ERA from the same IAU chain, total observer velocity for diurnal aberration; validate against pyerfa atco13 and Swiss
- [alpha-reduction-math] Heliocentric / barycentric / J2000 / astrometric / true-position variants: Swiss — All via flags · Zodiacs — None in the released contract (barycentric position deliberately excluded because the pack's proven barycentric Moon bound 0.449 km missed its 0.2 km target); fixed-J2000 geometric only in experimental search modes · exceed — Expose the existing stages as switches; for barycentric output either state the 0.449 km proven bound honestly or recompile the Moon at higher fidelity
- [alpha-reduction-math] Apparent rates (speeds): Swiss — FLG_SPEED, consistent with the apparent chain · Zodiacs — None reported pointwise; derivatives exist only inside the interval search modules · exceed — Differentiate the pointwise chain analytically (formulas already in aberration.mjs/deflection.mjs/frame-of-date.mjs) and report rates with the same bound discipline
- [alpha-reduction-math] Sidereal longitudes: Swiss — FLG_SIDEREAL with ~40 ayanamsas · Zodiacs — None · exceed — Compute ayanamsas from the same precession chain (mean equinox of date to a fixed epoch) so the offset shares the frame's bound
- [alpha-reduction-math] Rigorous error bounds on outputs: Swiss — None · Zodiacs — Proven enclosures of the pack polynomial inside the search modes (sound in every family tested; padding justified by slack in three places); nothing on the pointwise apparent() and the pack's proven fit bound excluded from results · exceed — Attach numerical + data + model uncertainty to every pointwise result (finding 12) and make the three slack-covered allowances explicit (finding 3); this is the one capability Swiss cannot match
- [alpha-reduction-math] Ephemeris series and range: Swiss — .se1 fits to DE431, 13201 BC to 17191 AD · Zodiacs — DE440s, 1850-2150 (pack range), Moon within 0.0104" of Swiss with a U-shaped epoch dependence consistent with DE431-vs-DE440 · exceed — Compile DE441 (or DE440 full) for the historical range with Vondrak precession and a long-term nutation; attribute the lunar difference with Horizons DE441 VECTORS
- [alpha-search-partition] Longitude crossing of the Sun and Moon: Swiss — swe_solcross / swe_mooncross: iterative 'next crossing after t', no bracket, no count, no error bound, no cancellation · Zodiacs — Released: proven-complete geometric-J2000 crossings (validated-geometric) and conditional apparent-of-date crossings (empirical); experimental: proven-complete crossings for retarded, retarded+aberrated and of-date (true-equinox) quantities over any window, with brackets ≤ 1e-4 s and typed support · exceed — Already exceeds in semantics. To exceed in the quantity Swiss reports (apparent of date): release rung 4 with a TT↔TDB/UTC adapter that states its error, and fix the Moon enclosure (finding 2) so rung 5 finishes; add planetary deflection and Shapiro terms as listed omissions with bounds (both far below 0.02″)
- [alpha-search-partition] Geocentric longitude crossing of planets (ingresses with retrograde loops): Swiss — None. swe_helio_cross is heliocentric only; users must scan and bisect themselves and can miss the three crossings of a retrograde loop · Zodiacs — Yes, all ten bodies, retrograde handled by construction (a projection, not a wrapped angle): the retro-3-in-record fixture returned 3 crossings proven in every rung · exceed — Already exceeds; keep the of-date rung as the flagship and publish the proof statement per result
- [alpha-search-partition] Stations / retrograde turns: Swiss — None built in (users detect a speed sign change from swe_calc's velocity, itself a numerical derivative in some paths) · Zodiacs — Not supported (SEARCH_CONTRACT.notSupported); production uses a ±0.25 d finite difference for speed · exceed — Roots of N(t) = x·y′ − y·x′ (ecliptic components), an explicit polynomial of degree 2n−2 per piece; the same exclusion/monotone proof gives proven-complete stations for the geometric quantity in ~2 days; lift to of-date via the interval chain (dλ/dt enclosure already exists as fDotEnclosure)
- [alpha-search-partition] Aspects between two bodies at any angle: Swiss — None (users iterate) · Zodiacs — Empirical mode kind 'aspect' only (conditional completeness, 20°/day rate assumption) · exceed — Aspect θ is a root of (x1·y2 − y1·x2)cosθ − (x1·x2 + y1·y2)sinθ, a polynomial in the two stored states on the common refinement of their records; proven-complete aspects for the geometric quantity in ~3 days including a product-coefficient builder in cheb.mjs
- [alpha-search-partition] Latitude / node / declination crossings: Swiss — swe_mooncross_node (Moon only, next crossing) · Zodiacs — None · exceed — Ecliptic latitude zero is the root of z_ecl(t), a polynomial; declination crossings need the equatorial component directly (also stored); proven for all ten bodies in ~1 day on top of the station work
- [alpha-search-partition] Elongation / angular-separation threshold events (including the solar-conjunction floor): Swiss — None as events; positions only · Zodiacs — The domain partition already brackets elongation = 5° crossings to a 60-s span for any body, without a target longitude · exceed — Generalise partitionDomain's floor to any angle and any body pair and expose boundary spans as bracketed events; with the finding-2 enclosure fix the brackets tighten to seconds at a few thousand evaluations
- [alpha-search-partition] Solar and lunar eclipses, occultations: Swiss — swe_sol_eclipse_when_glob/loc, swe_lun_eclipse_when, swe_lun_occult_when_glob/loc: iterative, global and local, with Besselian-style circumstances; no completeness semantics · Zodiacs — None · exceed — Eclipse detection = separation(Sun, Moon) < apparent radii sum, a threshold event on the same separation enclosure as above, proven-complete over a window; local circumstances and contact times additionally need a topocentric observer (weights for a site offset rotated by GAST); detection in ~3 days, local circumstances ~5 more
- [alpha-search-partition] Rise, set, transit: Swiss — swe_rise_trans and swe_rise_trans_true_hor with refraction and true horizon · Zodiacs — None; observer is the geocentre in every mode · exceed — Add a topocentric observer (site vector rotated by an interval GAST from the existing nutation/precession chain) and an altitude function h(t) with interval enclosure; proven-complete rise/set is then a threshold event; ~5 days; refraction stays a stated model term
- [alpha-search-partition] Completeness, error and budget semantics: Swiss — None: every finder returns a time or an error code; a missed or spurious crossing is undetectable from the API · Zodiacs — Typed support none/conditional/proven, exact totals only under established completeness, unresolved and excluded spans listed, whole-request budgets, cancellation as a result state; buildResult refuses contradictory results · exceed — Already exceeds; add the missing invariant gate for the partitioned contract (finding 9) and carry the declared trig bound into the enclosure (finding 8) so the theorem statement is fully self-contained
- [alpha-search-partition] Behaviour near solar conjunction (deflection domain): Swiss — Always answers, applying its own deflection with an internal limiter; no domain flag · Zodiacs — Rung 5 declines inside 5° and lists the span as excluded; the partition proves admissible/excluded spans; but the Moon cannot be decided at 60 s and the cost is 47–831x rung 4 · exceed — Finding 2 (merged-weight geocentric enclosure + centred elongation form) should make the partition decide the Moon at seconds and cut the deflected cost by one to two orders; then the honest 'declined below 5°, rung-4 downgrade recorded per body' policy of DEFLECTION-PROFILE §7 becomes a strict improvement over Swiss's silent limiter
- [angles-houses-aspects] ASC/MC accuracy (frame consistency): Swiss — Apparent sidereal time with true obliquity of date; long-term sidereal model outside 1850-2050 (1.9" RAMC departure from IAU 2006/2000A) · Zodiacs — Apparent sidereal time with MEAN obliquity: ASC p95 22", max 512" vs Swiss over lat 0-66; RAMC within 0.19" of the IAU arbiter · exceed — Switch to true obliquity (e_tilt().tobl, matches Swiss nutation to 0.03"): projected ASC residual vs the IAU arbiter ~7" worst (0.36" at |lat| <= 45) against Swiss's 68" at lat 66 after 2050; add optional IERS DUT1 for UT1 and disclose it in the receipt
- [angles-houses-aspects] House systems: Swiss — 25 codes (Placidus, Koch, Porphyry, Regiomontanus, Campanus, Equal x4 variants, Whole, Vehlow, Meridian, Horizon, Topocentric, Alcabitius, Morinus, Krusinski, Gauquelin, APC, Pullen SD/SR, Sunshine x2, Carter, Sripati) · Zodiacs — Whole sign and Placidus only; everything else rejected by validateBirthSettings · exceed — Implement Equal, Porphyry, Koch, Regiomontanus, Campanus, Alcabitius, Morinus, Meridian, Vehlow, Topocentric with closed forms (only Placidus/topocentric iterate), publish the contraction bound and iteration count for the iterative ones, validate each on the 55-66.6 ladder against houses_ex
- [angles-houses-aspects] Polar policy: Swiss — Placidus/Koch/Gauquelin/Sunshine-alt fail at |lat| >= 90 - eps and silently return Porphyry (status -1, wrapper raises); Regiomontanus/Campanus/Topocentric/APC/Sunshine flip the MC by 180 deg when below the horizon; others compute · Zodiacs — Placidus refused at a constant |lat| > 66 (0.56 deg short of Swiss), falls back to whole sign with a 'polar-fallback' flag; rising-intersection ASC rule matches Swiss at 66.6-89.9 · exceed — Dynamic 90 - eps_true limit, named fallbackSystem in the result (Porphyry or whole, caller's choice), per-system documented MC/ASC conventions, explicit degenerate-horizon handling at |lat| = 90

- [swiss-parity] Engineering: language, ports, determinism, browser: Swiss — C library, 25 years of use, ports to many languages, WASM builds exist; thread-safety caveats (global state: set_ephe_path, set_topo, set_sid_mode); GPL/AGPL or commercial licence · Zodiacs — ESM JavaScript, MIT (engine) with ERFA notice; one numerical core for browser and Node checked bit-identical; no global state; no C dependency · exceed — Cross-runtime byte-identical results and a permissive licence are genuine leads; maturity and breadth cannot be caught up quickly.
- [swiss-parity] Planet centre vs system barycentre: Swiss — Barycentre by default; exact centre with FLG_CENTER_BODY and sepm files (matches Horizons to 0.001 arcsec) · Zodiacs — Barycentre only, labelled isSystemBarycentre in the alpha; production astronomy-engine VSOP87 is heliocentric planet-centre series of ~arcsec quality so the question is moot there · exceed — Compile centre offsets from jup348/sat480/nep097/plu060 into the pack (2 days); effect ≤0.075 arcsec; parity, not lead.
- [swiss-parity] Nutation and deflection models: Swiss — IAU 2000B nutation (measured by the repo to sit within 0.0013 arcsec of 2000B), solar deflection, IAU 2006/Vondrak precession · Zodiacs — Production: 5-term nutation (astronomy-engine), no deflection. Alpha: IAU 2000B 77 terms, solar deflection, frame bias, IAU 2006 precession; 2000A measured at ≤0.0025 arcsec effect and not carried · exceed — IAU 2000A plus Jupiter/Saturn deflection would move results by ≤0.003 arcsec and ≤0.017 arcsec only within a planetary radius of the deflector — below every arbiter's convention noise (0.05-0.4 arcsec). Not a meaningful place to exceed.
- [time] Delta-T (TT-UT) model: Swiss — swe_deltat/swe_deltat_ex: Stephenson-Morrison-Hohenkerk 2016 LOD spline before the tabulated record, tabulated observed values 1620-present (updated with IERS), extrapolation past the table, tidal-acceleration adjustment to the ephemeris in use (TIDAL_DE431 -25.8, TIDAL_DE441 -25.936, MOSEPH -25.58), five selectable models (MOD_DELTAT_*), swe_set_delta_t_userdef, swe_set_tid_acc; within 0.3 s of the observed value in 2026 · Zodiacs — astronomy-engine DeltaT_EspenakMeeus 2004 polynomials, hard-wired, unexposed, no uncertainty; +5.9 s in 2026 (Moon +3.3 arcsec), +18 s by 2050, +30 s at 1600; no way to pin; receipt silent · exceed — Ship an observed TT-UT1 table (IERS finals + historical series) with a per-date uncertainty band, extrapolate transparently, adjust for DE440's tidal acceleration, expose value/model/band in results and receipts, allow caller pin; carry the band into per-body longitude uncertainty — Swiss gives a number, this would give a number with an error bar
- [time] UTC, leap seconds and UT1: Swiss — swe_utc_to_jd / swe_jdet_to_utc / swe_jdut1_to_utc with a leap-second table (accepts 23:59:60); UT1 = TT - Delta-T; swe_calc_ut takes UT1 · Zodiacs — JS Date (UTC) used as UT1 and as the Delta-T argument; no leap-second table; error bounded at 0.9 s (RAMC 13.5 arcsec, MC/ASC up to 44 arcsec at 66 deg, Moon 0.5 arcsec) · exceed — Exact UTC->TAI->TT via a leap-second table, UT1-UTC from IERS finals with 90-day predictions and a +-0.9 s fallback band; report both offsets in the receipt — more than Swiss, which does not model UT1-UTC at all
- [time] Calendars (Julian/Gregorian): Swiss — swe_julday/swe_revjul with SE_JUL_CAL/SE_GREG_CAL, swe_date_conversion('j'|'g'), swe_utc_time_zone; valid -13000..+17000 · Zodiacs — Proleptic Gregorian only (civil-date.ts, geo.js wallMilliseconds, Intl 'gregory'); Julian records entered as typed are 10-13 days off (Sun 9.5-13 deg); windows 1800-2199 admit Julian-era Russian/Balkan births · exceed — Add a calendar field with JDN conversion, a per-country adoption table that warns on likely Old Style dates, and record both the typed and converted dates in the receipt — Swiss converts but never warns
- [time] Sidereal time and obliquity for angles: Swiss — swe_sidtime/swe_sidtime0 (IAU 2006 precession, full nutation, selectable models), houses use true obliquity of date and apparent ARMC · Zodiacs — astronomy-engine SiderealTime: ERA from UT + IAU 2006 GMST polynomial in TT + equation of the equinoxes from a 5-term nutation; angles use MEAN obliquity evaluated at UTC centuries (ASC differs from the true-obliquity convention by up to 128 arcsec at 66 deg; UTC-vs-TT argument negligible) · exceed — Use the alpha's 77-term IAU 2000B nutation for both the equation of the equinoxes and the true obliquity, evaluate at TT, feed UT1 to the ERA; then angles match Swiss's convention with a stated nutation model — parity plus provenance
- [time] Local civil time to UTC (time zones): Swiss — None beyond swe_utc_time_zone offset arithmetic; Swiss-based products rely on an external atlas (e.g. Astrodienst/ACS) with full historical legal-time tables · Zodiacs — Intl-based resolver with fold/gap/LMT policy, verified over 1.68 M cases with 0 algorithmic failures; but host ICU history is the tzdb default without backzone, so 98 zones (Stockholm, Oslo, Copenhagen, Amsterdam, Reykjavik, Luxembourg, Aruba, Antigua, ...) return another city's pre-1970 offsets, off by 20-180 min · exceed — Ship a versioned backzone-complete tzdb table inside the engine (Intl as cross-check), stamp tzdb version and the applied transition into receipts, flag tzdb-marked-uncertain history, and later allow an atlas override — this would exceed Swiss (which has nothing) and match atlas-backed products for most of the world
- [time] Equation of time / LMT helpers: Swiss — swe_time_equ, swe_lmt_to_lat, swe_lat_to_lmt · Zodiacs — None (LMT handled implicitly through tzdb LMT offsets; 'lmt' flag is a fractional-minute heuristic that misfires) · exceed — Derive LMT/legal-time kind from the shipped tzdb types; add an equation-of-time helper from the existing Sun position for apparent-solar-time records (some historical birth records are in sundial time)
- [time] Provenance of time conversions (receipts): Swiss — None; Swiss returns numbers only · Zodiacs — Receipt CONVENTIONS declares calendar 'proleptic-gregorian' and angle/ephemeris conventions; runtime.tzdbVersion/icuVersion slots exist but are never filled; no Delta-T, UT1 or leap-second facts · exceed — Fill tzdbVersion from the shipped table, add conventions.timeScale {deltaT, sigma, model, ut1MinusUtc, leapSeconds}, and make receipts re-verifiable across host updates — a capability Swiss has never had
- [verification-honesty] Reference oracle for positions: Swiss — Validated by its authors against JPL DE431/DE441 directly (reads the series), with published per-body precision statements · Zodiacs — 13 Horizons literals at two epochs (gates 12-3600x looser than residuals), four frozen Swiss packs, one 180-row benchmark run once; no CI re-verification of the benchmark · exceed — Commit a versioned Horizons reference set (query, API version, kernels) with 3x-residual gates, and a quarterly benchmark re-run diffing p50/p95/max; then the alpha's 0.16 arcsec agreement becomes an enforced property rather than a report
- [verification-honesty] Time scale (Delta-T): Swiss — Table of observed values plus extrapolation; within 0.4 s of IERS in 2017-2026 · Zodiacs — astronomy-engine polynomial, +2.2 to +6.3 s vs IERS today and diverging; untested · exceed — Ship IERS historic + Bulletin A predictions with a yearly refresh and a 0.2 s gate; this beats Swiss 2.10.03's frozen table on every date after its release
- [verification-honesty] Observational or independent anchors: Swiss — None published as a test suite; relies on JPL heritage · Zodiacs — None in the tree until this audit; four canon events now measured (alpha within 1.2 s, engine within 21 s) · exceed — Commit canon eclipses/transits (and later IOTA occultations, USNO apparent places) as gated tests for both engines; a public 'anchored against published events' table is something Swiss does not offer
- [verification-honesty] Mutation-confirmed test families: Swiss — No mutation testing (C library with regression tests) · Zodiacs — 13 of 42 claimed families recorded; one recorded mutation survives; reviewers found 8-9 survivors in enclosure construction · exceed — A committed, scriptable mutation ledger with per-family failing tests, re-run in CI, would exceed anything Swiss publishes about its own tests
- [verification-honesty] Derivatives (speed) verification: Swiss — Analytic velocities from the series, tested · Zodiacs — Central difference +/-0.25 d, 2-6 arcsec/day slow near lunar perigee, no test · exceed — Use state-vector velocities (GeoMoonState already has them) and gate against frozen Swiss speeds at perigee/apogee and stations
- [verification-honesty] Data-dependent tests in CI: Swiss — Ships .se1 data with tests runnable anywhere · Zodiacs — Tier-b never runs in CI (no pack committed); tier-a uses synthetic packs · exceed — Commit a 1-2 MiB DE440s excerpt pack with digest and run real-pack tests in CI; the pack format's integrity checks make this safer than Swiss's unsigned data files
- [verification-honesty] House-system coverage near polar limits: Swiss — Placidus computed to its own failure point, then Porphyry with status -1 · Zodiacs — Placidus to 66 deg then whole-sign fallback; 9 arcsec residual at 65.9 N unverified until now; exact poles return ASC 0 with a generic flag · exceed — Test the 60-66 band, flag near-limit charts explicitly, and document the degenerate pole answer; parity first, then exceed by exposing the convergence status Swiss hides in a warning buffer
